# LLMLL Compiler Team Implementation Roadmap

> **Status:** Active — see [`../CHANGELOG.md § Latest`](../CHANGELOG.md#Latest) for the shipped version (this header does not version-stamp).
>
> **Governing criterion:** every deliverable is measured against *progress toward one-shot correctness* — does it reduce the iteration burden, increase obligation completeness, or shorten the repair distance for an agent writing LLMLL? The terminal state: an agent writes a program once, the compiler accepts it, contracts verify.
>
> **Relationship to `LLMLL.md`:** complementary. `LLMLL.md` is the language reference (types, semantics, grammar, builtins); a shipped user-visible feature is described inline in its relevant reference section (§3 / §5.3.3–§5.3.5 / §13). This doc is the engineering backlog (open tickets, acceptance criteria, decisions); release history lives in [`CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md). Shipped features move to Shipped Releases here.

---

## Versioning Conventions

- Items marked **[CT]** are compiler team implementation tasks.
- Items marked **[SPEC]** are language specification changes that must land in `LLMLL.md` before or alongside the implementation.
- Items marked **[DESIGN]** are design decisions resolved by the joint team, recorded here as implementation constraints.

| **EVENT-LOG-2** (`LLMLL.md §10a` specifies a mechanism the compiler does not implement) `[SPEC]` | **OPEN** — spec/code drift, four parts, surfaced while settling EFFECT-RESP Rev 4 | The event log ships and has since v0.3.1: `emitEventLogPreamble` ([`CodegenHs.hs:844`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) writes one `(stdin, stdout)` event per console-loop iteration to `<module>.event-log.jsonl` (`:922`, `:949`), and `llmll replay` re-executes the program against the recorded inputs and reports divergence ([`Replay.hs:120-153`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Replay.hs), `Main.hs:2547`). The guarantee is a **divergence oracle over an observable I/O trace**. §10a describes value-logging with injection, in the present indicative, in four parts: **D-1** capture gated on `:deterministic true` (`§10a:1652`; capture is unconditional and `capDeterministic`'s only consumer is `AstEmit.hs:433`); **D-2** a populated `:captures` array (`§10a:1661-1662`; `CodegenHs.hs:855` emits `"captures":[]` as a literal and `EventLogEntry` has no such field); **D-3** injection on replay (`§10a:1665`; `replayOne` compares, and the `:1637-1641` "Runtime Fix" column is unimplemented); **D-4** a `replayable` / `best-effort` status (`§10a:1669-1672`; `ReplayStatus` is never constructed). **Adjudicated: §10a narrows, the code does not move** — census is **zero** `:deterministic true` in-tree against twenty `:deterministic false`, so narrowing costs nothing. Next action is the six-point narrowing in [`design/event-log-scope-proposal.md`](design/event-log-scope-proposal.md) §"Proposed §10a text shape"; closes on that doc pass. Not a soundness defect: nothing claims replayability today. |
| **REPLAY-INJECT** (record each non-deterministic call's return and inject it on replay) `[CT]` | **OPEN — UNSCHEDULED**; **not** blocking any DRIVER-LL phase | The mechanism `LLMLL.md §10a` specifies and EVENT-LOG-2 removes from the spec, preserved as work rather than deleted because **EFFECT-RESP degrades the shipped oracle**: once RC-1 lands, `:step` consumes a `Response` produced by performing the previous command, so on replay `replayOne` re-runs `wasi.fs.read` against the filesystem as it stands at replay time and reports "the world changed" as divergence, indistinguishable from "the program changed". Injection makes the trace self-contained again and is the named repair. **MEASURED at v0.14.83, and the prediction named the wrong failure first.** This row expected replay to break on `wasi.fs.read` re-reading a changed world; it broke upstream of that, in harness line framing, on programs that read no files at all (`REPLAY-FRAME-1`, fixed). The EFFECT-RESP degradation below still stands and is still unmeasured. This row additionally now owns **`:init` output logging**, deferred from `REPLAY-FRAME-1`: `:init`'s output is printed but not logged, so replay misaligns by `count('\n')` lines for any program with a printing `:init`, and the only correct repair reverses the deliberate decision at `CodegenHs.hs:1543-1546` and changes shipped programs' stdout bytes. Scope: per-call interception at the codegen boundary for every `wasi.*` builtin, a populated `captures` array, a `captures` field on `EventLogEntry`, and a replay mode that swaps effect implementations for log reads — larger than EFFECT-RESP. Sub-items: **W-REPLAY-INERT**, a declaration-site warning on `(capability … :deterministic true)` whose **in-tree firing population is zero** (first firing site would be the driver), and retiring `ReplayStatus` ([`Syntax.hs:74`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Syntax.hs), `:871-873`) once §10a's status table is withdrawn. Design record: [`design/event-log-scope-proposal.md`](design/event-log-scope-proposal.md). |
| **HTTP-GET-1** (`wasi.http.get`, spun out of CAP-PROC) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN, and NOW ON the DRIVER-LL critical path.** This row said the opposite until v0.14.83, and Phase 3 measured it false: **stage A STOPPED on it**. The escape it named, reaching HTTP through a granted `curl` under `wasi.proc.run`, is real but does not rescue the replay, because no RFC source bytes are committed anywhere in this repository, so there is nothing for stage A to hash without a live fetch. Filed at v0.14.81 when CAP-PROC closed at five operations rather than six. The campaign reaches HTTP through a granted `curl` under `wasi.proc.run`: zero dependencies, byte-faithful, and it moves TLS trust into an ambient binary invoked with an unchecked argv, which is the tradeoff being accepted rather than hidden. Pick this up only as standalone capability work | Dropped from CAP-PROC on two independent grounds, either sufficient. (i) The Rev 5 arm mapping is `RText` body, which **cannot reproduce `stage_A_intake`**: the driver does `dest.write_bytes(r.read())` and then hashes the FILE, and an `RText` round trip is not byte-faithful. (ii) `http-client` + `http-client-tls` moves a generated project's dependency closure from **33 packages to 79** (`stack ls dependencies`, lts-22.43: crypton, tls, four crypton-x509-\*, three asn1-\*, socks, pem, hourglass, cereal), none of which are compiler deps, so CI's snapshot cache misses. **Signature already settled** for whenever it lands: `wasi.http.get : string -> string -> Command` (url, dest), `RNone` on 2xx and `RErr` carrying the status otherwise, **plus an atomicity clause: `dest` is either absent or contains the complete 2xx body, never a prefix.** Without that clause a truncated transfer feeds `wasi.fs.sha256` a valid-looking pin over a partial download, and that pin is the campaign's provenance root. Design: [`design/driver-ll-open-work.md`](design/driver-ll-open-work.md) R-11. |
| **FS-STAT-1** (`liveness.advancing` has no callable data source) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**; **its prerequisite `RESP-FACT-1` SHIPPED v0.17.0** (`d5dd5a8`, 2026-09-04), so the clamp's discharge claim below can now be stated as a fact request under `LLMLL.md` §9.7 (control-tag facts) and this row is unblocked. Filed by DRIVER-LL Phase 4 and spun out on the `HTTP-GET-1` precedent: a capability the campaign needs, carried as standalone work rather than inside a phase. **An mtime does not close this**, and the phase's Rev 5 routing note said it would. `advancing` takes an **age in seconds** (`liveness.llmll:6`, precondition `[S12-DOM]`) and `wasi.clock.monotonic` reports nanoseconds since an *unspecified* epoch, so no subtraction of the two is defined; producing an age from an mtime needs a wall clock on the mtime's epoch, and monotonic explicitly is not one. **Blocks driver-spec §12:405-428 through a §15.2:522-524 capability gap:** the §15.1:511 proof obligation is discharged (the predicate verifies today), the capability that would feed it does not exist, and the deferral must therefore be tiered under §15.1:504-505 rather than left silent | Proposed shape: `wasi.fs.stat` returns the artifact's **age in seconds, clamped at zero**, as `RCode`, carrying `Caps {EFsRead, ENonDet}`. Both labels are already in the closed catalog (`primEffect`, [`ObligationAssembly.hs:399`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/ObligationAssembly.hs)), so CAP-PROC's admissibility rule holds with **no new `Response` arm**. **The clamp is the design content, not an implementation detail**, and **the discharge claim it used to carry was CORRECTED 2026-08-19 because it named no channel.** The row said the clamp discharges `[S12-DOM]`'s first conjunct (`(>= newest-artifact-age 0)`) **by construction**. It does not, and it cannot today: `RCode` carries a bare `TInt`, `FixpointEmit.hs` contains **zero** occurrences of `Response`, so a clamp makes the fact true at runtime with no way for it to reach the caller, who must then guard or re-prove it. **`RESP-FACT-1` is therefore a PREREQUISITE of this row, not an adjacent concern**, and the discharge claim becomes true once it lands and not before. Design: [`design/resp-fact-proposal.md`](design/resp-fact-proposal.md), whose §9 records this correction and whose Rev 2 keys the fact on the program's own control tag, on the effective-precondition channel, after the professor round rejected Rev 1's match-arm binder. The clamp's own rationale is unaffected: the rejected alternative (an epoch mtime plus a new `wasi.clock.realtime`, subtracted shell-side) lets clock skew drive the age negative and turn a stale-liveness read into a precondition violation. Two consequences for this row: `[S12-DOM]`'s second conjunct (`(>= log-age newest-artifact-age)`) stays shell-side and is **not** discharged here; and an age-returning builtin is a **candidate first firing site for `W-REPLAY-INERT`** (the REPLAY-INJECT row above), whose in-tree firing population is recorded as zero. **Stated limit:** `RCode` now carries exit statuses, monotonic nanoseconds and clamped ages, with the unit nowhere in the type. That is a scope boundary LLMLL has chosen (no dimension-indexed numerics; cf. Kennedy, POPL 1997), and the in-scope move is to name the unit in the declared contract. Design: [`design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md) §14. |
| **MATCH-CATCHALL-1** (`emitMatch` suppresses its catch-all on a mixed constructor/literal match) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, a general crash-freedom hole surfaced by DRIVER-LL Phase 4 and explicitly **not** Phase 4 work. `isAdtExhaustive = not (null ctorNames)` inside `emitMatch` ([`CodegenHs.hs:1344`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) suppresses the emitted `; _ -> error "non-exhaustive match"` arm (`:1347-1349`) whenever **any** arm is a constructor pattern, and that ground fails when a literal arm sits beside it: the mixed match ships with no catch-all and falls through to GHC's pattern-match exception. **The precondition is what makes this non-obvious, and the row must carry it.** `checkExhaustive` ([`TypeCheck.hs:2255-2281`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) fires for `TSumType`, `TResult` **and `TBool`** (`:2255-2257`), not for known sum types alone, and a literal arm against a sum-typed or `Result`-typed scrutinee is type-incompatible and draws a `tcWarn` (`:2326-2332`). **The entry condition is therefore a program that ships past a warning**, not one that typechecks clean, which bounds the population and is why this is a row rather than a P0 | Either way the exception is raised inside a `Command`, which is the crash-freedom hazard `CodegenHs.hs:178` names for `wasi.fs.delete`, so the remedy is not "add a wildcard" but "the wildcard arm must produce a defined state transition". Cite the bindings (`emitMatch`, `isAdtExhaustive`, `checkExhaustive`) rather than the line numbers when working this: all four of the source case's Rev 5 citations were stale at HEAD when the phase re-audited them, two of them landing in unrelated functions, a rename being grep-detectable where a line shift is not. Evidence: [`design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md) §10 case 6, the row's sole witness, and §14 for the tag. |
| **PROC-BOUNDARY-1** (no entry mode combines argv, `Command` performance and a defined terminal status) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.85** (commit `4e5ff29`), **with two items explicitly owed** (next column), so this row is not closed. Three facts, measured in the compiler before the change rather than inferred, and together they are why it was a capability gap rather than an ergonomics complaint. (i) **`ModeCli` has argv and performs no `Command`**: the `ModeCli` equation of `emitMainBody` ([`CodegenHs.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) generates `args <- getArgs` then `print (step args)`, so it can read the flags and cannot act on them. (ii) **`ModeConsole` performs `Command`s and never sees argv**, and its loop ends at `if eof then return ()`, so a stdin that runs out before the run completes exits **0**, having written partial state, with no diagnostic of any kind; there is no terminal-marker check. (iii) **Neither mode can set an exit status**: the RFC-pipeline rig asserts `returncode == 2` at four sites in `test_rfc_pipeline_integration.py`, and no entry mode can produce it. The acceptance criterion of a shipped sub-phase was therefore unmeetable rather than merely awkward, and sub-phase 4a is now unblocked. **Filed as a row on R-14's promotion rule** ([`design/driver-ll-open-work.md`](design/driver-ll-open-work.md)): an R-item becomes a roadmap row the moment something is blocked on it, which is exactly how R-11 became `HTTP-GET-1`. Sub-phase 4a of DRIVER-LL Phase 4 is blocked on this one, so the rule fires. **The catalog does not grow.** `wasi.proc.args` maps to the **existing** `RList` arm under the **existing** `ENonDet` label, on the precedent CAP-PROC set when `wasi.fs.list` shared `EFsRead` rather than widening the closed six-label catalog: zero new arms, zero new labels, six labels before and six after. **The whole boundary lands in the auto-discharged verification fragment**, with nothing nonlinear and nothing escaping to Lean. The status range is QF-LIA, `:status`'s totality over the state type is decidable and rides `checkExhaustive`'s existing coverage check, and `ENonDet` leaves `EffectSummary`'s join unchanged with nothing widening to `Unbounded`. That is the consequence of splitting the two halves on category rather than treating them alike: argv is an effect (an ambient nondeterministic read, the same class as the clock) and the terminal status is not (nothing in the program observes it, and the program does not continue past it), so the status lands in `def-main` as a projection from state to `int`. A design that made the status an effect, or that threaded a terminal reason through a new type, would have paid for it in the verification mapping. **Shipped and measured.** `wasi.proc.args` lands nullary on the existing `RList` arm under the existing `ENonDet` label with `argv[0]` excluded, and Σ_eff stays six-wide. `:status` lands as an optional `def-main` field applied to the final state on the `:done?` path, and `llmll verify` on the gate fixture reports `body-faithful` and **SAFE (liquid-fixpoint)**, so §5's prediction that the whole boundary stays auto-discharged is now a measurement rather than a claim. **The exhaustion exit is gated on whether `:done?` is DECLARED, not on whether it fired**, which is Rev 3 of the design and is what makes this **not** a breaking change: a program declaring no completion predicate is a stream processor for which EOF is the normal end of input, and all three in-tree console programs in that population (`examples/replay-demo`, `examples/proof_required_test`, `compiler/test/fixtures/pair_type_test`) were built and run and exit 0 both before and after. That is the whole measured population, not a sample. The positive witness was built both ways rather than read off the diff: one fixture emitted by the pre-change compiler and by the post-change compiler, same GHC and same inputs, exits 0 then **70** on a starved run and 0 then **42** on a completed one. JSON-AST schema 0.10.0 to 0.11.0, additive; suite 1616 to 1638 examples | `wasi.proc.args` is one `builtinEnv` signature, one `primEffect` clause and one codegen case, riding RC-3 unchanged: a program that wants argv issues it as `:init`'s command and receives `RList` as `r0`, so `:init`'s arity does not move and every console program shipped to date keeps working. `def-main` gains **one optional field**, `:status`, a total function from the state type to `int` carrying a `0 <= v && v <= 255` range refinement; absent means exit 0, which is today's behaviour, so the field is additive. Two terminal paths, deliberately not alike: `:done?` holding applies `:status` and exits with the result, while stdin reaching EOF first exits a fixed, disclosed **70** and **does not consult `:status`**. Handing exhaustion to the harness is the design content, not a workaround. The rejected form made protection from the silent-success bug conditional on the caller having modelled a terminal phase; under this one **no program can exit 0 on a starved stdin**, whatever its state type. Disclosed limit: 70 is **not** reserved from the program, so a shell can tell that neither case is success but cannot separate exhaustion from a deliberate 70. Both spec surfaces are now written: `LLMLL.md` §9.5 carries `:status` and the two terminal paths, §12's grammar carries the field and its order discipline, and §13.9 carries `wasi.proc.args`. **TWO ITEMS ARE OWED, WHICH IS WHY THIS ROW IS NOT CLOSED.** (1) **The proposal's §6.3 `tcWarn` is NOT built**: it should fire when `:status` names a function carrying no range postcondition, and building it needs `checkStatusField` to look up the named function's contract, which that function does not do. It warns today on a non-console mode, on a `:status` declared with no `:done?`, and on a non-`int` return position, all read off the `:status` expression itself. Until the warning exists the range binds only where the program writes it, the compiler injecting nothing: an undeclared `:status` returning 300 truncates to 44 and one returning 256 exits **0 and reports success**. (2) **`DONE-TYPE-1`**, the pre-existing sibling defect this implementation found by executing; it has its own row below and was deliberately not fixed here. Design: [`design/proc-boundary-1-proposal.md`](design/proc-boundary-1-proposal.md) **Rev 4**, whose status line names the same owed item and whose new §5.1 records a further disclosed limit (the `0..255` range obligation is discharged as a contract obligation always, and **body-proved only for a scalar state**, since a `def` projecting a component out of a pair falls back; the proposal's own fixture takes `[s: int]`, the provable case, so its `body-faithful` line must not be read as covering a composite-state driver); implementation and measurements: [`design/proc-boundary-1-implementation-plan.md`](design/proc-boundary-1-implementation-plan.md); the now-unblocked consumer is [`design/driver-ll-phase4a-implementation-plan.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4a-implementation-plan.md). |
| **DONE-TYPE-1** (`def-main`'s `:done?` type check warns on every correct console program) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, a **pre-existing** defect surfaced by `PROC-BOUNDARY-1` and explicitly not that row's work. Found **by executing rather than by reading**, which is the only way it surfaces: the check looks right in the source. `checkStatement (SDefMain …)` infers the type of the WHOLE `:done?` expression and compares it against `TBool`, but `:done?` names a **function**, so its inferred type is `TFn [S] bool`, `compatibleWith` falls through to structural equality, returns `False`, and the warning fires. **Measured at v0.14.84 on an unmodified tree:** `llmll check` on `scripts/build-smoke/smoke.llmll`, a correct program, reports `warning: :done? should return bool; found non-bool type (ignored in v0.2)`. It fires on **every** console program in the corpus that declares `:done?` by name, so it carries no information at all and trains readers to discount the warning channel. The contrast is in the tree: `checkStatusField`, shipped at v0.14.85, was written from scratch rather than copied from this sibling precisely because of it, reads the RETURN POSITION of a `TFn` (which is what this check meant to do), and emits nothing on a correctly-typed named projection; `PB-19` pins that as an anti-regression | The repair is the one `checkStatusField` already demonstrates: expand the inferred type, take the return position when it is a `TFn`, compare that against `TBool`, and leave `TVar` compatible so an unannotated `def-shell` is not warned at, which is the same latitude `checkStepArity` extends. **Deliberately not taken as a rider on `PROC-BOUNDARY-1`**: it changes the diagnostic output of an unrelated check on every console program in the tree, and a behaviour change with that reach is owed its own row and its own release note rather than a line in someone else's. Measure the population first, as `RESERVED-NAME-1` also owes: the smoke fixture is one witness, not a census, and whether the repaired check should stay a warning or become an error depends on how many corpus programs it would newly speak about. Found in [`design/proc-boundary-1-implementation-plan.md`](design/proc-boundary-1-implementation-plan.md) finding 1, which routed it here. |
| **PROC-TIMEOUT-1** (`wasi.proc.run`'s timeout does not fire in a built program) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, found by the DRIVER-LL sub-phase 4b port and deliberately not fixed there. **Measured, not inferred:** the driver run with `--timeout 1` against a 30-second agent exits **0** with `seconds: 30` and the stage recorded `complete`, so the timeout is silently inert rather than late. The generated `package.yaml` carries no `ghc-options` and the built binary reports `RTS way: rts_v`; the same `timeout`/`waitForProcess` shape compiled plainly takes 20.4s, and with `-threaded` takes 1.5s, which is what identifies the threaded runtime as the discriminator. **THE ONE-LINE FIX DOES NOT WORK, and this row exists partly to say so.** Adding `ghc-options: -threaded` to the generated package **did not move the RTS way**, so anyone who reaches for `emitPackageYaml` and stops there will ship a change that measures identically to no change at all. Establish why the option does not reach the link step before writing the fix. **Consequence in the driver:** the budget-overrun halt is written and unreachable through the timeout path, so 4b exercises its `RErr` arm via spawn failure instead. That is a real gap in a shipped sub-phase's coverage, not a cosmetic one | Reproduce first: the measurement above is a driver-level observation, and the failing unit is smaller (a generated program that spawns a child and is asked to time it out). Then decide where the threaded runtime is declared, which is a codegen question rather than a builtin question. | **MATCH-TERM-EQ-1** (a nullary enum's param binder carried no tag domain, so a post could not relate `result` to the scrutinee) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.16.2** (commit `0fa4452`), **with one item explicitly owed** (next column), so this row is not closed. Found by the first proof-carrying-target feasibility read and **independently reproduced**. **This was a FALSE REFUTATION, which is worse than a fallback**: the verifier reported that a correct implementation violates its contract, rather than declining to decide. Minimal witness, on a three-constructor nullary sum with `(post (= result a))`: the direct body `a` is body-faithful and passes, and the semantically identical exhaustive `match` (`((Lo) Lo) ((Mid) Mid) ((Hi) Hi))` is **refuted** with `else-branch does not satisfy postcondition (constraint #3)`. Both functions are the identity. **Cause, CORRECTED at v0.16.2 by reading the emitted constraint system**: this row previously said a `match` arm never emits the term equation `scrutinee = Ctor`, and pointed at `$tag`. That was wrong and would have sent an implementer to a seam with no bearing on the defect: the emitted `.fq` contains **no** `$tag` variable for this shape, `tagKeys` does not contribute here, and the arm guards already refine the scrutinee directly. The real cause was the param binder. `emitParamBind` bound every param at `{ v : int | true }`, while a pure nullary enum is int-tag encoded to its declaration index `0..n-1` (`buildCtorTagMap` uses `zip [0..] ctors`). The last arm's guard is the negation of its siblings, so the final constraint read `(not (a=0)) && (not (a=1)) && (result=2) => (result=a)`, unsatisfiable at `a=7`. **The stated scope was also wrong**: this row said the defect affected every sum-type function in the language. Measured false. A post comparing the scrutinee to a **constructor** verified before the fix, and so did a full adjudicator mapping a nullary token sum to a nullary verdict sum. **Only a post naming the scrutinee VARIABLE across the equality failed**, which is why the in-tree corpus never hit it: it specifies classifiers by their inputs (`classify.llmll`). Same family as `MATCH-WIDEN`, and adjacent to the datatype-construction line (§5.3.3) which handles `(= result (Ctor n))` on a CONSTRUCTED result and not on a matched scrutinee. It did not surface earlier because the corpus specifies classifiers by their inputs (`(=> (>= n 0) (= result (Accepted n)))`, `classify.llmll`) rather than by the scrutinee, so the shape that fires has no in-tree population. Routed around, not blocked: an integer carrier with `if` guards verifies the same laws | **SHIPPED**: `nullaryEnumArity` ([`TypeAdmissibility.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeAdmissibility.hs)), consulted by `emitParamBind` ([`FixpointEmit.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/FixpointEmit.hs)), puts `(v >= 0) && (v <= n-1)` on the binder. It reuses the nullary test `isIntLike` already applies, so the gate and the fact derive from one predicate. QF-LIA, no new sort or theory. The fact enters as an environment **antecedent**, so it can only weaken an obligation; the single unsound direction is a wrong arity, pinned by tests at 2, 3 and 5 constructors. **The positive witness is frozen as a PAIR plus a crux** in `compiler/test/fixtures/match-term-eq/`: direct (control, verified before and after), matched (refuted to verified), and a refuting sibling that is still refuted, because a fixture asserting only that the matched form is SAFE would pass vacuously. **Not the `bytes[n]` case SAFE-ARG corrected**: that length is a caller obligation, this domain is guaranteed by the checker, executed rather than argued (an int at an enum position is a type error, and a constructor name shared across type definitions is rejected). **ONE ITEM OWED**: the `result` binder ([`FixpointEmit.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/FixpointEmit.hs), the post-constraint site) still carries `true`. The param binder alone moved the case, so it is not needed for this defect, but whether a post over a RETURNED enum needs the same fact is UNMEASURED. **Census DONE, on the `RESERVED-NAME-1` precedent**: payload-carrying arms do **not** share the gap. `clauseOverOpaqueSumParam` (MATCH-WIDEN, v0.14.12) forces contract-only fallback when a clause names a payload-bearing sum param by bare name, because an opaque sum has no value sort and would crash liquid-fixpoint with `Constraint with free vars`. Such a function reports `body-fallback`, so the verdict is disclosed as ungraded and `--strict-verified-core` rejects it. **The discriminator is the contract clause, not the type's signature position**: a payload sum in param position whose post does not name it stays body-faithful, and `examples/outcome-totality/classify.llmll` returns one and is body-faithful |
| **FRONTMATTER-GATE-1** (no gate checks that a design doc's frontmatter is well-formed YAML) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, found at v0.14.94 by breaking one and watching every gate stay green. **Executed rather than read off the source:** rewriting [`tool-ll-RESTART.md`](design/tool-ll-RESTART.md)'s `status:` block embedded double quotes inside its double-quoted YAML scalar, terminating the string early and making the frontmatter unparseable. [`doc_archive_gate.sh`](../scripts/doc_archive_gate.sh) (DRIFT-DOC-3), [`doc_path_lint.py`](../scripts/doc_path_lint.py) (DRIFT-DOC-4) and [`test_tool_rfc_standard.py`](../scripts/tests/test_tool_rfc_standard.py) all reported **PASS** against the broken file. **Two readers touch frontmatter and neither parses YAML:** DRIFT-DOC-3 extracts `archive-disposition` with an awk line match scoped to the block, and `_frontmatter` uses `re.match(r"^(\w+):\s*(.*)$", line)` with a `.strip('"')`. **The failure is invisible rather than wrong, and that is the precise shape:** probed on a synthetic malformed block, `_frontmatter` still returns `tool_state='oracle'` and a usable `status`, so the TOOL-RFC gate would pass an RFC no YAML consumer could read, without degrading any value it checks. Fails OPEN. **Census taken, on the `TOOL-ENCODING-1` precedent:** **67 of 318** tracked `.md` files carry frontmatter (42 `docs/design/`, 12 `archive/shipped-design-specs/`, 3 `archive/professor-reviews/`, 6 doc-archive fixtures) and **zero are currently unparseable**, which is why this has never fired. That row's lesson applies unchanged: the question is not whether the population is empty but whether it is where anything checks, and here nothing checks at all. The bite is that `tool-ll-RESTART.md` is the one file the campaign tells the next session to read FIRST, and it can be corrupted with every gate green | Add YAML well-formedness as a gate over the 67-file frontmatter population. PyYAML **6.0.1 is already available** to the harness and no script in `scripts/` imports it today (`grep -rln "import yaml" scripts/` exits 1), so the cost is one import rather than a dependency. It ships as **DRIFT-DOC-5** on the existing four-gate naming and belongs in the **banner job**, which needs no compiler. **The gate is the part with no precedent, and a passing run proves nothing here:** the in-tree unparseable population is zero, so the check must be mutation-checked against a deliberately malformed fixture or it is a green light over an empty set, which is `versiongate.llmll`'s vacuous `--strict-verified-core` pass one directory over. Whether the two line-oriented readers should ALSO move to a real parser is a separate call and deliberately not pre-empted: they work today, and replacing them is a behaviour change with its own blast radius |
| **RUN-STDIN-1** (`llmll run` hands every program an empty stdin, then hides the result) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, found at v0.14.94 by a TOOL-RFC-004 feasibility probe and **executed rather than read off the source**. **THREE defects in one command, filed as one row because they compose to hide each other and one patch closes them.** (i) [`Main.hs:903`](../compiler/app/Main.hs) runs the child as `readProcessWithExitCode stackBin [...] ""`, and that trailing `""` is the child's **stdin**. A `:mode console` program is a stdin-driven step machine, so it takes immediate EOF, executes **zero steps** and exits **70**, the harness's documented EOF convention. (ii) [`Main.hs:908`](../compiler/app/Main.hs) answers a failed child with `ExitFailure _ -> ... exitFailure`: the `_` **discards the child's code** and `exitFailure` is always 1, so the 70 that would have named the cause is reported as a bare 1. (iii) [`Main.hs:256`](../compiler/app/Main.hs) builds the pass-through from `many (strArgument ...)`, a POSITIONAL parser, so a flag-shaped argument is rejected by `llmll run`'s own parser with ``Invalid option `--root'`` although the help at [`Main.hs:186`](../compiler/app/Main.hs) advertises "Arguments passed through to the program". A `--` separator is the undocumented escape. **Measured, and the prediction that failed is why the mechanism is stated this precisely:** a probe built to a binary exits **70** on empty stdin and **0** with 50 stdin lines, printing both its expected lines; the SAME program through `llmll run` exits **1**, prints nothing, writes no diagnostic, and leaves an event log holding a header and no steps. An earlier hypothesis that the exit code would be the initial state's `:status` was **refuted** by changing that status to 7 and still observing 1, which is what exposed defect (ii). Confirmed on two independent programs. **Census, on the `TOOL-ENCODING-1` precedent: 20 of 20 committed `def-main` programs are `:mode console` and ZERO are `:mode cli`**, so the population where `llmll run` could work at all is empty. `:mode cli` is unaffected, reading `getArgs` rather than stdin, and per `MODE-CLI-1` it performs no `Command` and yields no exit status, so it is not an alternative for anything that touches the world. Fails silently in the worst way available: exit 1 with no output reads as "the program ran and failed", when the program ran correctly on input it was never given | **Forward the parent's stdin** rather than passing `""`, which means `createProcess` with `std_in = Inherit` instead of `readProcessWithExitCode`, and that also removes the output buffering that currently withholds a long-running gate's progress until it exits. **Propagate the child's code** with `exitWith code` rather than `exitFailure`; the two are independent and fixing only (ii) would leave a program that still does nothing while now reporting 70. **Give the pass-through `noIntersperse` or accept `--`**, and correct the help if the decision is that `--` is required, on the `--emit json-ast` precedent from v0.14.94 where the help text was the drifted side. **The gate is the part with no precedent, and a passing run proves nothing here**: every one of the 20 in-tree programs fails this way today, so the fixture must assert non-empty OUTPUT and a PROPAGATED non-zero code, not merely exit 0, and be mutation-checked by restoring the `""` and the `_`. Note the interaction with `CI-BUILD-TEST-1`: none of this is reachable from `stack test`, since no hspec example shells out to `llmll run` |
| **MODE-CLI-1** (`:mode cli` emits a pure `print` and can neither do IO nor set an exit status) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, filed by the TOOL-LL campaign's first port and deliberately not fixed there. **Measured:** `emitMainBody` for `ModeCli` ([`CodegenHs.hs:1837-1845`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) emits `args <- getArgs` then `print (step args)`. The step is a PURE function of `list[string]` whose result is printed; no `Command` is ever performed. So a `cli`-mode program cannot read a file, cannot write one, and cannot choose its exit status, which is `print` followed by an implicit 0. The mode parses ([`Parser.hs:488`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Parser.hs)) and is advertised as one of three. **In-tree firing population is ZERO**, measured across every committed `.llmll`. **The consequence is not local to `cli`:** `console` is the only usable entry mode for a program that touches the world, so every LLMLL program is a stdin-driven step machine consuming one line per step and exiting **70** on EOF. That is the single largest contributor to 58 code lines of `version_gate.sh` becoming 278 of LLMLL ([`design/tool-rfc-001-version-gate.md`](design/tool-rfc-001-version-gate.md) §5): seven file reads become seven arms plus a boot arm plus a decide arm, and the harness's per-step `putStrLn` puts a blank line in the output for every step whose command wrote nothing. **NEW EVIDENCE, measured 2026-08-10, and it is what the Next Action was waiting on.** This row says every program becomes a stdin-driven step machine consuming one line per step, which is true of the mechanism. Measured one level further: **four of the six committed console programs never read the line's CONTENT at all.** [`docarchive.llmll`](../tools/doc-archive/docarchive.llmll), [`docclaims.llmll`](../tools/doc-claims/docclaims.llmll), [`pathlint.llmll`](../tools/doc-path-lint/pathlint.llmll) and [`refutecrux.llmll`](../tools/refute-crux/refutecrux.llmll) each bind `input: string` in their top-level step and then dispatch on the control state and the `Response`, never on the line; all four are driven by `printf 'x\n%.0s'`. **stdin is a step CLOCK for them, not data.** The two that genuinely consume it are [`sequencer.llmll`](../tools/llmll-driver/sequencer.llmll) (8 sites) and [`replay-demo.llmll`](../examples/replay-demo/replay-demo.llmll), which is exactly the stream-processor population `PROC-BOUNDARY-1` Rev 3 measured, so **that row's category is confirmed here rather than contradicted**. The cost of the arrangement is a caller-supplied step count that nothing checks: under-supply exits a fixed 70 with no report, which is fail-closed by construction (`CodegenHs.hs:1768`, "no program exits 0 on a starved stdin") and is indistinguishable from a deliberate 70, disclosed at [`design/proc-boundary-1-proposal.md`](design/proc-boundary-1-proposal.md) §4.5 where reserving 70 was considered and rejected. **Shape (a) below removes the clock for those four; shape (b) does not.** Routed here rather than onto `PROC-BOUNDARY-1` because this row is the cause and that row governs the terminal status; settled by language-team 2026-08-10 after professor review | Two shapes, and the choice is a language-surface question that should not be made inside a codegen patch. **(a) Complete it:** `:step` returns `(state, Command)`, the harness performs it, and `:status` is honoured, which is `ModeConsole` minus the stdin loop and is what every tool in the TOOL-LL campaign actually wants. **(b) Withdraw it** from the parser, so the language stops advertising an entry mode that cannot do anything. Both are small. **A fixture is owed before either**, on the `REGEX-LOWER-1` and `RESERVED-NAME-1` precedent: a zero firing population means no existing gate would notice either way, which is how this reached v0.14.87 unexamined. Found by [`design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md`](design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md) §5 |
| **SPLIT-EMPTY-1** (`string-split` with an empty separator does not terminate, and there is no character decomposition) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, filed by the TOOL-LL campaign's first port. **Measured from the emitted preamble** ([`CodegenHs.hs:373-378`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)): the first equation `string_split _ [] = [""]` catches an empty SUBJECT, and the general equation guards on ``sep `isPrefixOf` s``, recursing on `drop (length sep) s`. For an empty separator `isPrefixOf "" s` is **always True** and `drop 0 s` is `s`, so `go` recurses on its own argument forever. `string-split "" ""` terminates by the first equation; `string-split "" "abc"` does not. **It typechecks and it VERIFIES**, `string-split` being uninterpreted, so the divergence is invisible to every check-time gate and surfaces only as a built program that hangs. **The row carries a design half as well as a defect, and THE DESIGN HALF IS REFUTED, 2026-08-16, by reading `builtinEnv`.** It claimed "no `string-to-list`, no character type, no integer range" and concluded that a string scan must be a fold over a literal index list with a hand-written bound. Two of those three absences are false and the conclusion falls with them. **`string-char-at : string int -> string`** is a `builtinEnv` entry at [`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs) and [`LLMLL.md`](../LLMLL.md) §13.6 documents it, returning `""` out of range. **`range`** shipped at v0.11 and is the same builtin that closed `LIST-RANGE-1` below. So `string-to-list` COMPOSES from two shipped names, `(list-map (range 0 (string-length s)) (fn [i: int] (string-char-at s i)))`, where `range` supplies the indices and `string-length` supplies the bound, and neither is hand-written. **`LLMLL.md` §13.5 ALREADY PRINTS that idiom**, `(list-map indices (fn [i: int] (string-char-at word i)))`, so the spec contained the refutation of this row the whole time. The idiom sits in §13.5 and the `string-char-at` row itself in §13.6, which is why a reader looking only at the string table would not find it. **ONE claim survives**: LLMLL has no character type, a character being a one-character string, which is a design fact and not an absence of decomposition. **THE DEFECT HALF IS UNTOUCHED and this row stays open on it**: `string-split ""` still does not terminate. **This is the SECOND row of this class**, `LIST-RANGE-1` being the first and closing the same day on the same ground: one read of the builtin list at filing time settles it. [`design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md`](design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md) §5 carries that correction and this row is its second citation. **The design half as filed follows, preserved so the claim survives the correction.** There is no character decomposition in the language at all: no `string-to-list`, no character type, no integer range. `string-split ""` is the obvious way to reach one and it is precisely the diverging call, so a scan over a string must be a fold over a **literal index list** with a hand-written bound (`versiongate.llmll`'s `leading-run`, bounded at 24 with a saturation check because a truncated semver is still a well-formed semver). Bounded-by-construction may well be the right idiom for a language with this decidability story, but today it is arrived at by accident | **Fix the divergence first, and choose the answer deliberately rather than for termination alone.** The two defensible values for `string_split "" str` are the CHARACTERS of `str`, which makes the builtin the decomposition primitive and closes the design half, or `[str]`, which makes it a no-op and leaves the half open. **The second sentence below is SUPERSEDED by the 2026-08-16 refutation and is kept for the record**: the decomposition idiom already exists and composes from `range` and `string-char-at`, so choosing `[str]` for `string_split ""` no longer leaves a design hole, and the question narrows to whether a `string-chars` name is worth having as sugar over a composition that works today. Then decide whether the bounded index-list fold is the intended idiom for string scanning or whether a `string-chars : string -> list[string]` belongs in the alphabet; note that an unbounded character fold is also the first unbounded loop in an otherwise bounded idiom. **A fixture is owed before either**: the in-tree firing population of `string-split ""` is ZERO, the same blind spot `REGEX-LOWER-1` records. Found by [`design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md`](design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md) §5 |
| **FS-EXISTS-1** (nothing answers "is there a file here" without moving the file's bytes) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-005 and owed a row since it was proposed. `os.path.exists` has no equivalent in the alphabet, so existence is decided by attempting a read: **measured**, `wasi.fs.read` on a missing path answers `RErr` carrying `openFile: does not exist`, which works and costs a full READ where the reference costs a stat. **On the live corpus that is 77.0 MB across about 1900 calls** in a job budgeted under a minute, so the port routes around it entirely by enumerating the tree ONCE with `git ls-files` and testing `list-contains`. **Deliberately NOT folded into `FS-STAT-1`**, which answers about an artifact's AGE for `liveness.advancing` and is a different question; collapsing two causes into one row is what produced a row that was wrong for a release at `ALIAS-LOWER-1` | A direct existence predicate, shape unsettled. `FS-STAT-1`'s clamp argument is the nearest precedent for how a filesystem probe lands in the effect catalog with no new `Response` arm. **A fixture is owed**: the workaround is invisible to every gate, since reading a present file and probing for it succeed identically. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md`](design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md) §5 |
| **LIST-KIND-1** (a listing carries no entry kind) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, raised by [`design/tool-rfc-004-doc-archive.md`](design/tool-rfc-004-doc-archive.md) §5 on 2026-08-09 and **carried for a release with no tag and no row**; verified absent from the campaign census, this roadmap and the restart record before filing. `wasi.fs.list` answers `RList [String]` ([`TypeCheck.hs:255`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) with no kind on any entry, so a file and a directory are indistinguishable without a second call. **The workaround is a control-flow use of an error channel**: `llmll_publish_io` catches `IOException` into `RErr` ([`CodegenHs.hs:503-506`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)), so listing an entry and probing its kind are the same operation and the response arm is the discriminator, at one IO call per entry. **A SECOND cause was measured at `FS-WALK-1`'s close and it is the sharper one: a symlink is not distinguishable at all.** `listDirectory` follows a symlink to a directory, so it answers `RList` exactly as a real directory does. Any recursive walk over a tree holding a symlink cycle therefore diverges, and no step budget repairs that: it truncates a divergent computation and reports exhaustion, which is a different claim from "the tree was walked" | An entry kind on the `RList` arm, or a separate probe. **Deliberately NOT folded into `FS-WALK-1`**, which asked about recursion where this asks about the shape of the listing result: same cause, different symptom, the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` distinction. **The symlink half is a prerequisite** for any future recursive-walk work and is recorded at `FS-WALK-1`'s closed row. Measured 2026-08-10: **zero** symlinks across `compiler/.stack-work` and `generated/`, so nothing in the tree exercises it today |
| **REGEX-CAPTURE-1** (`regex-match` returns `bool`, so nothing captures and nothing scans) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-005. `regex-match : (string, string) -> bool` cannot report WHERE it matched or WHAT, so it cannot locate a citation, a field or a delimiter inside a body. Independent of `REGEX-LOWER-1`, which was about lowering and shipped at v0.14.96. **The cost was measured and this row's first revision over-stated it**: the gap does NOT force a character-level scanner. A backtick-delimited citation is recoverable by splitting the body on the delimiter and validating each segment with one `regex-match`, so the cost is one `string-split`. **Measured against the reference over the whole live corpus: 955 citations, 172 files, ZERO disagreements** | A capturing form returning positions or matched text. **The residual cost is the part worth keeping**: the split model had to be measured against the reference rather than read off the regex, and **two plausible versions of it are wrong**. Keeping only odd-index segments per line desyncs on a backtick span that crosses a newline; doing it over the whole body finds **16 citations where the reference finds 94**. All three models agree on almost every file, which is why the instrument was a corpus-wide comparison and why a spot check would have passed. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md`](design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md) §4 |
| **REGEX-CASE-1** (no case-insensitive matching) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-005. **Measured**: `regex-match` lowers to `Text.Regex.TDFA`, which **rejects inline `(?i)` by returning False** rather than erroring, and no lowercase builtin exists, so a case-insensitive alternation has no direct form. **Live relevance measured rather than assumed**: `previously` matches `Previously` twice on the current tree, so the gap fires on real prose and not only on a fixture. It is silent in the dangerous direction, a rejected `(?i)` being a match that quietly does not happen | A case-insensitive flag on `regex-match`, or a `string-lower` builtin; the two close different amounts of the row. **The workaround is exact rather than an approximation**, which is why this is SHAPES and not BLOCKS: per-letter bracket classes (`[Pp][Rr]…`) are measured working and were settled at TOOL-RFC-005 D3 deliberately over a lowercase-then-compare approximation. The cost is verbosity at every case-folded site. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md`](design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md) §5 |
| **PATH-NORM-1** (no path normalization for `..`) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-005. No `os.path.normpath` equivalent exists, so a citation of the form `a/b/../c` cannot be resolved to `a/c` before it is tested for existence. **Measured on the live corpus: 58 of 947 citations contain `..`**, so this sits on the main path of a prose-citation check rather than at its edge, and a port that skipped it would silently fail to resolve six percent of what it checks | A `normpath` equivalent, or path values carrying their own type. The workaround folds the segment list and drops one segment per `..`, which is a handful of lines and is exercised on every real run. **The obligation it introduces stays in QF-LIA**: the fold's postcondition is a `list-length` relation over ints (`LLMLL.md` §5.3.3), so nothing here escapes the auto-discharged fragment. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md`](design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md) §5 |
| **PROC-ENV-1** (nothing SETS an environment variable for a child) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-006 on 2026-08-11. `wasi.proc.run` takes no environment. **`wasi.env.get` now exists and does NOT close this row**: it READS a variable and this row is about SETTING one for a child. **F7 IS RECONCILED, and the reconciliation is a measurement rather than history.** F7 recorded that two counts of the `wasi.*` set disagree at fourteen and at sixteen, and that nobody had measured whether the set changed between the two dates. **It did not change.** The count was 14 at every release from v0.14.90 through v0.14.99, and it is **15** from v0.15.0 with `wasi.env.get`. [`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs) holds sixteen distinct `wasi.*` strings, of which fourteen were `builtinEnv` entries and two are the capability namespaces `wasi.fs` and `wasi.io`. **Both counts were correct about different things**, so the disagreement is a category error and not drift. **Measured**: a child INHERITS the parent's environment, so `LC_ALL=C` set outside an LLMLL program does reach the child it spawns, and the program still cannot set it. One site needs this, [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) line 450, where the FS-ENCODING-1 stage pipes stdin into the built binary under `LC_ALL=C`. **Deliberately NOT folded into the campaign's older "no env access" row**, which is about a program READING its own environment and was tested at port 005 and held; this row is about SETTING one for a child. Same namespace, two directions, and collapsing them is the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` error. **MEASURED 2026-08-16 against `llmll 0.16.0` on aarch64-osx, by a probe that ran five cells and printed what the runtime answered, and the measurement NARROWS this row rather than closing it.** `wasi.proc.run "/usr/bin/env" ["LC_ALL=C" "/usr/bin/env"]` exits 0 and the CHILD's environment holds `LC_ALL=C`, while the parent's own environment holds no `LC_ALL` at all, and the byte delta between the two environments is exactly 9, which is the length of the assignment plus its newline. Two assignments at once work, and the child may carry its own arguments. **A stdin path passed to that same `wasi.proc.run` call REACHES THE CHILD**, because `env` execs in place and the file descriptor survives the exec. The FS-ENCODING-1 stage needs an environment assignment and a stdin feed at the same time, so that is the cell that decides the port. **So this row does not block the port, and the RFC's claim that it did is refuted**: [`design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md`](design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md) §2 recorded stage 5b FS-ENCODING-1 as BLOCKED on this row, and a sibling correction is removing that. **What stays open is why the row does not close.** `wasi.proc.run` still takes no environment; setting one variable costs one extra process and one executable outside the language, and the assignment reaches the child only through an intermediary the language does not model. This is the shape `LIST-RANGE-1` had, a row filed and then refuted by the very stage it was filed against, with one difference that decides both: `LIST-RANGE-1` CLOSED on 2026-08-16 because the builtin it called absent had shipped at v0.11, while this row's remaining half composes from nothing in the alphabet | An environment parameter on `wasi.proc.run`, or a `wasi.env.set` name. The workaround spawns `/usr/bin/env` with the assignments and the real command in one argv vector, measured working beside the stdin path that `PROC-STDIN-1` shipped, so the `sh -c` string this row previously named is no longer needed at that site. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md`](design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md) §5 |
| **EVENT-CAPTURE-1** (`LLMLL.md` §10a specifies an event-log capture mechanism that the compiler does not have) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, found by `ENV-READ-1`'s implementation on 2026-08-14 while checking whether that row's own security rationale rested on anything. [`LLMLL.md`](../LLMLL.md) §10a states that `:deterministic true` "captures the return value of every call and appends it to the Event Log", and that on replay those calls "read from the log instead of invoking the real system call". §10 item 8 states that replay is bitwise deterministic for modules that set the flag on clock and PRNG imports. **NEITHER IS IMPLEMENTED.** Measured four ways at v0.14.99, then confirmed by execution. (i) `capDeterministic` has exactly ONE consumer in the source tree, [`AstEmit.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/AstEmit.hs), which serialises it to JSON; nothing reads it in codegen, in the type checker, or in replay. (ii) `emitEventLogPreamble` in [`CodegenHs.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs) writes the `captures` array as a **literal `[]`**, and its `eventJsonL` takes five parameters of which none is a capture. (iii) [`Replay.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Replay.hs) never mentions captures; `runReplay` spawns the built executable and compares logged stdout, injecting nothing. (iv) `ReplayStatus`, `Replayable` and `BestEffortReplay` have **zero** consumers outside [`Syntax.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Syntax.hs). **The witness was built and run rather than read off the source**: a console program carrying `:deterministic true` on a clock import and issuing the clock every step produces a log whose every event records `"captures":[]`. So the flag parses, round-trips through the JSON-AST, and does nothing. **This is a specification claiming a guarantee, which is worse than a missing feature**: a reader budgets for replay determinism they do not have, and `ENV-READ-1`'s refusal of the flag reads as a live protection when it is a forward one | Two shapes, and the choice is language-team's rather than an engineer's. **(a) Build it**: capture each command's `Response` into the log's `captures` array and inject it on replay, which is what §10a already describes and would make the existing text true. **(b) Delete the guarantee**: strike the capture sentence and narrow item 8 to what replay actually does, which is compare logged stdout against a re-run. **A third and smaller piece is owed either way and was already named before this**: item 8 enumerates clock and PRNG imports and reads as a universal, which becomes a defect the moment a third source exists, and `wasi.env.get` is now that third source. Do not repair one side silently: the compiler is correct here and the spec is not, so a doc pass must not quietly rewrite §10a to match. Found in [`design/env-read-1-implementation-plan.md`](design/env-read-1-implementation-plan.md) finding F1, which routed it here |
| **FS-RMDIR-1** (nothing removes a DIRECTORY, so a gate cannot clear the state it is about to measure) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.16.0.** `wasi.fs.rmdir : string -> Command` removes an **empty** directory, is idempotent on a missing path, and answers `RErr` for a non-empty directory and for a path naming a file. **The recursive-or-empty question settled EMPTY-ONLY**, and the ground is a gap rather than taste: there is no runtime capability check (`CAP-1-REAL`), so a recursive delete would run under a root that is declared and unenforced, and composing recursion in LLMLL is unsound over symlinks today (`LIST-KIND-1`). No new `Response` arm, no new effect label (it joins the existing `EFsWrite`), no new namespace, no schema delta; Σ_eff stays seven-wide and the `wasi.*` surface moves fifteen to sixteen. **The port now grades what it could only report**: `buildsmoke.llmll` stage 5 clears the scratch directory with three idempotent deletes and one `rmdir`, and the NOT-GRADED path stays with its trigger changed from "no builtin removes a directory" to "the clear did not succeed". Both cells were run and they differ only by one unexpected entry, so the graded pass is earned. **THE ROW FOUND A LIVE DEFECT ON THE WAY IN AND THE SAME COMMIT FIXES IT**: `wasi.fs.delete` published `RNone` for a directory and removed nothing, because `doesFileExist` answers `False` on a directory and the idempotence guard took its false branch. That is success reported for a removal that did not happen. It now answers `RErr` naming `wasi.fs.rmdir`; **zero in-tree call sites pass a directory**, measured across `tools/`, `examples/`, `scripts/` and `compiler/test/`. Design: [`design/fs-capability-trio-proposal.md`](design/fs-capability-trio-proposal.md) Rev 2, whose §3 is this row and whose §11 routed the delete defect here. Original filing follows. **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-006's port on 2026-08-12. `wasi.fs.delete` removes a FILE ([`LLMLL.md`](../LLMLL.md) §13.9) and no builtin removes a directory, empty or otherwise. **The consequence is a vacuous assertion and not merely a missing convenience.** [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) stage 5 runs `rm -rf /tmp/llmll-capproc-exec` BEFORE the fixture runs, then asserts the directory exists afterwards, which is how it grades `wasi.fs.mkdir`. Without the delete, a directory left by any earlier run satisfies that assertion whatever this run's `mkdir` did. **The port therefore MEASURES whether the directory pre-existed and prints `NOT GRADED: wasi.fs.mkdir` when it did**, rather than reporting a pass it did not earn: that is `SKIP-SILENT-1`'s class, and a gate that cannot decide must not report that it decided. Measured 2026-08-12: consecutive runs of [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll) produce the graded line then the NOT-GRADED line, which is the row's own witness | **Closed by the empty-only shape.** The recursive-or-empty question was the design decision and it settled empty-only, on the two grounds in the status column. **`FS-EXISTS-1` and `FS-STAT-1` STAY OPEN and are NOT closed by this**: three questions about the filesystem that share no answer, and folding them is the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` error. Both were proposed alongside this row and both were held back after measurement refuted part of each — see [`design/fs-capability-trio-proposal.md`](design/fs-capability-trio-proposal.md) §4 and §6. **One follow-on is owed and is not a defect here**: `FR-5` pins the `wasi.fs.delete` fix by preamble shape rather than by behaviour, because the hspec suite does not build and run generated programs; the runtime evidence is the witness in the design record |
| **BYTES-READ-1** (no byte-level file access; `wasi.fs.read` is UTF-8 text only) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, filed 2026-08-15 by TOOL-RFC-006's port, which spawns `od` to hex-dump a program's stdout because the language cannot look at bytes. `wasi.fs.read : string -> Command` delivers `RText`, decoded as UTF-8 ([`CodegenHs.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)), so **a file whose bytes are not valid UTF-8 cannot be read at all** and a valid one cannot be inspected byte-wise. **This is not a missing function, and that is why it is Tier L.** Two independent obstacles meet here. (i) The `Response` arm set carries no bytes arm, and a new arm must pass the four-part admissibility rule at [`design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md`](design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md). (ii) LLMLL's byte type is `bytes[n]`, **length-indexed**, and a file's length is not known statically, so `bytes[n]` cannot type a file read even if an arm existed. **Related but NOT the same row**: `FS-ENCODING-1` was about the ENCODING a read uses and shipped; this is about there being no byte channel at all. `wasi.fs.copy` and `wasi.fs.sha256` already move and hash bytes **inside** builtins precisely because the language cannot hold them | Two shapes and the choice is language-team's. **(a) A hex-text read**, `wasi.fs.hex : string -> Command` answering lowercase hex as `RText`, on the `wasi.fs.sha256` precedent: that name already reads bytes and answers hex text, so it needs **no new arm and no new type** and would remove the `od` spawn outright. **(b) A real byte sequence**, which needs a non-indexed byte type and a new `Response` arm, and is the larger change. Shape (a) is the in-scope move and shape (b) is the principled one; do not ship (a) while describing it as (b) |
| **BYTES-WRITE-1** (nothing writes bytes that are not text; `wasi.fs.write` encodes its argument as UTF-8) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, filed 2026-08-16 by TOOL-RFC-006's port, which spawns `/usr/bin/printf` to create a fixture input the language cannot author. **Dispositioned SHAPES**: the port proceeds and the substitution is one exec. `wasi.fs.write : (string, string) -> Command` ([`TypeCheck.hs:163`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) takes a path and a STRING, and the runtime opens the handle, sets its encoding to UTF-8 and writes characters ([`CodegenHs.hs:549-554`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)), so there is no route from a module's source to an arbitrary byte. The encoding pin is deliberate and its own comment gives the reason: the byte image of a write should be a property of the program and not of the locale. **The site is stage 5b, FS-ENCODING-1**, which must create the fixture's input `bin.dat`, a file carrying a lone `0xFF` that no `.llmll` string literal can hold; [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll) therefore hands `/usr/bin/printf` a format of octal escapes and redirects its stdout into the file. **MEASURED 2026-08-16**: the file that lands is `62 69 6e 61 72 79 20 ff fe 00 20 72 61 77 0a`, which is [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) line 402's `printf 'binary \377\376\000 raw\n'` byte for byte. The substitution is therefore exact, and what is missing is the route rather than the result. **Same namespace as `BYTES-READ-1` and the OPPOSITE direction, so it is a separate row.** That row is about getting bytes INTO the language; this one is about getting them OUT of it. Folding opposite directions into one row is the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` error this project has already made once, and the same split was made deliberately for `ENV-READ-1` against `PROC-ENV-1` and for `FS-STAT-1` against `FS-EXISTS-1`. `wasi.fs.copy`'s own comment records the read half of the same wall, that a read-then-write cannot express a copy of a binary artifact. **How this was found is the discipline working, and it is `LIST-KIND-1`'s failure with the polarity reversed**: the port's comment at stage 5b NAMES the tag `BYTES-WRITE-1` and states the gap, and the tag appeared in NO roadmap row, so a search over tag names finds the name and learns nothing from it. `LIST-KIND-1` sat in no census for a release because port 004 recorded it with no tag at all; here the tag existed and the row did not | A byte-level write, or a hex-text write on the `wasi.fs.sha256` precedent, that name already producing hex text from bytes inside a builtin. **Design it PAIRED with `BYTES-READ-1` so the two directions settle together**: that row offers a hex-text read needing no new `Response` arm and no new type, and a real byte sequence needing both, and choosing one shape for reading and the other for writing is the outcome to avoid. Found by TOOL-RFC-006's port at [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll) stage 5b, whose comment carries the measurement above |
| **PLATFORM-1** (nothing reports the host platform or operating system name) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, filed 2026-08-16 by TOOL-RFC-006's port, which spawns `/usr/bin/uname` with `["-s"]` and tests the answer for `Darwin` because no name in the language reports the host. **Dispositioned SHAPES**: the port proceeds, and the language forced a design it would not otherwise have. [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) branches on `uname -s` in its FS-ENCODING-1 stage and prints NOT EXERCISED on Darwin, where GHC resolves UTF-8 whatever `LC_ALL` says, so the stage's encoding claim is untestable there; the port must reproduce that verdict, so it needs the platform. **THE NEGATIVE TEST WAS RUN BEFORE FILING, and two compositions were checked and rejected**, which is the correction at [`design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md`](design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md) §5: read the builtin list, then check whether the operation COMPOSES from what is there, before filing an absence. (a) `wasi.env.get` shipped at v0.15.0 and READS a variable, but no exported environment variable names the platform portably. (b) `stack path --local-install-root` answers `aarch64-osx` on Darwin and `x86_64-linux` on Linux, and [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll) ALREADY holds that string for another reason, so this composition costs no new spawn. It is rejected because a test on it must default to NOT EXERCISED to fail in the safe direction, which INVERTS the subject's polarity (the subject treats Darwin as the exception and every other platform as honouring the locale) and so diverges from the reference on any platform that is neither Darwin nor Linux. **A probe of the fixture is not the answer, and the subject says so itself**: `build_smoke.sh` refuses to discriminate by probing, and gives the reason in its own comment, that after the encoding fix the program behaves identically under both locales by construction, so nothing the fixture can print distinguishes them | A language-level platform or host name, in the shape of the existing `wasi.env.get`; or a decision that spawning `uname` is the permanent answer for this class. **The spawn owes this row rather than passing unseen**: under the amended gap discipline at [`design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md`](design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md) §5, "A spawned utility is a workaround, and the FFI bar does not catch it", a spawned program that does work the language should do owes a row, and a zero-FFI count cannot see it. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md`](design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md) §5 |
| **ENV-READ-1** (a program cannot READ its own environment, and argv does not carry what it did not receive) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.15.0** (commit `cd9cb42`). **THE SHIPPED SIGNATURE IS NOT THE ONE THIS ROW ASKED FOR, and that is a supersession rather than a miss.** The Next Action column proposed a name "answering `Result[string,string]`". What ships is `wasi.env.get : string -> Command`, delivering `RText` for a set variable and `RErr` for an unset one **on the response channel**, because a `Command` is a request to perform an effect and its result arrives as a `Response` (§9.7); an in-band `Result` return would make this the only `wasi.*` name that answers that way. **The property this row actually wanted is preserved and was measured on a built program**: `ENVPROBE=hello` delivers `RText "hello"`, `ENVPROBE=` delivers `RText ""`, and an unset name delivers `RErr`, so set-and-empty stays distinguishable from unset. Three fail-closed type-level side conditions ship with it, each with a firing witness and a sibling that does not fire: `:deterministic true` on a `wasi.env` import is a type error (and stays legal on `wasi.clock`), and a **literal** name that is empty or contains `=` is a type error (a computed one is not, and that limit is pinned by a test rather than hidden). The `wasi.*` surface moves fourteen to fifteen and Σ_eff widens six to seven with a new `env.read` label, which is the one place this diverges from `PROC-BOUNDARY-1`'s no-catalog-growth precedent: a summary reporting `nondet` cannot separate a clock read from a credential read. **`PROC-ENV-1` is NOT closed by this**; the two directions are one namespace and two rows, and collapsing them is the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` error. Design: [`design/env-channel-proposal.md`](design/env-channel-proposal.md) Rev 2; implementation and measurements: [`design/env-read-1-implementation-plan.md`](design/env-read-1-implementation-plan.md), which also filed `EVENT-CAPTURE-1`. Original filing follows. **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-006's port on 2026-08-12, and it is a **reclassification rather than a new discovery**. The campaign's census carried "no env access" as COSMETIC with the rationale "argv carries it", TESTED at port 005 where it HELD. **It fails on its second use.** [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) line 118 tries three sources for the compiler in order: `$LLMLL_BIN`, then `command -v llmll`, then `$HOME/.local/bin/llmll`. A `--subject` flag reproduces the first and a bare `llmll` reproduces the second. **The third needs `$HOME`, and argv carries what a caller passes.** So [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll) drops that branch, which is a behaviour difference and not an invocation difference, and a port is supposed to reproduce its reference. `sh -c 'echo $HOME'` would work and is REFUSED, because TOOL-RFC-006 D1 forbids this port handing any string to a shell; taking it would trade a stated gap for an unstated one. **Distinct from `PROC-ENV-1`**, which is about SETTING a variable for a child: same namespace, opposite directions, and collapsing them is the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` error the campaign already made once | A `wasi.env.get` name, answering `Result[string,string]` so an unset variable is distinguishable from an empty one. **The general lesson is the more useful half and belongs in the census, not only here**: a disposition tested against one port measures that port, not the language. "Nothing lost" survived one use and failed the next |
| **PROC-REDIRECT-1** (the three redirection paths are three same-typed positional strings, and one of them carries a rule) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, raised by TOOL-RFC-006 and its professor review on 2026-08-11, and filed so an interim is recorded as an interim. `wasi.proc.run` takes `stdout-path`, `stderr-path` and `stdin-path` as `string`, so a wrong permutation of the first two type-checks; `LLMLL.md` §13 has recorded that since the builtin shipped. **`PROC-STDIN-1` did not fix this and was not meant to.** It placed the new path after the `int` so the most probable transposition, `stdin-path` against `stdout-path`, became a type error, and that mitigation is a **coincidence of the current signature**: exactly one parameter is an `int` and nothing pins it at position 6. A second added path has no second `int`. **The string-equality test compounds it**: `out == err` means MERGE, and equality on a representation is an approximation of resource identity that `LLMLL.md` §13 already admits (`"log"` and `"./log"` do not merge) | A redirection type covering all three paths, not a `Stdin` sum type for one of them. The one-of-three shape was considered at TOOL-RFC-006 and rejected: it makes one sibling a different kind of thing and leaves the other two blind. Such a type must also absorb the merge rule that string equality now carries, which is why this is its own row and not an amendment. **No builtin takes a `TCustom` argument today** ([`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs), `builtinEnv`), so this would be first of its kind and is not free |
| **PROC-PATH-LINT-1** (nothing warns when a `wasi.proc.run` call names one literal path as both an input and an output) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, proposed by professor review of TOOL-RFC-006 on 2026-08-11 and **deliberately not built**. `PROC-STDIN-1` defines what `in == out` does rather than rejecting it: the child reads an empty file, because `WriteMode` truncates first. That is correct and it matches the shell, and it is still almost certainly a defect when a writer does it. A **runtime** rejection was considered and dropped, because it shortens no repair distance for an agent, which is the governing criterion at the top of this file | `tcWarnOrError` ([`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)), which warns normally and raises an error under `--strict`. Fire when the `stdin-path` argument and either output-path argument are **syntactically equal string literals** and the literal is not the null device. **It must not fire on `stdout-path == stderr-path`**, which is the merge idiom and is correct. **A positive witness is owed before this ships**: `(wasi.proc.run "cat" [] "." "log.txt" "err.txt" 30 "log.txt")` must fire and `(... "log.txt" "log.txt" 30 "/dev/null")` must not. **Nothing in the tree's 17 call sites trips it today**, so a guard built now would have no in-tree witness |
| **DRIVER-LL** (a fully functional RFC-SWARM driver in LLMLL) `[EXP][CT]` | **OPEN. Phases 0-3 COMPLETE; sub-phases 4a, 4b, 4c and 4e SHIPPED.** Sub-phase state MEASURED from git on 2026-08-18 rather than carried forward, after this cell read "Next is 4c" while 4c and 4e were both already in main: **4a** `2b82464`, first tagged **v0.14.86** (v0.14.85 shipped `PROC-BOUNDARY-1`, the unblocker, not 4a); **4b** `ba2f93d`, **v0.14.87**; **4c** `b9904a6` (stages D, F, G), **v0.14.88**; **4e** `fa859b7` + `6511bf8` (`wave.llmll` and its seven-cell cover), **v0.14.88**. Parts of **4d** also landed at v0.14.88 (`oracle.llmll` `375aaf6`, `--llmll-cmd` `4c0014f`) while 4d itself stays parked. **Neither 4a nor 4c has a CHANGELOG entry** ("sub-phase 4c" appears zero times), so their design docs and commits are the only record. **Nothing is queued:** the CI-gate port that followed 4e is TOOL-LL, complete and closed at v0.16.1. What remains is 4d parked, 4f and program unification deferred, and stage A a filed STOP on `HTTP-GET-1`. Phases 0 and 1 released as v0.14.80; CAP-PROC v0.14.81; JSON-1 v0.14.82 closed Phase 2; **Phase 3 closes at v0.14.83 with stage A filed as a STOP**. [`../tools/llmll-driver/spine.llmll`](../tools/llmll-driver/spine.llmll) runs stages **E, J, L and G2** end to end against the committed TFTP execution, built and RUN, zero FFI declarations, **nine** body-faithful `def`s carrying the pins as contracts, each stage's verdict shown eliminative by pin perturbation in both the verify and the runtime channel. **Stage A is a STOP, not an omission**: it fetches over HTTP GET (`HTTP-GET-1`), no RFC source bytes are committed anywhere in this repository so its `if dest.exists()` skip cannot fire, and `self_test()` carries no stage A block, so it contributes nothing to the acceptance clause. Per the campaign's STOP rule the stage halts and files the gap. **Two stages are ported in part and say so in their own sections**: L pins only RFC-COV-1's exit status, which is the only value `self_test` pins for it, and G2 ports the strength half only, the pinned RFCs being deliberately absent from this repository. **Phase 4 is under way: sub-phases 4a and 4b have SHIPPED, and 4c is MERGED with its release ceremony HELD.** Its first three tasks shipped at v0.14.84 and its proposal stands at **Rev 11**. 4c's narrative and its version land with that ceremony, not before it. **4a** (`2b82464`, v0.14.85) lands the sequencer, the manifest, the resume gate and both halt channels, and it ships **with no shim**: it was blocked on the process boundary, `PROC-BOUNDARY-1` was specified and shipped to close it, and the port then took argv as flags and exits through `:status`, so nothing sits between the acceptance assertions and the program under test. **4b** (`ba2f93d`, v0.14.87) lands stages B, C and I plus §6's validation obligations as **one shared facility**: `verdict-of` takes no string, so no subject's conventions are in scope, and three of its clauses **prove** that 4b constructs exactly two of `Outcome`'s four arms where the design had asserted it. The transition cover stands at **31 cells**. Plans: [`design/driver-ll-phase4a-implementation-plan.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4a-implementation-plan.md) and [`design/driver-ll-phase4b-implementation-plan.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4b-implementation-plan.md), [`design/driver-ll-phase4c-implementation-plan.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4c-implementation-plan.md). The 4b port also found `PROC-TIMEOUT-1`, filed above and not fixed there. **Two of Phase 4's filed gaps are Phase 5 constraints, recorded rather than repaired.** `SPEC-TIER-1`: driver-spec §15.1:504-505 requires every obligation to occupy exactly one of three tiers, yet §15.1:509's range sentence ("sections 4 through 13") contradicts both its own characterisation at `:509-510` and its enumeration at `:512-515`, neither of which covers reporting, so §13's prose MUSTs fit none of the three. It is a **target-spec defect** and driver-spec is pinned (§14:473-474), so it cannot be repaired here; it bounds what Phase 5's §15.4 conformance claim may assert, and no tier is manufactured for those MUSTs (stretching §15.2's scope sentence past "effectful operations" is the over-reading the phase has been refuted on twice). `FS-ISOLATION-1`: `audit_blindness` implements driver-spec §8:330-332 and the port defers it, like `--status`; a spec obligation deferred as operator surface, which carries a **disclosure obligation at phase close under §15.1:504-505** rather than being dropped. Both are filed in [`design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md) §14. Open work: [`design/driver-ll-open-work.md`](design/driver-ll-open-work.md); findings: [`../experiments/rfc-swarm/DRIVER-LL-PORT-FINDINGS.md`](../experiments/rfc-swarm/DRIVER-LL-PORT-FINDINGS.md) | Five phases: DO-ACCUM-1 (P0-marker / DISCARD-1; sequenced first for attributability, **not** because Phases 1-5 depend on it), EFFECT-RESP, CAP-PROC + JSON-1 in parallel, the mechanical spine (stages A/E/G2/J/L over the proved cores already shipped in [`../tools/llmll-driver/`](../tools/llmll-driver/) at v0.14.70), then the agent-delegated stages with a **serial** wave, then the §15.4 conformance claim. Serial is a decision, not a limitation found late: LLMLL has no concurrency surface, and parallelism is throughput rather than correctness. Standing rule, pre-registered: every `haskell.*` or `c.*` declaration written in driver code is a **filed gap with a named missing capability**, and the FFI declaration count is a reported metric of every phase; the bar from Phase 3 onward is **zero**. Without it the exercise measures persistence rather than the language. Supersedes [`design/rfc-swarm-roadmap-proposal.md`](design/rfc-swarm-roadmap-proposal.md) §5.2 ("do not activate R2") **for this campaign only**; §5.2 stands for the demo, whose acceptance criteria it was scoped to. Design: [`design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md`](design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md). |
| **STRLIT-BODY-1** (a string-literal comparison in a `def` BODY falls back) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, found by DRIVER-LL Phase 3 | `LLMLL.md §5.3.5`'s STRLIT reflection covers a `(= s "lit")` predicate in **contract** position; a `def` whose body performs the comparison falls back to `contract-checked` whatever its contract says. Measured at v0.14.82: an int-only body is body-faithful, `(= s "0.9535")` is body-fallback, mixed is body-fallback. Bite: it bounds what any proved centre over extracted scalars can cover, which is the shape every ported driver stage uses. Disclosed in [`../tools/llmll-driver/spine.llmll`](../tools/llmll-driver/spine.llmll) rather than hidden. |
