| **RESULT-CTOR-RRW** (thread the declaring type into `rewriteCtor`) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, partial fix shipped v0.14.82 | `CodegenHs.rewriteCtor` rewrites any constructor named `Success`/`Error` to `Right`/`Left` with no knowledge of its declaring type, because `Result[t,e]` emits as `Either e t`. v0.14.82 ships the **guard** (a check-time `reserved-constructor-name` error) rather than the fix, so the two names are now unusable in a user sum type. The principled fix threads the declaring type through `emitPat`/`emitExpr` so user constructors are never confused with `Result`'s, and frees the names. |
| **JSON-TESTSUITE-1** (differential-test the JSON parser against `nst/JSONTestSuite`) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, undelivered from JSON-1 | The parser ships at the `asserted` tier over agent-authored input and its only oracle is a ten-case hand-written battery in [`../scripts/build-smoke/capproc_exec.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/capproc_exec.llmll). The standard ~318-case corpus needs a decision first: the parser exists only as codegen preamble **text**, so a differential run against `aeson` (already a compiler dependency, free to the generated closure) needs either a second copy of the parser inside the compiler or one GHC build per case. |
| **ADT-CYCLE-TLIST** (`admissibleDatatype` does not descend `TList` payloads) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, routed from JSON-1 | `FixpointEmit.hs` `sumOf` descends `TSumType` and `TCustom` only, so a sum recursive through `list[…]` is classified a leaf and receives a **fielded** `FQDataDecl`. `LLMLL.md:452` says such a sum is "firewalled"; it is not, by that mechanism. Measured: the shape tested still lands at `body-fallback`, so this is stated-mechanism drift and **not** a claim that a false body-faithful verdict exists. Decide whether the spec sentence or the gate moves. |
| **CAP-1-REAL** (the `capability` clause is declarative; four spec sentences claimed enforcement) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN — design question, not a bug fix.** **A NEW SURFACE ARRIVED AT v0.14.98 and it is recorded here rather than in its own row.** `PROC-STDIN-1` gave `wasi.proc.run` a stdin path, so the `wasi.proc` namespace now performs a FILESYSTEM READ. Nothing breaks today, because the clause is declarative. When `CAP-1` enforces, a program holding `exec` and no `fs.read` can name a file, spawn `cat`, and collect the bytes from the stdout path it does own. The stdin path is the first parameter of this builtin that reads the filesystem, so the hole is new even though the enforcement gap is old. Docs corrected at v0.14.80; the code is unchanged. Absorbs **WASI-RT residue (ii)**, which is the same finding seen from one operation. Measured at HEAD with four witnesses: a bare `(import wasi.fs)` with no clause authorizes every `wasi.fs.*` call; `(capability read "/tmp")` authorizes `wasi.fs.delete` and a write to any path; an invented verb parses and is ignored; and a wholly absent namespace import errors **only for a builtin in APPLICATION position**. That last clause read "only a wholly absent namespace import errors" until the DRIFT-CI-1 LLMLL port refuted it by tripping over it. **CAP-NULLARY-1, a fifth witness and the first that reaches an effect with NO import at all:** `checkWasiCapability` is invoked from exactly one site, `inferExpr (EApp func args)` ([`TypeCheck.hs:2033`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)), so a builtin used as a bare value is an `EVar` that never reaches the check. Measured at v0.14.87 with a control: a module importing only `wasi.io` and evaluating `wasi.proc.args` passes `llmll check` clean, and so does the same module with `wasi.clock.monotonic`, while the same module calling `(wasi.fs.read p)` is correctly rejected with the capability error. Both nullary `wasi.*` builtins are affected and they are the whole population, so **argv and the wall clock are reachable today with no capability import naming them**. `crux-shell-undeclared-authority.llmll` does not cover this: it writes the filesystem, which is an application. The site's own comment explains the placement as covering every nesting context an `EApp` can appear in, which is exactly the reasoning that misses the form that is not an application. `checkWasiCapability` ([`TypeCheck.hs:1543-1553`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) tests `importPath imp == ns`; `importCapability` has no consumer outside JSON re-emission. `CodegenHs` has zero capability references, so `LLMLL.md`'s former runtime-`CapabilityError` claim had no implementation | Split into three, per the language-team proposal. **CAP-1-WF** (an import reaching `wasi.*` must carry a clause) is well-formedness with **zero in-tree migration cost**, measured. **CAP-1-VERB** (the granted verb names an operation set, checked by membership) is a *total* static check with no silent region, because a called builtin is a name and never a computed value; migration is one file (`smoke.llmll` needs `stderr`, `delete`, `response`) plus dropping a caveat from `crux-shell-undeclared-authority.llmll`, which today passes for a coarser reason than its comment claims. **CAP-1-TARGET** is the open design question: a path is a value-level property with a silent region wherever it is computed, so a literal-only static check is worse than none. Recommended shape is to stratify it as `LLMLL.md §5.3.3` already stratifies contracts, since the target *is* a precondition on a builtin's argument; note that a prefix predicate over strings is outside Σ_auto today. **Do not ship CAP-1-VERB as "capabilities are enforced"** without saying the target is not. Blocks the driver-spec §15.2 conformance wording at DRIVER-LL Phase 5; blocks nothing before it |
| **CONSOLE-INIT-1** (a console `def-main` with no `:init` ignores the step's declared state type) `[CT]` | **OPEN.** `emitMainBody ModeConsole` emits `let state0 = ()` when `:init` is absent ([`CodegenHs.hs:1132-1136`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)), regardless of the declared state type. **The silent case is the worse one and was mis-stated when first filed:** because generated definitions carry no type signatures, GHC generalizes a step that merely threads its state, so the program *builds and runs* with `()` where it declared `string`. Both in-tree programs with no `:init` (`examples/replay-demo`, `examples/proof_required_test`) are in exactly that shape and build clean; the loud check-passes/build-fails case needs a step body that *constrains* the state type. Verified both directions at HEAD | Require `:init` unless the step's declared state type is `unit`, which is a first-class type ([`Parser.hs:586`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Parser.hs)). Type-directed, invents no implicit initial value, and turns the silent case into a check error. **Migration surface is two files**, both `.llmll`, zero JSON-AST; measured. Rejected alternatives: inferring `state0` from the declared type needs a zero-value-per-type notion, which is implicit initialization of a program's precondition; a typed `undefined` moves a compile-time-detectable error to run time. Hazard for DRIVER-LL Phase 3's build-acceptance clause rather than a blocker, since the driver will carry real state and therefore an `:init` |
| **CMD-A** (parameterize `Command[a]`) `[CT]` | **RECORDED TARGET — not scheduled.** When it lands it retires `RESP-FACT-1`'s delivery rule (v0.17.0), because the producer then lives in the type of the response rather than in a syntactic surrogate ([`design/resp-fact-proposal.md`](design/resp-fact-proposal.md) §5.5) | The free-monad-over-signature construction (Swierstra, *Data Types à la Carte*, JFP 2008; Kiselyov and Ishii, *Freer Monads, More Extensible Effects*, Haskell '15): each operation carries its own result type and the harness is the interpreter. Closes EFFECT-RESP's command-to-response pairing residue. Cheaper than the GADT framing suggests, because the effect constructors live in the sealed `builtinEnv` and never reach the user-facing `type` grammar. **Adds no verification surface**: a bind is an IO construct so it lands in `def-shell` by the existing `LLMLL.md:451` rule, and its continuation is a lambda, already outside `Σ_auto` per the `ELambda` row at `:238`. **Declines algebraic-effect handlers** (Plotkin and Pretnar, ESOP 2009; Bauer and Pretnar, JLAMP 2015): delimited continuations would break the crash-freedom rule at `LLMLL.md:1747` and make a linear event log an inadequate replay record. That exclusion belongs in the CMD-A proposal when written, rather than being rediscovered. |
| **RET-RESOLVE** (resolve a wildcard `τ_ret` transitively in a verification-facing pass) `[CT]` | **OPEN** — design settled (Rev 2), queued behind WILD-ASSUME-2 | Closes nine measured crash shapes at the root instead of one shape at a time: `collectTopLevel` registers an unannotated return as `TVar "?"`, callers inherit it, and `sortA1` lowers it to `FQInt`. Kleene iteration over the recorded return-type map with three side conditions (wildcard-only refinement; a sandboxed pass so acceptance is unchanged by construction; an SCC-conditioned `if`-join preference). Corpus census predicts a **byte-identical** `.fq` across all 128 files, which is the gate that refuted the withdrawn HOLE-RET. Four behaviour channels, one of which (`resultLenFact` assumption injection) can turn a crash into `verified`, so it must not land before WILD-ASSUME is in place. **That channel no longer exists as of v0.14.78**: FACT-AG-LEN Stage 3 deleted `resultLenFact` from `lhsPred` and moved the fact into the effective post, so the row's stated blocking argument is partly dissolved and its prerequisite chain has to be **re-scoped before this row is scheduled**. The re-scoping is not attempted here, and neither is the corresponding edit to the design doc's four-channel table (`design/ret-resolve-proposal.md` §4, channel 4): both are language-team or compiler-engineer calls, and this note routes them rather than pre-empting them. Design: [`design/ret-resolve-proposal.md`](design/ret-resolve-proposal.md) (Rev 2) with its paired four-round review. |
| **IFACE-CONFORM** (enforce the `def-interface` conformance check `§8.8` already specifies) `[CT]` | **OPEN** — spec drift, surfaced by ADMIT-SHARED review | `LLMLL.md §8.8` specifies that the compiler looks up the interface shape in the implementing module's `ModuleEnv`, checks structural compatibility per method, and emits `interface-mismatch` — down to a documented JSON shape with a `bytes[64]` example. **No production path performs it.** `Module.checkInterfaceMismatch:382-404` implements it and is reachable only from `ModuleSpec.hs:242,249,254`; the live path, `TypeCheck.hs:1301-1306`, inserts each declared interface method type into `tcEnv` unconditionally and compares it against nothing. Measured on `llmll 0.14.74`: an interface declaring `-> bytes[64]` against an implementation exporting `bytes[32]` reports `OK (2 statements)`. **The drifting side is the compiler**; §8.8's contract is the right one. Scope to non-FFI prefixes — the same surface carries `haskell.*` / `c.*` declarations where no conformance check is possible and the declaration is necessarily the sole type source (`LLMLL.md:1130-1175`), and that split is currently stated in no spec section. Worded as a **missing enforcement and an undeclared trust boundary, not a second SAFE-ARG instance**: a false SAFE through this channel was **not** reproduced in three attempted shapes (the caller falls back or is refused at strict-core admissibility). Blast radius unmeasured — the check has never run, so a corpus sweep with it enabled is the first step. |
| **ARR-RANGE-NAME** (`bytesRootedArr` decides a ground fact on an FQ variable-name suffix) `[CT]` | **OPEN** — routed out of SAFE-ARG | `bytesRootedArr (FQVar n) = not ("$has" isSuffixOf n || "$val" isSuffixOf n)` (`FixpointEmit.hs:4159`): every array-sorted FQ variable without a map-component suffix receives ground `0 <= select(...) <= 255` facts. Default-**true**, decided on a generated name rather than a type, so it is unstable under renaming and not repairable by normalization — ADMIT-SHARED cannot reach it, since there is no declared type in the decision. A live false-fact channel in the SAFE-ARG family that no WILD-ASSUME arm guards. Fix is to thread the declared type. |
| **TRUST-AXIOM** (a builtin's assumed fact reaches the solver on no channel of the trust report) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, surfaced by FACT-AG-LEN Stage 2; classified as an **undisclosed trust dependency**, not a false SAFE. **First disclosed population landed with `RESP-FACT-1` v0.17.0** (`d5dd5a8`): the trust report's per-function `assumed_facts` rows name each control-tag fact per (tag, builtin, arm) with its premise case, the granularity [`design/resp-fact-proposal.md`](design/resp-fact-proposal.md) §12 fixes; the `bytes-set` and `bytes-zero` axioms below stay under this row | `bytes-set` emits `bytesLen(r) = bytesLen(b)` as an assumed post (`FixpointEmit.hs:3361`), and FACT-AG-LEN Stage 2 (v0.14.77) adds `bytesLen(r) = n` for `(bytes-zero)` the same way. Both are **axioms about sealed builtins**, valid only because codegen reads the same annotation to emit the value they describe, so both ride the `codegen_semantics_version` stamp (§3.5) rather than any solver discharge. **Measured: neither appears on any reporting surface.** `verify --obligation-report` on a `bytes-set`-bodied def yields exactly one obligation (the genuine `call-pre:bytes-set` index and value-range PROVE) and `verify --trust-report` reads `post: verified (liquid-fixpoint)`; the preservation lemma is named nowhere. `TrustReport.hs`, `ObligationMining.hs`, `ObligationAssembly.hs` and `WeaknessCheck.hs` contain no `bytesLen` or `bytes-set` reference at all, and `cvPostAssumption` is consumed only inside `FixpointEmit`. So a `verified` tier already rests on a codegen-faithfulness assumption the report never names, which is exactly the class `LLMLL.md` §5.4's anti-laundering discipline exists to prevent. The shipped position is a **silence**, not an over-claim: no fact is false and no verdict is wrong, but the justification surface is incomplete, and a reader cannot tell from any output that the tier has a trust-channel dependency. Fix is a `TrustReport` change: surface builtin-axiom dependencies as their own trust-channel line, enumerated from the axiom-emitting sites rather than re-derived. Scope explicitly **excludes** FACT-AG-LEN: Stage 2 inherits the silence but did not create it, and Stage 3 does not close it. Prerequisite question for the design: whether the line is per-function (which builtins' axioms this function's evidence rests on) or per-artifact, and whether it composes into `--strict-verified-core` admission or stays disclosure-only. |
| **HASH-PRE-ASYM** (the evidence-hash preimage folds the augmented post but the raw pre) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, surfaced by FACT-AG-LEN Stage 3; classified as an **uncovered staleness channel with an empty in-tree population**, not a false SAFE | `canonicalDefEvidenceHash`'s preimage is `(formTag, body, RAW pre, AUGMENTED post, decreases)`, and it is the same on the write side (`Main.hs:1531`) and the read side (`TrustReport.hs:586`). The asymmetry is invisible while a change moves the post, and FACT-AG-LEN Stage 3 depended on it working that way: every `bytes[n]`-returning function's hash moved, so `downgradeStaleVerifiedSidecar` downgraded exactly the affected population per function, which is why v0.14.78 did not have to spend `checker_soundness_version`. **Stage 1 got no such service.** `bytesLenParamPre` augments the PRE, which the preimage does not fold, so v0.14.76 moved no hash at all. A function that gained a call-site length obligation there without carrying a `bytes[n]` type of its own keeps a `verified` sidecar written by v0.14.73 through v0.14.75 (all stamped `checker_soundness_version = "1"`, so `sidecarNeedsRevalidation` passes them), and that new obligation can turn SAFE into REFUTED. **In-tree the population is empty**, measured zero on both halves under FACT-AG-LEN Rev 3's delta enumeration, so no corpus artifact is affected and no wrong verdict is known: the defect is that the staleness mechanism does not cover the pre channel, not that it has been observed failing. Fix is to fold the augmented pre into the preimage on both sides. That is a one-time whole-corpus hash move, so it wants its own release rather than riding another change, and it should land before the next release that augments a precondition. |
| **RET-BRANCH-PREF** (prefer the concrete branch at an `if` join when the other synthesizes the `?` wildcard) `[CT]` | **Stage 1 SHIPPED v0.14.72; Stage 2 recorded, not proposed** | `inferExpr (EIf ...)` returned `thenType` unconditionally, so a wildcard then-branch silently won over a concrete else-branch. That left the last member of the FQ-RESULT-SORT-1 trigger set crashing: an unannotated contracted **recursive** def whose recursive call sits in `then` position synthesized `TVar "?"`, which `sortA1` lowers to `FQInt`. Stage 1 (`preferConcreteOnSelfCall`) prefers the concrete branch **only when the other is a self-recursive call**, where the wildcard is the enclosing function's own return type and the concrete branch determines it, making the preference a least-fixpoint step rather than a guess. Conservative: only a bare `EApp` headed by the enclosing function matches, so a self-call under `let` or wrapped in an operator declines and pre-existing behaviour stands. **Stage 2** (dropping the side condition, which would also fire for a *foreign* unannotated callee) is recorded and gated on a corpus `.fq` byte-diff plus a typecheck-acceptance diff; a wildcard return is idiomatic, 104 unannotated def heads corpus-wide and 72 alongside another unannotated callee, so the general form must be measured before it ships. Design: [`design/ret-branch-pref-proposal.md`](design/ret-branch-pref-proposal.md) (Rev 0) with its paired professor review. Tests RBP-1..3; corpus `.fq` byte-identical across 128 files. |
| **CDP default-on** (promote `--cdp` / `--weakness-check` / `--spec-coverage` into the default serious-verify path) | **DEFERRED (nice-to-have) — not pursued** | Available opt-in via `--strict-verify`. All preconditions closed by v0.14.4; wall-clock characterized (`experiments/cdp-perf-0/`: `--cdp` roughly 2–3× verify time on modules with candidates). Flipping the default was considered and deferred on cost/UX grounds (unprompted diagnostic surface on every `verify`; the `--strict-verify` opt-in is preferred). Revisit if a concrete need emerges. |
| **OBLIG-1-FOLLOWON** (populate reserved obligation-report fields) | **v1 + v2a + v2b SHIPPED — residual one province deferred** | The `assumptions` field carries: (v1, v0.14.29) refinement predicates of in-scope refinement-typed **params**, α-renamed to the binder; (v2a, v0.14.31) **let-definitional equalities `(= y e)`** for in-scope let-bindings with a QF-LIA RHS (`ScopeBinding.sbDef` + `tcDefs`); (v2b, v0.14.32) **match-scrutinee case hypotheses `(= s (Ctor x))`** (nullary: `(= s Ctor)`), accumulated per-path across nested/sequential matches, shadow-guarded (`SketchHole.shHyps` + `matchHypothesis`/`withHyp`). **Deferred province**: `def-invariant` axioms (needs provenance tagging → schema bump, an unverified invariant being a TCB assumption). All value paths (`expected_return_type`, `available_functions`, `assumptions`) populated. |
| **INT-3** (`MachineInt` QF-BV alias) | **P3 — open** | LT design when scheduled; promote to P1 if INT-PRE shows TOTP regression > 5× (cleared at 1.015×, so dormant) |
| **BUILTIN-BODY-1** (a declared builtin with no reachable definition) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.16.0, with work still owed in this row.** `builtinEnv` declared `sha1` ([`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) and the codegen preamble defined `sha1_hash`, so the name reached the generic fallthrough in `emitApp` and emitted a call to an identifier nothing defines. **A program calling it passed `llmll check`, verified SAFE, wrote a sidecar, and died at GHC** with `Variable not in scope: sha1`. **The check for this class already existed and was scoped to a prefix**: the [`Spec.hs`](../compiler/test/Spec.hs) fold written after `WASI-RT` filtered on `wasi.` and covered **16 of 101** names. **MEASURED partition at `f86585e`, no residue**: 16 operators route to `emitOp` inside `emitApp`, 11 have hand-written `emitApp` cases, 9 resolve elsewhere (`abs`/`min`/`max`/`not` against GHC's Prelude, `RNone` through `RList` as `data Response` constructors), 66 reach the fallthrough. **`sha1` was the ONLY one of the 101 with no reachable definition.** The fold now classifies by RUNNING `emitApp` and reading the emitted text, so operators and hand-written cases classify themselves rather than living in a list someone must remember to extend. The test landed BEFORE the fix and was seen to fail, once, naming `sha1`; a guard that has never fired is not known to work | **ONE RESIDUE LEFT. The second closed 2026-08-16, and closing it sharpened the first.** (2) **CLOSED.** [`scripts/build-smoke/smoke.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/smoke.llmll) calls `sha1` through `smoke-sha1`, and `sha1_hash` joins the emitted-preamble name lists in [`scripts/build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) **and** its LLMLL port in the same change, which is what the port's own comment asks for after two names drifted out of that list. **No new stage and no new verdict line**, so [`scripts/build_smoke_cover.py`](../scripts/build_smoke_cover.py)'s stage set is unchanged and the port needed no counterpart stage; that shape was chosen for it. The BUILD is the assertion rather than any grep, measured by rewriting the emitted `smoke_sha1` call site back to the pre-fix lowering and rebuilding, which gives `Variable not in scope: sha1 :: [Word8] -> [Word8]`. (1) **OPEN, and sharper than when it was filed**: the fix EXEMPTED `sha1` from the guard that found it, because `emitApp` now answers `(sha1_hash …)` and the fold classifies anything not headed by the mangled builtin name as a hand-written case. That is the 71 to 70 class-size move, and it means the guard cannot catch a typo in the equation that repaired the defect. **MEASURED over `scripts/build-smoke/*.llmll`, so the gap is a number**: of the twelve hand-written equations the corpus called three (`pair`, `list-length`, `string-length`) and now calls four; **eight are called by no build fixture** (`first`, `second`, `bytes-length`, `bytes-get`, `bytes-set`, `list-nth`, `string-slice`, `string-char-at`), and five of those wrap an argument in `fromIntegral` at the `LT-INT` seam where a wrong wrapper is a GHC type error. Two routes close it: extend the fixture to call the eight, which is cheap and covers only what it calls, or close it at the check by extracting the emitted identifier, which needs three more allowlist entries and a special case for `pair`, which emits tuple syntax with no identifier |
| **TOTP-CHECK-1** (a shipped example does not typecheck, and no wired gate says so) `[CT]` | **OPEN**, found 2026-08-16 while measuring `BUILTIN-BODY-1`. `llmll check` on [`examples/totp_rfc6238/totp_filled.ast.json`](../examples/totp_rfc6238/totp_filled.ast.json) FAILS: a type mismatch on `dynamic-truncate`, expected `bytes[20]`, got an unannotated return type, emitted from [`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs). This is upstream of codegen and unrelated to the `sha1` lowering. **A SECOND cause was measured and it is the one that matters**: the example's gate [`scripts/benchmark-totp.sh`](../scripts/benchmark-totp.sh) runs only `llmll check` and `llmll verify`, never `llmll build`, **and no workflow and no test references it at all**. So nothing in CI has decided anything about this example. That is finding 6's shape in [`design/tool-ll-RESTART.md`](design/tool-ll-RESTART.md): a gate that is not wired in decides nothing. **The example is cited by `LLMLL.md` §5.3 as the demonstration of mixed trust tiers**, so it is not inert documentation | Decide whether the example is repaired or retired, and separately decide whether its gate is wired into a workflow or recorded as grading nothing. **Do not fold the two**: one is an example defect and one is a gate that was never connected, the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` distinction |
| **SHA1-DOMAIN-1** (the declared `sha1` domain is narrower than the standard it names) `[CT][SPEC]` | **OPEN**, filed 2026-08-16 by the `BUILTIN-BODY-1` work and deliberately excluded from it. `sha1 : bytes[20] → bytes[20]` in `builtinEnv`, so it hashes exactly twenty bytes. FIPS 180-4 admits any length, and the preamble's own `sha1_hash :: [Word8] -> [Word8]` also takes any length and returns exactly 20 bytes via `take 20`. **So the declared domain is a narrowing the runtime does not need.** [`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs) records that a `bytes[n]` signature needs machinery this row does not have, in the comment that already anticipates this shape for a pure `sha256` | Widen to `bytes[n] → bytes[20]` when the real crypto backend that `LLMLL.md` §13.11 requires for distribution builds replaces the stub. **Not urgent while the backend is a stub**: the narrow domain and the stub are two independent defects, and fixing the type without the backend buys nothing |
| **CRYPTO-2** (`LLMLL.md` §13.11 states the trust report emits a channel that does not exist) `[SPEC][CT]` | **SHIPPED 2026-08-19, and the citation sweep found four more references than this row named.** Routed 2026-08-18; adjudicated 2026-08-03 as `R-10` in [`design/driver-ll-open-work.md`](design/driver-ll-open-work.md) and as row `CRYPTO-2` of [`design/critique-2026-05-23-triage.md`](design/critique-2026-05-23-triage.md), and never given a row until now, which is why the retraction stood unexecuted. **The claim is in the spec, in an `[!IMPORTANT]` block, in the present tense:** `LLMLL.md:2727` reads "The trust report annotates dependencies on these builtins as `asserted-with-stub-backend` until backend replacement is verified". **It does not.** `rg 'stub-backend' compiler/` returns zero hits and `TrustReport.hs:1630` emits `"asserted"` beside `tested`, `tested_joint`, `no_contract` and `drifts`; the commit credited with shipping the channel (`7ccd925`) is `docs(spec, schema)`, documentation-only. **What holds is unchanged and sufficient:** the `asserted` cap on every function whose trust closure reaches `sha1` / `hmac-sha1` is real and emitted. **Two archived proposals build on the non-existent channel as a real admissibility criterion**, so both encode a gate that can never fire: `core-shell-inversion-proposal.md:162` and `refinement-metatheory-of-record-proposal.md:143`, both under `docs/archive/shipped-design-specs/`. **A tier is the wrong shape and none should be added:** a tier is a position in an ordered lattice and must be monotone under composition, whereas "reaches an axiom known to be false" says the assumption set is inconsistent, and `LLMLL.md §4.4.1`'s diamond has no bottom element meaning *unsound*. **WHAT LANDED.** The `LLMLL.md` §13.11 `[!IMPORTANT]` block now states that the `asserted` cap is the whole mechanism, that no separate channel, field or tier exists, and that the report therefore cannot distinguish an opaque algorithm from a known-incorrect implementation. That last consequence was not in the retraction as adjudicated; it is the user-facing fact a consumer needs and it was added rather than left implied. Both archived proposals carry a dated `CRYPTO-2` correction note, placed beside the citation and rewriting nothing, and in both the cited criterion survives unchanged because it already turned on the `asserted` tier. **The row named two citations and a repo-wide `rg 'stub-backend'` returns twelve hits across seven files.** Four more sit in `docs/design/` bodies (`critique-2026-05-23-triage.md` rows `CRYPTO-1` and `CRYPTO-2` plus its §182-185 measurement, `driver-ll-open-work.md` `R-10`, `critique-2026-07-19-triage.md`), which are language-team's to close, and one is the `CHANGELOG.md` v0.16.2 entry, which is a past-tense record of what that release filed and is exempt from repointing per [`docs/UPDATE-PROTOCOL.md`](UPDATE-PROTOCOL.md). **No version was cut and no CHANGELOG line was written**, the change being prose in three files with no distribution effect; the next release owes the entry. `REPORT-GATE-1` (`R-12`) stays unrouted and is still the class gap that let this survive: DRIFT-CT-2 guards claims about what the compiler *rejects*, and "the trust report emits field X" has no expressible fixture in that harness. | Retract the sentence at `LLMLL.md:2727`: state that the `asserted` cap is the mechanism and that no separate channel or field exists. Leave the naming decision alone — retaining the RFC 2104 / FIPS 180-4 contract names is settled and unaffected. Then annotate the two archived proposals so neither reads as a live gate; they are archived, so a dated note is enough and a rewrite is not wanted. **The principled successor is `assumed_axioms`, an axiom-dependency report over the justification closure (Lean `#print axioms`, Coq `Print Assumptions`; cf. CompCert's TCB enumeration, Leroy CACM 2009, and foundational PCC, Appel LICS 2001), and it is deliberately NOT SCHEDULED by this row**; it would be an additive report field beside the tier rather than a trust-model change, and `computeEffectSummary` (`ObligationAssembly.hs:467-470`) already has the fixpoint it would reuse. **Why no gate caught this:** DRIFT-CT-2 guards *restriction* claims by running `.llmll` fixtures through `llmll check`, and "the trust report emits field X" has no expressible fixture in that harness. That class gap is `REPORT-GATE-1` (`R-12` in the open-work record), still unrouted and deliberately not folded into this row. |

> **Recently retired (shipped):** REF-META, Bundle B0, NIW Phase 1 (v0.12.0); COMP-4 / PAIR-RET lines (v0.13.x); PROOF-ARTIFACT, R5 differential-implementation-pressure (v0.14.x); **REC-BODY-VC — the whole line: (b0) hash integrity v0.14.22, (a) partiality marker v0.14.23, (c) REC-DESCENT surface v0.14.24 + discharge/admission v0.14.25 + lexicographic k>1 v0.14.27 (design [`rec-body-vc-proposal.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/rec-body-vc-proposal.md); R7 single-slice descent promoted, TERM-1 disclaimer retired for discharged recursion).** **MATCH-WIDEN-2 (n-arm sums + sequential matches, v0.14.26)** — the body-faithful match fragment widened from two arms to any arity + sequential composition ([`match-widen-stretch-plan.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/match-widen-stretch-plan.md) §S4). **REFINE-REUSE (v0.14.29)** — `refine`'s advisory reuse retrieval (`reuse_suggestions` + non-blocking `W-REUSE`; design [`refine-reuse-gate-proposal.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/refine-reuse-gate-proposal.md)); Stage 4 of Cascading Refinement. **CLASSIFY-EOP (v0.14.30)** — `isQfLia`/`classifyContractFragment` recognize the `EOp` operator form (classification/reporting fix, not soundness). Provenance in [Shipped Releases](#shipped-releases) + `CHANGELOG.md`. Still future: Bundle B1 (awaits a B1-native experiment), NIW Phase 2, B2/B3; R5's forced-diversity follow-on is experiment-lead-owned.

## Future — Module System Codegen (unversioned)

**Theme:** Complete the module system's codegen-level guarantees.

> The module system is compile-time correct; codegen-level enforcement (export hiding, qualified imports, interface checks) is absent.

| # | ID | Description | Prerequisite | Status |
|---|-----|-------------|-------------|--------|
| 1 | **MOD-2** | **[CT]** Per-module Haskell file emission: instead of concatenating all module statements into a single `Lib.hs`, emit one `.hs` file per module with Haskell `module` export lists. Enables true codegen-level export hiding and qualified access. | None | ☐ |
| 2 | **MOD-3** | **[CT]** Qualified access at codegen: with per-module `.hs` files, translate `module.function` to Haskell qualified imports. Makes §8.5.1 "Qualified Access" operational. | MOD-2 | ☐ |
| 3 | **MOD-4** | **[CT]** `loadFromFile` strict typecheck migration: switch the DFS module loader from permissive `typeCheck` to strict `typeCheckStrictWithCache`. Requires all examples and library modules to have correct `(open ...)` declarations. | MOD-1 | ☐ |
| 4 | **MOD-5** | **[CT]** `checkInterfaceMismatch` wiring: add `interfaceName` field to `Import` AST in `Syntax.hs`, parse `(interface ...)` clause in `Parser.hs`, expand `meAliasMap` types before comparison in `Module.hs`. **Now also motivated by XMOD-AG (v0.14.17):** cross-module assume-guarantee inherits nominal-by-name ADT identity (same-named imported ADTs compared by constructor names, not structure); MOD-5's structural check would close that limitation. | MOD-2 | ☐ |

**Trigger:** a production use case requiring true namespace isolation.

## Future — WASM Sandboxing (unversioned)

**Theme:** Replace Docker with WASM-WASI as the primary sandbox.

> A confirmed future direction, not pinned to a version. Docker + CAP-1 already provide two enforcement layers (compile-time capability gating + container isolation); WASM adds a hardware-enforced third layer, prioritized when real users run untrusted agent code in production. Feasibility confirmed (v0.3.2 PoC; `effectful` compatibility spike GO). Design: [`wasm-poc-report.md`](archive/wasm-investigations/wasm-poc-report.md).

### WASM Build Target (~7 days)

| Phase | Work | Effort | Status |
|-------|------|--------|--------|
| Phase 0 | Install `ghc-wasm-meta` + `wasmtime`, manual compile of hangman | 1 day | ☐ |
| Phase 1 | `--target wasm` flag, generate `.cabal` file, invoke `wasm32-wasi-cabal` | 2–3 days | ☐ |
| Phase 2 | Strip check blocks for WASM, WASI capability import mapping | 2 days | ☐ |
| Phase 3 | CI integration, setup script, docs | 1 day | ☐ |

**Acceptance criteria:**

- `llmll build --target wasm examples/hangman_sexp/hangman.llmll` produces a `.wasm` binary
- `wasmtime hangman.wasm` runs the game correctly
- WASI capability imports align with LLMLL capability declarations
- Typed effect rows (`effectful`) integrate with WASI enforcement

**Risk:** `ghc-wasm-meta` toolchain maintenance is low-bus-factor. If it falls behind GHC releases, this work slips without affecting anything else.

**Trigger — when to schedule this:**

- Real users running untrusted agent code outside development environments
- Docker proving insufficient as a sandbox (capability granularity, startup latency, distribution)

---

## Future — Data Scope Extension (unversioned)

> Widening the verification surface (`Σ_auto`) from *integers + non-recursive ADTs + length-measures*
> to **complex data structures** (lists, arrays, maps, recursive types). Didactic reference and full
> rationale: [`docs/design/data-scope-extension.md`](design/data-scope-extension.md). **Lever A
> shipped (v0.14.33–51)** — arrays (`bytes[n]`) and maps (`{int,string}` keys × `{int,bool,string}`
> values) now verify (index-in-bounds, get-after-put, key-presence), and the flagship-example goal
> is met (`examples/bytes-bounds/`, `examples/token-revocation-emergent/`). The remaining track
> answers "can LLMLL verify **lists** / **recursive** data?" — today *no, deliberately*: the
> boundary is **decidability** (the guarantee that "SAFE" is a decidable predicate on a fixed VC).
> Levers **B** (lists) and **C** (recursive) below are that future work.

| Lever | Item | Theory / decidability | Unlocks | Status / dependency |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Theory of arrays** for `bytes[n]` / `map[k,v]` | QF theory of arrays (`select`/`store` + extensionality) — **decidable**, polite-combines with QF-LIA; **stays in `Σ_auto`** | array index-in-bounds (real memory-safety, not a length proxy); map get-after-put / key-presence | **A0–A4 COMPLETE — the whole lever: A0–A3 + A2.1 + A2.2-bool (v0.14.33–43), A2.2-string values (v0.14.46), the residue lift (v0.14.47: string-map returns + param-string puts), the STRLIT body-channel flip (v0.14.48), and the A4 flagship [`examples/token-revocation-emergent/`](../examples/token-revocation-emergent/) — an RFC 7009/7662 token service, contracts `:source`-derived, bodies invented by blind agents via cascading refine (8 fns / 5 modules body-faithful, 2 agent-invented; 9 frozen gate cases (5 refute cruxes + 4 good twins) in the refute-crux gate)** — design [`data-scope-lever-a-arrays-proposal.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/data-scope-lever-a-arrays-proposal.md) (Rev 1.1) / [`-review.md`](archive/professor-reviews/data-scope-lever-a-arrays-review.md) / [`-feasibility.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/data-scope-lever-a-feasibility.md). A3 = classification derives from the emitter's own §6.1 guards (`contractArrGuardsBlock`); checkout briefs list the ops as type-gated `builtin` vocabulary; `examples/bytes-bounds/` = the regression witness. A2.1 (v0.14.41) lifted the realistic map deferrals: read-modify-write bodies (`mapRetChain`), cross-module map assume-guarantee (component-aware substitution — the previously-wrongly-passing bad twin now refutes at the call site), and map-returning callee results; new `FQMapArr` sort renders identically to `(Map_t int int)` but distinguishes map from bytes returns. **Open residue:** A3.x only — the DATA class is complete: `{int,string}` keys × `{int,bool,string}` values, `map-empty` construction, RMW, returns, tail-call composition, cross-module A-G (v0.14.50/51 closed the last two items) (reuse-retrieval driver abstains on array contracts; CDP array-sorted candidate bases; classifier callee-leg imprecision). Language-team dispositions SETTLED (2026-07-12): v1 `bytes-zero` context rule blessed; contract-position reads = total selects, sound both directions, status-quo + scoped lint ([`contract-position-reads-disposition.md`](design/contract-position-reads-disposition.md) → CONTRACT-READ-LINT row). |
| **B** | **Dependent lengths** (`list[t]{len=n}`) + widened measure catalog | QF-LIA + EUF (+ arrays) — **decidable** | safe list indexing (bridge from "count a list" to "index a list") | **Proposed**; depends on Lever A; **overlaps R1** (dependent types, `Vect n a`, below) |
| **C** | **Inductive datatypes + induction** (recursive ADTs, measure unfolding, PLE / refinement reflection) | inductive datatype theory — **undecidable in general**; **leaves `Σ_auto`** → new weaker tier *or* route to the Lean tier | list / tree / stack structural invariants (sortedness, balance, acyclicity, use-after-free) | **Research frontier**; gated on **LEAN-GA** (`verified-lean` discharge); the R7 strict-descent gate cleared (shipped v0.14.25/27) |

> **Evaluation-integrity rule** (standing, for every example built on this track): the `checkout`
> brief is the *sole* information channel to a hole-filling agent — we neither force a failure nor
> leak hints beyond the returned contract (pre/post goal, expected return type, in-scope bindings,
> callable contracts). See [`data-scope-extension.md`](design/data-scope-extension.md) Post 8. This
> is *why* the extension matters: a hint-free data-structure hole is only fillable-and-checkable if
> the contract alone is expressive enough — which requires a richer data theory — delivered by Lever A (shipped v0.14.33–51).

---

## Future — Cascading Refinement (unversioned)

> Agent-driven **recursive hole decomposition** — the "third track" of the north star. Today a scaffold's
> decomposition is authored up front (holed out of a full solution); cascading refinement makes it
> *emergent* — a `refine` op installs a hole's body **and spawns new contracted sub-holes** (new functions
> with their own contracts), recursively, growing a refinement tree. Design (professor-folded):
> [`docs/design/cascading-refinement-proposal.md`](design/cascading-refinement-proposal.md) (Rev 8).
> **Layers 1–4 shipped — the line is complete** (v0.14.13 `refine` op + CDP vacuity gate, exercised
> end-to-end by `examples/secure-channel-emergent/`; v0.14.29 reuse retrieval; v0.14.52 feasibility
> (no-miracle) gate; v0.14.53 decomposition-trust meet). **Open:** none — the design-accepted
> joint-vacuity limitation is delegated to R5 (Layer 3(c)). The control- and data-side
> fragment-wideners ship the *bodies*; this ships the *decomposition* — the project's own stated
> differentiator (`docs/design/strategic-positioning.md:22`).

| Layer | What | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — substrate** | per-step verification = existing assume-guarantee (`cvPreObligation`/`cvPostAssumption`); no new fragment | reuse |
| **2 — protocol** | a `refine` op (dual of `patch`: install body + spawn sub-holes atomically, scope-relaxation safety predicate — fresh, body-referenced, hole-bodied spawns only), reusing the `applyPatch` lifecycle (compare-and-swap resync + assume-guarantee re-verify) | **✅ SHIPPED v0.14.13** (`examples/refine-demo/`) |
| **3 — contract quality** | spawn-time **CDP** vacuity gate (rejects a trivially-satisfiable sub-contract) + **feasibility** (no-miracle) gate + decomposition-trust **meet** (floored on contract-only cycle members) + **`CLAUSE-INDEP-1`**, a clause-side independence check (a `post` clause entailed by its siblings) | vacuity gate **✅ SHIPPED v0.14.13**; feasibility (no-miracle) gate **✅ SHIPPED v0.14.52** (`LLMLL.Feasibility`); decomposition-trust meet **✅ SHIPPED v0.14.53** (`unvouched_cdp_meet`), extending **CDP** (the **CDP default-on** row under [Active Items](#active-items)); **`CLAUSE-INDEP-1` OPEN**, filed by DRIVER-LL Phase 4. A `post` clause entailed by its siblings contributes no eliminative power while occupying a §15.1 tier of the driver's conformance report. Confirmed instance: `[S5-PRESENCE]` follows propositionally from `[S5-SKIP]` (`skip.llmll:19-27`), so its refute-crux set is a **subset** of `[S5-SKIP]`'s and no mutation refutes it alone. The check is the clause-side dual `SAT(⋀_{j≠i} Pⱼ ∧ ¬Pᵢ)`, no quantifier alternation, and is the cheapest of the three. Per the project's tracked-concept discipline it **extends CDP on the axis CDP's own Ω-relativity comment names as its limit** (`CDP.hs:142-149` distinguishes genuine vacuity from tightness-for-Ω, and `LLMLL.Feasibility` is the Ω-independent one); it does not approximate or sidestep CDP. **The in-scope move needs no compiler change:** record the entailment in `[S5-PRESENCE]`'s `:source` text so the clause keeps its traceability to a distinct driver-spec sentence while a reader sees that it is derived, and let the tiering report count three obligations of which one is marked derived. Design: [`design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md`](design/driver-ll-phase4-proposal.md) §14 |
| **4 — reuse retrieval (REFINE-REUSE)** | spawn-time **reuse retrieval** (*not a gate — never rejects*): surface existing in-scope defs that *subsume* a spawned sub-contract (contract-implication `preₛ⇒pre_D ∧ post_D⇒postₛ`, α-normalized, not name/syntax). Advisory `reuse_suggestions` brief channel + non-blocking `W-REUSE` warning on an exact-equivalent; reuses **COMP-4(b)** subtyping-Horn (`:70`) | **✅ SHIPPED v0.14.29** (`LLMLL.RefineReuse`; a NEW whole-contract subsumption driver — two standalone Horn constraints, not the call-site COMP-4(b) payload driver); design [`refine-reuse-gate-proposal.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/refine-reuse-gate-proposal.md) |

> **Acyclicity policy — DECIDED (Option 3):** `refine` admits a cycle-creating spawn, detects the cycle,
> and degrades its members to contract-only (trust meet floored so nothing is laundered) — the
> `letrec` partial-correctness treatment (`LLMLL.md §5.3.5`). The R7 strict-descent total-correctness
> upgrade shipped (v0.14.25/27): a discharging `(decreases …)` measure on the cycle members upgrades the
> cycle to total. One open design question (per-contract vs composed gating). **Depends-on:**
> MATCH-WIDEN (shipped v0.14.12 — gives the cascade non-trivial verified leaves), R2 (self-hosted
> orchestrator), R8 (incremental re-verify). Standing rule: the `checkout` brief is the sole information
> channel to a hole-filling agent (no forced failures, no hints).

Distinct from single-agent repair-loop and the `OBLIG-*` family. | [`docs/archive/research-track.md §5`](archive/research-track.md) |
| **R7** | **Call-Site Strict Descent** (`measure(args') < measure(args)` constraint at each recursive call site; independent of BODY-VC per consultant correction 2026-04-28) | ~~Independent design spec for descent constraint generation~~ → **SHIPPED via REC-DESCENT** (v0.14.25 single-measure discharge + strict-core admission; v0.14.27 lexicographic k>1; design [`rec-body-vc-proposal.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/rec-body-vc-proposal.md)) | TERM-1's partiality disclaimer is retired for descent-discharged recursion; an undischarged (measureless / mixed-arity / non-QF-LIA-measure) cycle stays partial and keeps the `termination_unverified` marker. | [`docs/archive/research-track.md §7`](archive/research-track.md) |
| **R8** | **Incremental Patch Re-Verification** (dependency-slice re-verify for `llmll patch` instead of whole-module) | Design spec proving the slice is sound — that node-VC + fill-induced callee slice + staleness-recheck captures every obligation the whole-module re-verify emits — plus a demonstrated repair-loop latency pain point | Surfaced by language-team, 2026-07. Current behavior: `PatchApply.applyPatch` re-parses + typechecks + Fixpoint-emits the whole merged module on every patch (`PatchApply.hs:291-303`). The checkout brief already snapshots most of a sound slice's inputs — enclosing pre, path condition, callee `fePre`/`fePost` (`Checkout.hs:186-197`). Wall-clock basis: `experiments/cdp-perf-0/`. Distinct from CDP default-on. **Rev 0 proposal written** ([`incremental-reverify-r8-proposal.md`](design/incremental-reverify-r8-proposal.md), 2026-07-20): both soundness premises code-verified — `patch` fills only body-position holes (contracts never change, `HoleAnalysis.hs:622`) over assume-guarantee-modular VCs (`FixpointEmit.hs:32`) — so the sound-and-complete slice is the **singleton `{F}`** (the patched function), no callee/staleness slice needed. Implementation = an `emitBodyVCTargets` emit option; fail-safe to whole-module. **SHIPPED v0.14.61**: `EmitOptions.emitBodyVCTargets`, `PatchApply.patchTargetFns` resolves the patched function from the op paths, `reVerify` slices to `{F}` (fail-safe to whole-module on any unresolvable/`/statements/-`/multi-statement patch). Verdict-preservation proven: all existing patch tests pass through the sliced path + 4 slice unit tests. Latency benchmark = deferred residue (correctness proven; the O(module)→O(1) body-VC win is structural). | New → **SHIPPED v0.14.61** |

# Policy & Scope

## What's NOT on this Roadmap (and why)

| Item | Reason |
|------|--------|
| Rust codegen backend | Dropped in v0.1.2; Haskell is the permanent target |
| Python FFI tier | Breaks WASM compatibility; dynamically typed |
| Full Lean 4 proof agent built from scratch | Lean discharge goes through Leanstral (LEAN-GA — `--leanstral` demo slice shipped v0.14.8, production rebuild parking-lotted), not a hand-built prover |
| UI/web frontend | LLMLL's target domains are backend, not UI |
| IDE plugins (VS Code, etc.) | Premature — stabilize the CLI/HTTP interface first |
| Indexed/dependent types | Research track (R1) — deferred by professor consensus (2026-05-01), not a v0.x target |
| `ELambda` higher-order body VCs | Out of `Σ_auto` by design — higher-order refinements (defunctionalization / abstract-refinement machinery) are a research axis with no target-domain demand; lambdas lower to runtime contracts. Revisit only with a concrete verified-HOF use case. |
| `EDo` / effectful bodies in `Σ_auto` | Effects verify on the capability / effect-row axis (Bundle B; B0 shipped v0.12.0, B2/B3 future), not via SMT posts — `EDo` bodies keep runtime contracts + effect summaries by design. |
| Uncontracted-callee body VCs (bounded inlining / contract inference) | The agent workflow (scaffold/checkout) mandates contracts, so uncontracted callees barely occur in the target flow; contract inference was deliberately kept local (REF-META-5, professor consensus). Bounded inlining is possible but low-value; the contract-only fallback is the recorded decision. |

> The former freeze-era exclusions (new builtins, new syntax constructs, broader FFI, more WASI surface, compiler-side orchestration, typeclass law machinery) are no longer blanket-excluded. A proposed addition goes through the normal design → review → ship pipeline — language-team design, professor critique, compiler-engineer implementation — and lands with a written soundness argument as part of its design record. The original v0.8.1a–v0.10 feature freeze was lifted at v0.11; historical rationale: [`core-shell-inversion-direction.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/core-shell-inversion-direction.md).

# History

## Shipped Releases

> Compact summary, newest-first. One line per version: headline — ship date — test count — pointer to its `CHANGELOG.md §vX.Y.Z` section for detail. Canonical release narrative is [`../CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md). This `#shipped-releases` anchor is stable and is the entry point other docs link to.

| Version | Headline | Date | Tests | Detail |
|---------|----------|------|-------|--------|
| **v0.17.0** | **A `Command` result carries a proved property to its caller, keyed on the control tag.** `RESP-FACT-1` ships: a def whose `pre` has `(= p T)` on an all-nullary tag declared in the entry module, with a `Response` parameter, receives the declared fact for the builtin `T` is bound to on the `Response` arm binder (`wasi.http.response`/`RCode` `{v : int | v >= 100}`, the one fact declared). Three checker rules, every failure a hard error: the issuing rule over every returned `(σ, Command)` pair, the delivery rule from `:step` by bare variables, and the export condition (`(export)`). The trust report discloses each assumed fact with its premise case (`assumed_facts`). Two emitter fixes: `(Ran)` lowers like `Ran`; a closed-true call-pre folds, a closed-false one is kept. `checker_soundness_version` "2". No new builtin, no schema change, no CLI change | 2026-09-04 | 1831 hspec, 180 Python (10 skipped) | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.17.0`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.16.0** | **A directory can be removed, and the name that claimed to remove one stops lying.** `FS-RMDIR-1` ships `wasi.fs.rmdir : string -> Command`, **empty-only**, idempotent on a missing path, `RErr` for a non-empty directory and for a path naming a file. Recursive removal is deliberately absent: there is no runtime capability check (`CAP-1-REAL`), so a recursive delete would run under a root that is declared and unenforced, and composing recursion in LLMLL is unsound over symlinks today (`LIST-KIND-1`). **No new `Response` arm, no new effect label, no new namespace, no schema delta**; Σ_eff stays seven-wide and the `wasi.*` surface moves fifteen to sixteen. **The row found a live defect on the way in and the same commit fixes it**: `wasi.fs.delete` published `RNone` for a directory and removed nothing, because `doesFileExist` answers `False` on a directory and the idempotence guard took its false branch. Measured before the change; zero in-tree call sites pass a directory, so nothing in the repository changes behaviour. **Port 006 now grades stage 5** instead of reporting `NOT GRADED`, and the NOT-GRADED path stays with its trigger changed to "the clear did not succeed". Both cells were run and differ by one entry. All five new tests were checked by mutation | 2026-08-15 | 1713 hspec, 179 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.16.0`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.15.0** | **A program can read its environment, and the flag that would log it is refused.** `ENV-READ-1` ships the READ direction only; `PROC-ENV-1` (setting a variable for a child) stays open, the two being one namespace and two rows. `wasi.env.get : string -> Command` delivers `RText` for a set variable and `RErr` for an unset one, so a variable that is set and **empty** stays distinguishable from one that is unset, measured on a built program. **The shipped signature is not the one the row asked for**: it proposed `Result[string,string]` and the settled design puts the answer on the response channel like every other `wasi.*` name. Three fail-closed type-level side conditions, each with a firing witness and a sibling that does not fire: `:deterministic true` on a `wasi.env` import is a type error, and a **literal** name that is empty or contains `=` is a type error, while a computed one is not and that limit is pinned rather than hidden. Σ_eff widens six to seven with `env.read`, the first catalog growth since it closed, because a summary reporting `nondet` cannot separate a clock read from a credential read. **Filed `EVENT-CAPTURE-1`, and it is why the refusal is worded as a forward guard**: `LLMLL.md` §10a specifies an event-log capture that the compiler does not implement, measured four ways and confirmed by building a program whose log records `"captures":[]` on every event. Six commits of incomplete TOOL-RFC-006 port work ride along | 2026-08-14 | 1707 Haskell, 179 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.15.0`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.99** | **A reference is deleted, and the only instrument that graded its replacement goes with it.** No compiler change. `TOOL-RFC-005` retires, the campaign's first: `scripts/doc_path_lint.py` and its 22-cell differential cover are deleted one release after the port landed, and DRIFT-DOC-4 is now [`pathlint.llmll`](../tools/doc-path-lint/pathlint.llmll) alone. The cover pinned `REF` at the reference and could not outlive it, so **the port is now ungraded and a live green run is not evidence it is correct**. Deleting the subject broke 13 prose citations in 6 files, measured BEFORE the deletion by moving the reference aside: two repointed, five carried as `ALLOW` entries in the port. Last differential measurement, taken at the moment of deletion: both implementations report 977 citations in 173 living files, all resolve. `version-gate.yml` and `docs/UPDATE-PROTOCOL.md` both advertised a `test_clean_on_live_repo` merge block that was deleted a day earlier | 2026-08-12 | 1688 Haskell, 179 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.99`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.98** | **A builtin stops sharing the caller's stdin, and the last search idiom leaves the tree.** `PROC-STDIN-1` and `PROC-STDIN-SHARE-1` closed together: [`CodegenHs.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs) left `std_in` unset, so `createProcess` inherited and a child could read a torn fragment of a `:mode console` parent's own step input above the 8 KiB handle buffer. `wasi.proc.run` gains a seventh parameter, a stdin path, placed after the `int` so that transposing it with `stdout-path` is a type error. 17 call sites in 9 files migrate in one commit. **The campaign's first gap closed by the compiler rather than worked around by a port**, and it was latent in all five shipped ports | 2026-08-12 | 1688 Haskell, 179 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.98`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.97** | **The fifth gate ports, and the row blocking the sixth closes on a measurement nobody had run.** `TOOL-RFC-005` ports DRIFT-DOC-4 to [`pathlint.llmll`](../tools/doc-path-lint/pathlint.llmll) over a proved core, both implementations adjacent in `spec-roundtrip`, which is what `tool_state: oracle` means. `FS-WALK-1` closed COSMETIC: `build_smoke.sh` had twelve walk sites, not the nine the row claimed, and every one was the same query, so asking stack for the install root removed all twelve and unblocked port 006 | 2026-08-10 | 1683 Haskell, Python count not recorded at release | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.97`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.96** | **A builtin that typechecked, verified, and did not build.** `REGEX-LOWER-1` closed. `isOperator` ([`Parser.hs:940-947`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Parser.hs)) named two things `emitOp` cannot lower, and its fallback intercalates the operator name LITERALLY between its arguments, so `(regex-match p s)` emitted `(p regex-match s)`, GHC lexed the hyphen as subtraction, and the preamble's own `regex_match` binding was referenced by nothing for the builtin's whole life. **Fixed by deleting the two names rather than adding `emitOp` cases**, the narrower shape, which leaves no second list to drift from the first: `regex-match` now reaches `emitApp`'s default and is mangled by `toHsIdent` into the preamble binding's exact name, and **no codegen was written**. **The census the row demanded corrected the row**, for the second time and in the same way: it grouped `regex-match` with `is-valid?`, and executed they are different classes discriminated by `builtinEnv` membership, so the population reaching the fallback was exactly ONE name. `is-valid?` was a **phantom** named at `Parser.hs:943` and nowhere else in the compiler, and now fails `check` at exit 1 instead of warning and exiting 0. **The gate had no precedent** because the defect is downstream of every check-time gate: it lives in BUILD-GATE-1, and its negative control caught one of our own assertions wrong by construction, matching `[a-z_]+ regex-match ` against an emission whose preceding character is a quote. `ALIAS-LOWER-1` deliberately untouched and pinned by a test; the six glyphs are now its entire scope | 2026-08-10 | 1683 Haskell, 203 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.96`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.95** | **The fourth gate, and the three defects its live green run could not reach.** No compiler change. `TOOL-RFC-004` ports DRIFT-DOC-3 ([`doc_archive_gate.sh`](../scripts/doc_archive_gate.sh), 218 bash lines) into **575 of LLMLL across two modules**, and both decide in `spec-roundtrip` as an `oracle`. **The 17-cell differential cover (14 mutations, 3 negative controls) found three defects that had already survived a live green run and six cells run by hand**: criterion 1 was not implemented at all, criterion 7 printed its headline without the reference's two-line remedy epilogue, and two cover cells were wrong by construction. **The reason a live run does not decide this is measured**: of 59 files under `docs/archive/`, exactly ONE declares a disposition, so the live corpus exercises one of four vocabulary values and **zero of the four violation classes**. The adjudicator is [its own module](../tools/doc-archive/adjudicate.llmll) because a `def` cannot call a sibling `def` and the failure is at check time, and its contract is **input-side** because the scrutinee-relating alternative is falsely refuted by `MATCH-TERM-EQ-1`; measured, it verifies SAFE `body-faithful: side-of` and misrouting one arm is REFUTED at constraint #1. First port to answer the standard's `## 7. Verification` in full, with three instruments that fail differently and `STRLIT-BODY-1` recorded as its one absence. DRIFT-DOC-3 leaves the fast job for `spec-roundtrip`, a knowing distribution deviation: the port needs a compiler the published image does not ship. Filed `RUN-STDIN-1`. Four records corrected, one of them a CI job's display name, and the restart record rewritten in ASD-STE100 | 2026-08-10 | 1679 Haskell, 203 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.95`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.94** | **The example suite runs in CI, and the binary contradicts its own help text.** `CI-BUILD-TEST-1` closed: all 1679 hspec examples had been a local-only gate for the life of `version-gate.yml`, so a green `main` said nothing about them. Wired into `spec-roundtrip` under `--fail-on=pending`, which is not decoration: 55 examples go pending without a solver and hspec scores pending as non-failure, so a solver-free job would print the same headline and the same exit 0 while skipping all 55. **The ubuntu figure the filing row could not supply is now measured at ~4m07s**, the suite's own first CI run being what measured it, so the rejected third-job option stays rejected. `FEAS-MIN-1` fixed: `minimizeWitness` asserted only a decreasing bound, so convergence was a property of z3's heuristic rather than of the algorithm, and on the shipped z3 4.8.12 it descended by 39 per round from a seed of 1000000000. The νZ objective the design called normative is now emitted, in a **separate renderer**, because `(minimize ...)` beside `(check-sat-using qsat)` makes z3 discard the objective **silently** on both 4.8.12 and 4.15.4. Verdicts cannot move by construction, the call sitting inside the `Z3Sat` branch after the verdict is mapped. Also: three advertised invocations that do not exist are corrected (`build --emit json-ast` in the compiler's own help, `hub fetch pkg@ver` in two docs), the README command table is reconciled against the running binary by the **reverse** audit that can find a lie rather than only omissions, the TOOL-RFC standard gains `## 7. Verification` (name two instruments that fail differently; the three shipped RFCs retrofitted rather than exempted, and 001's `--strict-verified-core` pass named as vacuous), and `LLMLL.md` §4.4.1 is corrected from partial order to **preorder**, `verified` and `verified-lean` being peer tops. Filed `MATCH-TERM-EQ-1` (a `match` arm refines the tag but not the term, so an identity function with a `post` relating `result` to its scrutinee is **falsely refuted**) and `TOOL-ORACLE-1` | 2026-08-09 | 1679 Haskell, 203 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.94`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.93** | **The toolchain reads its own source as UTF-8.** `TOOL-ENCODING-1` closed. `LLMLL.md` §2 has always said source files **are** UTF-8; the compiler read them through `TIO.readFile`, which decodes via the ambient locale, so a source file's meaning depended on the environment that compiled it. Seven sites move to bytes-plus-explicit-decode, including `Module.hs`'s `parseFile`, the site governing import resolution that the roadmap row had missed. Invalid UTF-8 and a byte-order mark now produce a **parse diagnostic** instead of an escaped GHC exception; the BOM is rejected rather than stripped, and the offset is computed by walking Unicode table 3-7 on the failure path, because the codec's exception carries the offending byte and not its position. **The acceptance criterion was the removal of `doc_claims_cover.py`'s `LC_ALL` pin**, shipped pre-marked at v0.14.92: with no locale set that cover is the only gate that fails if the compiler ever again decodes through the environment, and it went green on Linux with 17 cells and 3 negative controls. **The census the row asked for is taken and its premise was wrong**: 142 of 259 committed `.llmll` files hold non-ASCII bytes, but until this release **zero** held one in token position, which is why an empty-looking population had been carrying the bytes all along. Filed `ALIAS-LOWER-1` (eight of nine §2.4 aliases do not work, and the row that implied they were a `REGEX-LOWER-1` subset is corrected), `DIAG-KIND-SEXPR-1` and `READ-SILENT-1` | 2026-08-08 | 1675 Haskell, 203 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.93`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.92** | **The third gate, and two defects the live corpus could not reach.** No compiler change: the binary, the JSON-AST schema and the language surface are identical to v0.14.91, and what ships is the TOOL-LL campaign's third port. [`docclaims.llmll`](../tools/doc-claims/docclaims.llmll) reproduces `doc_claims_gate.sh` (15 fixtures, each run through a named compiler and graded against its `@expect:` header), at `tool_state: oracle` with both implementations wired as adjacent steps in `spec-roundtrip`. The `@expect` grammar is implemented in FULL, which campaign §2's retirement rule forces rather than taste: deleting the reference one release later turns any shortfall into a capability regression. **The differential cover found two real defects in the port before either shipped, and neither was reachable from the live corpus**, which always names a working compiler and always passes: a `warn` observation promoted on the presence of `warning:` alone where the reference requires the pinned substring too, and a subject probe that SKIPPED where the reference FAILS. **Two bugs in the cover itself as well**, the instructive one being that the two implementations were given DIFFERENT ENVIRONMENTS, so the port found an `llmll` on `PATH` the reference could not see; a cover that varies the environment between its sides compares two worlds, not two implementations. `SKIP-SILENT-1` filed rather than fixed here, on the rule that a port reproduces its reference | 2026-08-08 | 1666 Haskell, 197 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.92`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.91** | **Two absences, and what the callers did instead.** Both gaps were filed by the TOOL-LL campaign's second port and left open there, because campaign §9 makes a `[CT][SPEC]` gap's SHAPE language-team's call and not the porter's. Both are the same species: an operation the language did not have, whose absence every call site worked around locally, so nothing in the toolchain could see a defect. `JSON-SCALAR-1` ships **four** projections (`json-as-string`/`int`/`bool`/`number`, each `Json -> Result[T,string]`), the field family minus the key; `json-array` was already a projection of that shape, so this completes a one-member family and the five partition `Json` with no coercion between them. **The silence was misattributed and the closure corrects it**: `(json-get-string x "")` on a scalar is `err`, not `""`, and the empty string came from the port's own `(unwrap-or ... "")` habit, which is why a `Result` shape rather than a `""`-on-failure one is the whole adjudication. `PROC-MERGE-1` ships **equal path strings mean merge**, one handle on both streams, no signature change, repairing a case that was previously two handles at position 0 overwriting each other. **Its prior question was settled by measurement before the mechanism was chosen: ordering is not part of the contract and cannot be**, the interleaving being the child's flush order (measured: a child writing stdout first lands stderr first). Both fixtures are **executed** rather than compiled, because neither defect is visible to a check-only gate, and **both were mutation-checked**: an unconditional merge flips the split control, and a coercing `json-as-string` flips exactly the two refusal cells while all three value cells stay correct | 2026-08-08 | 1666 Haskell, 197 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.91`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.90** | **A handle with no encoding to set.** `CAPTURE-ENCODING-1`: a console program emitted a string literal's non-ASCII characters as `codepoint mod 256` — `→` as `c2 92`, `✅` as `05`, an astral character as a **NUL** — although the emitted `main` already called `setLocaleEncoding utf8` and pinned `utf8` on all three standard handles. **The cause is that `System.Posix.IO.fdToHandle` returns a BINARY handle, and binary mode is the ABSENCE of a codec rather than a wrong one**, so there was nothing for the locale to inform: probed, `getLocaleEncoding` answers `UTF-8` while both pipe ends answer `hGetEncoding = Nothing`. The v0.14.86 note claiming `setLocaleEncoding` covered "the createPipe/fdToHandle pair without naming them" was half wrong — right about the `openFile` event log, false about the pipe. Fixed by pinning both ends; each was ablated separately and **placement is part of the fix**, the write-end pin having to precede `hDuplicateTo` or the redirect carries binary mode onto `stdout`. **Why no gate saw it**: no committed `.llmll` had a non-ASCII literal reaching stdout, and the one encoding fixture that existed uses `§` — a FIXED POINT of the bug, since every codepoint below U+0100 survives the truncation unchanged. Ships a fixture that compares BYTES and is exercised on every platform, and removes both pre-marked workarounds, so the refute-crux port and its reference now have their labels compared rather than reconciled | 2026-08-08 | 1656 Haskell, 197 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.90`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.89** | **The gate that could not survive its own corpus.** `FD-CAPTURE-1`: `captureStdout` duplicated stdout on **every step** and never closed the duplicate, so the descriptor came back only when GC happened to finalize the `Handle`, and under the non-threaded RTS a long console run dies with `file descriptor NNNN out of range for select (0--1024)`. **Nothing in the tree had hit it** (`versiongate.llmll` takes nine steps), so the first program needing four figures of steps was the first to find it, by dying at fd 1103 partway through its corpus; `lsof` on a live run showed **139 open write handles to the program's own redirected stdout**, and three isolation probes (1400 stdout-only steps, 1400 `wasi.fs.copy`, 400 `wasi.proc.run` spawns) each survive, which is what names the leak a creation-versus-finalization race rather than any one builtin. Fix is `hClose oldStdout` **after** the restore, placement included, and it changes the emitted Haskell for every generated `console` program. The regression test pins the EMITTED SOURCE and not a step count, because a GC-timing defect makes "N steps, no crash" pass on a broken tree whenever the collector keeps up. Plus **TOOL-RFC-002**: the refute-crux gate in LLMLL, reproducing all 80 frozen verdicts and agreeing with the shell reference, `tool_state: oracle`, both running as adjacent steps. **The RFC's own feasibility read had concluded "nothing here is BLOCKS" and "no new gap was discovered", and both were false**: §5 keeps the wrong conclusion quoted above the correction. Filed and not fixed: `JSON-SCALAR-1` (a bare scalar in a JSON array has no accessor, so the port ran its whole corpus with the flags silently dropped) and `PROC-MERGE-1` | 2026-08-07 | 1656 Haskell, 191 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.89`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.88** | **The sibling holes the brief called filled, and six gates that had to be found before they could be ported.** `HOLE-STATUS-SIBLING`, a follow-on to `HOLE-STATUS` (v0.14.21): that fix made the checkout brief tell the truth about the function being checked out and left every **other** function reading `filled` unconditionally, so a sibling whose body still held a hole was presented to a fill agent as an available callable carrying a discharged contract. **The brief is the sole information channel to that agent.** Measured on a three-def fixture, `hole-two` read `filled` while genuinely being a hole. `status` becomes three-way (`hole` for the enclosing function, the new **`unfilled`** for a body containing a hole, `filled` otherwise); the enclosing function deliberately does not fold into `unfilled`, because the brief carries no `enclosing_function` field and `hole` is its only identification of the target by name. **Unfilled siblings are marked, not withheld**: `refine` spawns contracted sub-holes meant to be called before they are filled, so withholding would break cascading decomposition. The predicate is a positive match on `HoleKind` and **not** a negation of `holeStatus'`, whose catch-all collapses `HNamed` into the same bucket as `HProofRequired` and would have made the filter a no-op on exactly the holes at issue: that was the first attempt, caught by measuring rather than reading. `brief_version` moves to 0.12.3. Plus **DRIVER-LL sub-phase 4e** and the **TOOL-LL** campaign: a standard, an eight-section RFC template, a pytest gate enforcing both, `TOOL-RFC-001` recording the shipped version-gate port retroactively, and `TOOL-RFC-002` as the first port written **before** its code. `refute-crux-gate.sh` froze 80 verify verdicts and **no workflow ran it**; it was written in response to `ENUM-EQ-FALLBACK` (refutation silently lost for twenty versions, v0.14.12 to v0.14.31) and then never wired in, so the answer to that incident ran only when someone typed `make`. It now runs in `version-gate.yml`'s `spec-roundtrip` job. Filed and not fixed: `MODE-CLI-1`, `SPLIT-EMPTY-1`, `FS-WALK-1`. Release hygiene: **v0.14.84 through v0.14.87 shipped with no tag and no image** and are now backfilled and published | 2026-08-07 | 1656 Haskell, 189 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.88`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.87** | **A slice that clamps, and a validator the subject cannot reach into.** `string-slice` returned the wrong **length** rather than the wrong offset: the unclamped `take (to - from) (drop from s)` fed a negative `start` into the take count while `drop` no-opped on it, so `(string-slice "abc" -3 0)` returned the whole string for a window ending at zero. Both endpoints now clamp into `[0, string-length s]`, matching the `string-char-at` out-of-range convention the family already had; a transposed pair still yields `""`, nothing in the tree depended on the old behaviour, and there is no verification impact since `string-slice` is uninterpreted. The `schema-version-mismatch` rejection stopped citing a `json-ast-versioning.md` under the docs directory, **which never existed**, and points at the schema's own `description` field instead; `doc_path_lint` structurally cannot catch that class, since a citation compiled into the binary is invisible to a markdown linter, so a test now scans the message and asserts each `docs/` path resolves. Plus **DRIVER-LL sub-phase 4b**. Filed and not fixed: `PROC-TIMEOUT-1` | 2026-08-05 | 1651 Haskell, 131 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.87`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.86** | **The program wrote the bytes correctly, then died printing them.** `FS-ENCODING-1` completed: the v0.14.84 implementation pinned UTF-8 on `wasi_fs_read` / `wasi_fs_write` and left the generated program's **standard** handles on the ambient locale, so under `LC_ALL=C` the `fs-encoding` fixture wrote 17 bytes of correct UTF-8 to disk, read them back correctly, and died printing them (`hPutChar: invalid argument (cannot encode character '\167')`). Steps 3 through 9 never ran, so **one** encode failure surfaced as four independent gate failures plus an absent `big.copy`, and the report named `wasi.fs.copy`, **which had not executed at all**. `main` now issues `setLocaleEncoding utf8` and pins `stdin` / `stdout` / `stderr` explicitly in both entry modes that run; both halves are needed, because the RTS creates the standard handles before `main` (so the locale move cannot reach them retroactively) and GHC's `dupHandle_` rebuilds a duplicate's codec from `getLocaleEncoding` (so `captureStdout`'s `hDuplicate` / `hDuplicateTo` pair reset stdout's encoding every step, whatever was pinned). **This changes the emitted Haskell for every generated `console` and `cli` program**, which is why the release is a bump and not a patch to the gate. **The gate stops over-claiming**: GHC on macOS resolves UTF-8 under every `LC_ALL`, so stage 5b's encoding half is a structural no-op there, and on Darwin it now prints **NOT EXERCISED** for that half rather than the PASS it reported for the whole of v0.14.84 while the property was false. A second, independently blocking defect fixed in the same change: `build_smoke.sh` resolved `$LLMLL_BIN` **after** stages had `cd`'d out of the caller's directory, so CI's `stack exec llmll --` took the temp directory for its project root; the binary is now anchored to an absolute path once, before anything changes directory. Verified red to green in a `haskell:9.6.6` container reproducing the CI invocation exactly. Two residues of the same class recorded untouched as `TOOL-ENCODING-1` | 2026-08-05 | 1642 Haskell, 123 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.86`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.85** | **argv in, status out, and a starved stdin stops reporting success.** `PROC-BOUNDARY-1`, the process boundary DRIVER-LL sub-phase 4a was blocked on. **`wasi.proc.args`** delivers the argument vector (`argv[0]` excluded) nullary, on the **existing** `RList` arm under the **existing** `ENonDet` label: no new arm, no seventh effect label, no grammar or schema change for this half, and it rides RC-3, so a program issues it as `:init`'s command and reads it as `r0` while `:init`'s arity stays put. **`def-main :status`** is a new optional field, a total projection from the state type to `int`, applied to the final state when `:done?` holds; absent means exit 0. Where `:done?` **is** declared and stdin exhausts first the program exits a fixed **70** and `:status` is not consulted, which closes the defect that a console loop ending at `if eof then return ()` wrote partial state and exited 0 with no diagnostic. **No breaking change, and the reason is the gate**: the exhaustion rule keys on whether `:done?` is DECLARED rather than on whether it fired, so a program with no completion predicate keeps exiting 0 on EOF, measured on all three such programs in the tree. The range refinement `0 <= result <= 255` is the **user's** to declare and the compiler injects nothing, so an undeclared `:status` returning 256 still exits 0 and reports success; the `tcWarn` for that case is named in the design and **owed**, as is `DONE-TYPE-1`, a pre-existing `:done?` check defect this work found by executing. | **v0.14.84** | **The bytes the text channel could not carry.** Two filesystem gaps DRIVER-LL Phase 4 needed. **`FS-ENCODING-1`**: `wasi.fs.read`/`write` went through `readFile`/`writeFile` and so decoded and encoded via the ambient locale, which under a POSIX locale fails to read a **valid** UTF-8 file and fails to write any non-ASCII string; both bodies now pin `utf8` on an explicit handle. The design record's mechanism was **wrong and is corrected here**: nothing threw, because `llmll_publish_io`'s `try` plus the existing `evaluate` already made the failure a value, so the defect was availability rather than crash-freedom, and a gate asserting the absence of a traceback would have passed before and after. Measured against a forced ASCII handle encoding; macOS GHC ignores `LC_ALL`, so **no Linux run has confirmed it** and the new gate is what will. **`FS-COPY-1`**: `wasi.fs.copy` ships, byte-faithful, because read-then-write cannot express a copy of a binary artifact at all (`wasi.fs.read` of one returns `RErr` under UTF-8 as much as under any encoding); carries `Caps {EFsRead, EFsWrite}`, exactly what read-plus-write already grants, with no new `Response` arm, effect label, capability namespace, or schema change. `build_smoke.sh` gains a stage that runs both under `LC_ALL=C`, hashing a file and its copy and round-tripping a multi-buffer read; the second is the guard for a force escaping the `bracket`, which returns truncated contents with **no error**, mutation-checked at "178890 bytes in, 93 out". Also: the driver's halt channels split three ways so a stage-contract failure stops recording as a fired gate (37 sites moved, nine stay `stopped` by clause), and **DRIVER-LL Phase 4 is specified** at Rev 5, whose §3.6 found that the classification rule used two of `Outcome`'s four proved constructors and would have lost `PartialThenHalt`. **`FS-ENCODING-1` was incomplete as shipped here and is completed at v0.14.86** (`f001394`): it pinned the two fs bodies' handles and not the generated program's own, so the sentence above about no Linux run having confirmed it got its answer on the first CI run, which failed. The new gate did exactly what this row said it would, and what it found was that this row's fix was half of one | 2026-08-04 | 1616 Haskell, 115 Python | [`CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.84`](../CHANGELOG.md) |
| **v0.14.83** | **Four stages of the driver, and the replay that could not check them.** DRIVER-LL Phase 3's porting work finishes: `spine.llmll` runs stages **E, J, L and G2** end to end against the committed TFTP execution with **nine** body-faithful `def`s, zero FFI declarations, and every proved core called rather than reimplemented. **Stage A is a STOP**, filed rather than worked around, which puts `HTTP-GET-1` on the critical path its own row had denied. The larger half is `llmll replay`, which had misread its own log format since the log existed: a turn's wire bytes are `output ++ "\n"`, so an event is `count('\n')+1` lines and `replayOne` read one.
