Four bodies added: real IO for write, an idempotent `doesFileExist`-guarded delete (`removeFile` throws on a missing path, and an uncaught exception inside a `Command` breaks crash-freedom), a **performing-and-discarding** read forced with `evaluate . length` (`readFile` is lazy, so a discarded lazy result performs no read at all and would compile, run, and silently do nothing), and a stderr-diagnosed non-network `http.post` stub also surfaced through `stmtWarnings`, previously a `_ -> []` stub. | **v0.14.78** | **The `bytes[n]` length is earned at every position, and the FACT-AG-LEN line closes.** **Stage 3 of 3**, the return position: no `bytes[n]` length now enters a VC antecedent from a declaration without an obligation discharging it. `bytesLenRetPost`, the symmetric twin of Stage 1's `bytesLenParamPre`, conjoins the length into `returnRefinementPost` so `augmentContractPost` folds it into the effective post; `resultLenFact` is deleted from `lhsPred`, so a body proves its own result length and callers recover it as an assumed post; `bytesLenReft` is deleted with it. `admits` narrows to `boolValuedMapTy`, which the design argued is its terminal state rather than the EMPTY predicate its module header claimed. **Two implementation findings changed the settled design.** `admits` and the WILD-ASSUME seams had to split into a soundness set (`admits`) and a diagnostic set (`wildAssumeRejects` = `admits` plus bytes), because dropping the arm from a shared predicate makes the seams stop firing; containment holds by construction, and there is a third `admits` call site (`unify`) where the design named two. And Stage 3 **empties a solver-crash class no revision predicted**: a contract-free bytes-returning callee bound its call result at `FQInt` because `calleeRetSort` reads the stored contract, so `bytesLen` over it crashed liquid-fixpoint instead of returning a verdict. **The payoff**: a contract-free `bytes[n]`-returning function is now body-faithful and `verified`, which is the composition the line existed to enable. **Not closed**: recursive `bytes[n]` returns stay on the assumption channel at tier `asserted`, and the identity function on bytes still cannot prove its own length (a pre-existing body-VC fragment boundary, measured identical at Stage 2). Gate: corpus **252 files, 250 verdict-identical**, the only two changes being the two new fixtures, both `SOLVER-CRASH` at Stage 2, and no pre-existing file moving; `examples/` held no bytes-composition program before this release, so the sweep is a regression check and the witness set is those fixtures plus `Spec.hs`. No `checker_soundness_version` bump, because the evidence hash already identifies the affected population per function. New row **HASH-PRE-ASYM** (the hash preimage folds the augmented post but the raw pre, so Stage 1's pre augmentation moved no hash; empty in-tree population). refute-crux gate 59 to 61 cases. +12 tests. | 2026-08-01 | 1475 Hs / 107 Py | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.78` |
| **v0.14.77** | **The bytes constructor establishes its own length instead of borrowing it.** FACT-AG-LEN **Stage 2 of 3**, the `(bytes-zero)` constructor axiom. `(bytes-zero)` translates to `Map_default(0)`, which carries no length, so a constructed buffer's length came entirely from `resultLenFact` on the constraint LHS. A new `bodyToPredM` equation emits it as an axiom instead: a `CallVC` with `cvPreObligation = Nothing` and `cvPostAssumption = Just (r = Map_default(0) and bytesLen(r) = n)`, ASSUME polarity with no PROVE side. `reifyBytesZeroLen` supplies `n` at the `body'` seam, since `bodyToPredM` sees neither an `AliasMap` nor an `mRet`; the match is head-syntactic on `TBytes n`, agreeing with the checker and codegen. **The axiom does real work**, measured by deleting `resultLenFact` with no axiom added, which flips `zeros8` SAFE to REFUTED. **Its validity is a trust-channel dependency** on codegen faithfulness under the `codegen_semantics_version` stamp, not a contract discharge, and it appears on no channel of the trust report (new row TRUST-AXIOM). **The bytes algebra does not close here**, contrary to the design's Rev 2: the two lemmas cannot meet inside one body, so closure is Stage 3's. Gate is **verdict-identity on the `bytes-zero`-bodied population plus the two new tests**, not the corpus sweep: the 273-file sweep shows 0 verdict deltas but `examples/` held no `bytes-zero` at all, so it is silent on this change rather than passing it. `.fq` byte-identity unavailable (the fresh axiom binder shifts the body counter). New construction-class fixture `examples/bytes-bounds/zero-buffer.llmll`; refute-crux gate 58 to 59 cases. No `checker_soundness_version` bump. +2 tests. | 2026-08-01 | 1463 Hs / 107 Py | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.77` |
| **v0.14.76** | **The `bytes[n]` length is earned at the call site, not asserted from the declaration.** FACT-AG-LEN **Stage 1 of 3**, the parameter position. `bytesLenParamPre` moves `bytesLen(v) = n` off the parameter binder refinement into the effective pre, so §5.3.4's call rule proves it at each call site under PROVE polarity and the callee assumes it, exactly as for a hand-written `pre`; `buildContractEnvWith` already augments the stored `ContractEnv`, so one edit supplies both the definition-site assume and the call-site prove. The LEVER-A1 gate **self-activates** (`bytes-length` is in `bytesOpNames` and `arrGateActive` reads the augmented contract), so `typeToSort` is untouched and the review's proposed widening, which would have array-sorted `Result` and ADT components, was rejected on that measurement. `emitParamBind` keeps `byteArraySort` and drops only the predicate. **Does not close SAFE-ARG**: the return position is still shielded by `verify` rather than discharged by an obligation, and 10 attempted shapes produced **no witness**, which is a measured absence and not a proof. Stages 2 (the `bytes-zero` constructor axiom) and 3 (return position) are open, and the stage ordering is a correctness constraint. Gates: corpus **251 files, 0 verdict delta** on a revert-and-rebuild baseline, evidence hashes unchanged, no `checker_soundness_version` bump. Two of the three pre-declared delta populations have **zero** in-tree witnesses, so the sweep is silent on them rather than passing them. +1 test. | 2026-08-01 | 1461 Hs / 107 Py | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.76` |
| **v0.14.75** | **One type-admissibility predicate, so the checker and the emitter cannot disagree.** v0.14.74 fixed CR-01 by adding a `TDependent` clause; the defect was the *two* type-membership algorithms serving one semantic notion with nothing relating them, and a second instance was already queued at component positions (`assumesFactBoolValue` had no `TCustom` clause). New leaf module `LLMLL.TypeAdmissibility` holds the alias normalization and `admits`, **defined over the emitter's own fact-injection gates** (`bytesLenOf`, `boolValuedMapTy`) rather than mirroring them, so agreement is definitional. Total on unnormalized input; `compatibleWith` threads `AliasMap` so the guard lives inside the structural predicate and every caller inherits it. **ADMIT-OVER** stated: `admits` must over-approximate the emitter's injection set, one-directionally — too wide costs a rejection, too narrow is a false SAFE — and deliberately ignores the body-dependent `arrGateActive`, because type acceptance must not depend on a callee's body. Cycle guards added: `resolveAliasTy` previously diverged on a non-contractive alias, shielded only by `check` failing before `verify` ran, a shield this change removes. Gates: acceptance delta **0** across 151 files, `.fq` **byte-identical 145/145**, sidecars reproduced byte-for-byte, no `checker_soundness_version` bump. +11 tests. | 2026-08-01 | 1460 Hs / 107 Py | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.75` |
| **v0.14.74** | **WILD-ASSUME-2: the `map[k,bool]` arm closes, with no reaching-SAFE witness.** `assumesFact` extends to the map class with the same key/value admissibility `FixpointEmit.boolValuedMapTy` uses, reaching both seams (`structuralUnify` for arguments, `compatibleWith`/`unify` for returns) through one predicate. SA-8/SA-9 prove it live, laundering through an unannotated hop against the freshened `?$N` form; SA-6, SA-10, SA-12, SA-14, SA-15 hold the over-breadth line; SA-11 answers alias coverage by measurement, zero code change. `wildAssumeFactNoun` names the fact per class (SA-16). `checker_soundness_version` **not bumped** (cited gate reading plus a zero-delta corpus comparison). This phase's own code review then found `assumesFact` matched at the outermost constructor only, so a `where`-wrapped base type evaded the restriction on BOTH arms (the shipped `bytes[n]` arm included, affected v0.14.34..v0.14.73) while `FixpointEmit.resolveAliasTy` stripped the wrapper and asserted the fact anyway; `assumesFact` now strips `TDependent`, and the diagnostic takes its noun from the expanded type so an aliased rejection stops degrading to the generic fallback (SA-17, both arms). This closes a measured member of the SAFE-ARG class with no reaching-SAFE witness; the corpus run is a regression check, not evidence the fix works. +10 tests. | 2026-08-01 | 1449 Hs / 107 Py | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.74` |

Not bool-specific and not literal-specific. Stage (a) records `tau_ret` on `TCState` and consumes it via `effRet`; stage (b) carries it cross-module on `meRetTypes` and through the synthetic emit paths. A third stage (HOLE-RET) was implemented and withdrawn after it demoted 12 corpus functions. **RET-BRANCH-PREF Stage 1** additionally prefers the concrete branch at an `if` join when the other is a self-recursive call. +12 tests. | 2026-07-29 | 1428 Hs / 106 Py | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.72` |
| **v0.14.71** | **Stage G2, the artifact audit; and three checks that were never run.** New pipeline stage between the disposition pass and the probes checks every citation against the bytes stage A pinned, which nothing did: spec §7 validates a delegated output's *shape*, which a reason misreading its own quote satisfies, and §14 pins the source without checking that any stage read it. STOPs on a citation that does not resolve; **reports and never thresholds** a near-miss span and an absent strength, both of which fire on correct rows; delegates the reading of each stated reason and requires the flag to quote both sides so the driver can check the words occur. A misread reason on a **characteristic-core** row STOPs, since that is the unevidenced input gate J would otherwise rule on. The mechanical test is **token coverage with a measured threshold**: a substring test fails **22 of 113 correct RFC 1350 rows** (censuses elide and flatten diagrams), while true citations score ≥ 0.875 against wrong spans at 0.500 p99. Replayed on the real bytes: 109/109 resolve. Also: `--self-test` skipped gate J's third condition, unevaluable on its own data since TFTP's 53 exclusions predate the `barrier` field, and ran in no CI job or test at all; refute-crux gains a third verdict kind so a **capability violation** stops counting as a solver refutation; and `B7`'s retired test is annotated in the two frozen records that still carry it, which yields a second unprompted sample (3/4 on ARP) for an open question without a fourth run. +15 tests. | 2026-07-28 | tooling/doc/CI only, no Haskell change; 106 Py + 58 crux | `CHANGELOG.md §v0.14.71` |

RFC 4648 is the first run to STOP for a real reason: gate J halted on the characteristic-core condition after stages A-I, 76 min, no wave; the committed prediction called the outcome and missed the mechanism (line wrapping, not bit regrouping) and forecast <20% carried against a measured 84.6%. `B7`'s test was undecidable as written (Budd and Angluin 1982) and false on the row that fired the gate, replaced by a named entailment; driver-spec §6 rewritten from the observed STOP; three driver crash/liveness/workdir failures fixed; refute-crux gate addresses suites by path and copies the whole suite (41 → 58 cases). | **STRICT-SIBLING** (same-run sibling evidence for strict-core admission) | **Settled by design (2026-07-12)** — bottom-up sidecar staging IS the evidence model: admission runs at type-check before verification (no in-pass fresh-evidence channel, `TypeCheck.hs:486-490`), the sidecar is the single trust path, and the designed idiom (`def` leaves + `def-shell` layers) verifies whole files in one command (`banking_ledger`). Same-run admission declined + scoped with a reopening trigger: [`strict-sibling-disposition.md`](archive/shipped-design-specs/strict-sibling-disposition.md). |
| **XMOD-STALE** (persistent ModuleCache coherence under LSP/Serve/watch) | **Closed — no consumer exists (2026-07-11).** The hypothesized long-lived-cache consumer does not exist: `ModuleCache` appears only on the per-invocation CLI path; `Serve.hs` is stateless per request by recorded design (no `IORef`/`MVar`/`TVar`, source re-read per request; professor + language-team decision 2026-03-28), and no LSP server or watch mode exists in `compiler/`. Nothing to build. **Standing constraint:** any *future* long-lived `ModuleCache` consumer (Serve/LSP/watch) must evict a callee `ModuleEnv` on a callee source/sidecar mtime change, per [`def-ret-staleness-hash-review.md`](archive/professor-reviews/def-ret-staleness-hash-review.md) §G2. |

That premise is false. Exactness was never a property of this analysis. |

> **Twenty-nine rows**, counted 2026-09-04, that had shipped, settled or been refuted but were still sitting in [Active Items](#active-items), the oldest since v0.14.66.
