Only the column headings differ, because "Current Status" is not what a history section holds.
>
> **This section grows, so the heading's date is an identity and not a claim.** The `2026-08-09` in the heading is load-bearing as an anchor: it is deep-linked from the [Active Items](#active-items) note and from `docs/UPDATE-PROTOCOL.md` §3.1, so it does not move when a row arrives later. **The count in this paragraph is the claim; count the table rather than reading the heading.** The count was already stale once, reading "twenty-three" while the table held twenty-four, because `REGEX-LOWER-1` was moved in on 2026-08-10 without it.
>
> **Four rows are exceptions to the verbatim rule, and "closed after the 2026-08-09 move" does not identify them.** Five rows have now closed after that move, and `PROC-STDIN-1` and `PROC-STDIN-SHARE-1` are not exceptions: measured against the tree before `ec8be9a`, each of those two differs from its Active Items text by twelve bytes, the status verb and nothing else. The exception is a cell REWRITTEN rather than preserved. For each of the four, the status cell records the closing shape, which is a status move and doc-lead's to make. **`CI-BUILD-TEST-1`** (closed v0.14.94) keeps its "Next Action (as filed)" cell unchanged even though `bc7107d` is the feasibility read that cell asks for; rewriting it to say so is the originating role's authoring act, on the same D1 boundary named below. **`REGEX-LOWER-1`** (shipped v0.14.96) is the second. **`FS-WALK-1`** (closed 2026-08-10) is the third, and it was the first row here to close **without shipping anything**: it was retired by a measurement showing the gap does not bite any gate in scope, so both of its cells are rewritten rather than preserved. **`LIST-RANGE-1`** (closed 2026-08-16) is the fourth and is the second to close without shipping, on different ground again: `FS-WALK-1` named a real gap that no gate in scope meets, while this one was REFUTED, the builtin it called absent having shipped at v0.11. Both of its cells are rewritten and each carries the text it replaces inside it, so the claim survives the correction. A row can close because the work was done, because the work turned out not to be needed, or because the gap was never there, and those leave three different records.
>
> **Why here and not [Shipped Releases](#shipped-releases).** That table is one line per *version* (`Version | Headline | Date | Tests | Detail`); these are per-*row* records and do not fit it. This section is the per-item destination, and the [Active Items](#active-items) note already names it as such. **Nothing was condensed into the one-line idiom used by the table above**: rewriting a row's cells is an authoring act reserved to the row's originating role, not available to a doc pass ([`UPDATE-PROTOCOL.md`](UPDATE-PROTOCOL.md) D1 excludes table-row cells even from doc-lead's verbatim-apply path). A later pass by the owning role may compress these. Per-version narrative for every row below is in [`../CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md).

<details><summary><strong>Closed <code>[CT]</code> rows, verbatim (click to expand)</strong></summary>

| Item | Status at closure | Next Action (as filed) |
|------|-------------------|------------------------|
| **PROC-STDIN-1** (`wasi.proc.run` cannot supply a child's stdin) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.98.** Raised by TOOL-RFC-006 on 2026-08-11 and **measured on a built binary rather than read off the table**. `wasi.proc.run` takes six parameters, executable, argv, cwd, stdout path, stderr path and timeout ([`TypeCheck.hs:200`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)), and the codegen sets `std_out` and `std_err` and leaves `std_in` unset ([`CodegenHs.hs:740-744`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)). **A console-mode LLMLL program takes one line of stdin per step, so DRIVING one IS feeding it stdin.** That makes this gap structural for port 006 rather than incidental: [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) pipes input into the binary it just built at **thirteen sites**, at lines 327, 443, 535, 594, 655, 837, 843, 850, 858, 865, 892, 894 and 1065, and those sites hold **295 of 504 stage code lines**. **The disposition is SHAPES and not BLOCKS, on a measurement**: `wasi.proc.run` can spawn `/bin/sh -c`, and a real console-mode binary driven through such a pipe completed its whole step loop with the input the parent chose. **Distinct from `RUN-STDIN-1`**, which is about `llmll run` passing `""` to its child from `Main.hs:903`; this row is about a builtin that has no parameter to pass. Deliberately not folded, on the `ALIAS-LOWER-1` precedent | A seventh parameter, a stdin path, symmetric with the two path parameters the builtin already takes. **The cost of not fixing it is specific and is not a line count**: `TypeCheck.hs:190-196` states that the exec/argv split exists so the executable is a syntactic constant a reader can enumerate, and so shell metacharacter interpretation stops being a category. Routing thirteen sites through `sh -c` rebuilds the shell string at exactly the sites that run the other five ports. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md`](design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md) §4 |
| **PROC-STDIN-SHARE-1** (a child spawned by `wasi.proc.run` shares the parent's stdin and can steal a torn fragment of it) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.98.** Raised by TOOL-RFC-006 on 2026-08-11. **This is a defect, not only a missing feature.** Because `std_in` is unset, `createProcess` inherits, so parent and child read the SAME descriptor. **Measured on aarch64-osx with a console-mode parent and a child running `read L`**: at 55 and 7,893 bytes of parent stdin the child reads nothing and the parent's step sequence is intact; at 9,693 bytes the child reads `NE1034`, which is the TAIL of `LINE1034` cut where the parent's 8 KiB buffer ended. **The threshold is the handle buffer and the outcome is a race**: three runs at 138,893 bytes took three different victims, `NE1034`, `LINE18` and `LINE4`, and in the third the parent SKIPPED a step input. A file-backed stdin of the same size showed no theft. **The defect needs TWO conditions: a child that reads stdin, AND a parent holding more than 8,192 bytes on its own.** A first census asked only about the child and was too narrow. **The population is 16 call sites across 8 committed programs**, counted 2026-08-11, and two of them spawn an AI agent CLI ([`sequencer.llmll:1248`](../tools/llmll-driver/sequencer.llmll), [`wave.llmll:623`](../tools/llmll-driver/wave.llmll)), so condition one is not ruled out. **What protects the tree today is condition TWO**: measured, the covers feed `sequencer` 1,600 bytes (`driver_ll_cover.py`, `BUDGET = 800`), `wave` 800 bytes (`wave_cover.py`, `BUDGET = 400`) and `versiongate` 120 bytes. All are under 8,192. **The margin is a number nobody chose for this reason**: raising `BUDGET` to 4,097 crosses it, no comment states the constraint and no gate checks it. **A passing gate is therefore not evidence of safety here**, only evidence that three stdin volumes are small | Bind `std_in` explicitly. Closing `PROC-STDIN-1` with a stdin path closes this at the same time, since a named path removes the sharing. **A note for `RUN-STDIN-1`'s fix**: that row proposes `std_in = Inherit` for `llmll run`, which is correct while the child is the only reader, and this row is the case where a second reader exists. Found by [`design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md`](design/tool-rfc-006-build-smoke.md) F2 |
| **FD-CAPTURE-1** (`captureStdout` leaks one file descriptor per step) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.89**, found by the TOOL-LL campaign's second port and fixed in the same change, the fix being one line. `captureStdout` ([`CodegenHs.hs:1523-1559`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) opens `oldStdout <- hDuplicate stdout` on **every step**, restores through `hDuplicateTo oldStdout stdout`, and **never closes `oldStdout`**. `writeEnd` is closed explicitly and `readEnd` is closed by `hGetContents` at EOF; the duplicate of the original stdout is reclaimed only when GC happens to finalize the `Handle`, which is not a bound. Under the **non-threaded** RTS the program then dies with `file descriptor NNNN out of range for select (0--1024)` once fd numbers pass `FD_SETSIZE`. **Measured, and the measurement is what makes this a defect rather than a theory**: `lsof` on a live run showed **139 open write handles to the program's own redirected stdout**, growing monotonically with cases (2 case-dirs → 11 fds, 29 → 353). The refute-crux port died at fd 1103 after ~51 of 80 cases; with the one-line fix the same binary runs all 80 in 71s. **Nothing in the tree had hit it**: `versiongate.llmll` takes nine steps and the driver few enough, so the first program needing four figures of steps was the first to find it. Three isolation probes are recorded in [`design/tool-rfc-002-refute-crux.md`](design/tool-rfc-002-refute-crux.md) §5: 1400 pure-stdout steps, 1400 `wasi.fs.copy` steps and 400 `wasi.proc.run` spawns each survive, so the leak is neither unconditional-per-step nor inside any one builtin, but a race between fd creation and finalization that a long console run loses | Closed by `hClose oldStdout` after the restore. **The regression test is deliberately not the obvious one**: the defect is GC-timing dependent, so a test asserting "N steps do not crash" would be flaky in the wrong direction and would pass on a broken tree whenever GC happened to keep up. `scripts/tests/test_codegen_capture_fd.py` pins the EMITTED SOURCE instead, asserting every handle `captureStdout` opens is closed on the success path. A later change that reintroduces reliance on finalization fails that check rather than failing a race |
| **CAPTURE-ENCODING-1** (a console program cannot put a non-ASCII character on its own stdout) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.90**, filed by the TOOL-LL campaign's second port and closed by the campaign's next step. **Measured end to end, and the rule was `codepoint mod 256`**: an LLMLL string literal survives the compiler intact (`→` reaches the generated Haskell as `" \8594 "` in `Lib.hs`) and the running program then writes the codepoint's LOW BYTE. `✔ ✘ ✅` (U+2714, U+2718, U+2705) emit as `14 18 05`; `→` (U+2192) emits as `c2 92`, which is the same truncation with the result re-encoded because 0x92 exceeds 0x7F. That is a latin1/char8 encoder, not a dropped character. **The defect is EMIT-ONLY, which bounds it usefully**: a probe reading a file containing `✅` and matching it with `string-contains` against a non-ASCII literal answers YES, and `string-length` returns a character count, so `wasi.fs.read` decoding and in-memory comparison are both unaffected. Only what a console program puts on stdout is corrupted. This happens although the emitted `main` calls `setLocaleEncoding utf8` and pins `utf8` on `stdin`, `stdout` and `stderr` (`CodegenHs.hs` mode preamble), and although the host locale is `en_US.UTF-8`, so neither the v0.14.86 fix nor the ambient locale is what is missing. **The suspect was the capture path and the cause is now MEASURED, not inferred: `System.Posix.IO.fdToHandle` returns a handle in BINARY mode, and binary mode is the ABSENCE of a text codec rather than a wrong one** — which is exactly why `setLocaleEncoding` could never reach it, there being nothing for the locale to inform. Probed directly: after `setLocaleEncoding utf8`, `getLocaleEncoding` answers `UTF-8` while `createPipe >>= fdToHandle >>= hGetEncoding` answers `Nothing` on BOTH ends, and `hSetEncoding h utf8` moves it to `Just UTF-8`. A binary handle writes a `Char`'s low byte and reads a byte back as latin-1, which is both halves of the observed round trip. **The v0.14.86 note in `CodegenHs.hs` claimed `setLocaleEncoding` "covers the event-log handle and the createPipe/fdToHandle pair without naming them"; the event-log half is right (`openFile` is a TEXT handle) and the pipe half was false.** `createPipe`/`fdToHandle` occur nowhere else in codegen. **Sibling of `TOOL-ENCODING-1` one level in**: that row is about handles the TOOLCHAIN owns, this one is about the generated program's own capture pipe | Closed by pinning `utf8` on both `fdToHandle` handles in `captureStdout`. **Both are required and each was ablated separately**: write end only leaves the pipe carrying correct UTF-8 for an unpinned read to decode as latin-1 (`c3 a2 c2 86 c2 92`); read end only leaves the write truncating and the UTF-8 read rejecting the lone continuation byte. **Placement is part of the fix and is also measured**, as it was for `FD-CAPTURE-1`: the write-end pin must PRECEDE `hDuplicateTo writeEnd stdout`, because the redirect copies that handle onto `stdout` — one line lower it fails with `cannot decode byte sequence starting from 146`, and it fails on the READ, so an ordering bug presents as a problem with the other handle. **The fixture was written before the fix, as the row demanded**: [`../scripts/build-smoke/capture_encoding.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/capture_encoding.llmll), gated by `build_smoke.sh` on BYTES rather than characters, since the broken output carries control bytes and a NUL that a shell `case` and a terminal each mangle differently. It reports `string-length` beside the characters so a corrupted WRITE is distinguishable from a literal that never survived the front end, and unlike the `LC_ALL=C` stage beside it this gate is **exercised on every platform**, the defect never having depended on the locale. **The one encoding fixture the repository already had could not have caught this**: `fs_encoding.llmll` uses `§` (U+00A7) throughout, and every codepoint below U+0100 is a FIXED POINT of the bug — truncate, latin-1 decode, UTF-8 encode returns it unchanged. Two source-level pins in `scripts/tests/test_codegen_capture_fd.py` fail in the toolchain-free job in milliseconds. Both workarounds are gone: [`../tools/refute-crux/refutecrux.llmll`](../tools/refute-crux/refutecrux.llmll) writes a real `→` and `refute_crux_cover.py` compares the labels instead of normalising them |
| **JSON-SCALAR-1** (a bare scalar in a JSON array has no accessor) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.91**, filed by the TOOL-LL campaign's second port, left open there because campaign §9 makes a `[CT][SPEC]` gap's SHAPE language-team's call, and closed at the WIDER of the two shapes below: four projections, `json-as-string`/`int`/`bool`/`number`, each `Json -> Result[T,string]`, shaped as the field family MINUS THE KEY. `json-array` was already a projection of that shape, so this completes a family that had one member rather than adding a new kind of operation, and the five partition `Json`. **One correction the closure forced**: the row below says `(json-get-string x "")` on a scalar *answers* `""`. It does not. `json-get` on a non-object is `err`, so the accessor is `err`; the empty string came from the CALLER, the port's `jstr` wrapping every field read in `(unwrap-or ... "")`. That strengthens the row rather than weakening it: the defect was a missing operation whose absence every call site papered over locally, which is why no gate could see it, and why the replacement is `Result`-valued. Fixture: [`json_scalar.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/json_scalar.llmll), executed by `build_smoke.sh` and mutation-checked. Original filing follows. `json-array` returns `list[Json]`, and every element accessor in `builtinEnv` (`json-get-string`, `json-get-int`, `json-get-bool`, `json-get-number`) takes a **key** and reads a FIELD OF AN OBJECT. No operation reads a scalar `Json` AS a value, so `["--strict-verified-core"]` cannot be turned back into a `string` except through `json-serialize`, which renders it **with its quotes** and leaves the caller to strip them. **Measured, and it fails silently**: `(json-get-string x "")` on a scalar answers `""`, so the refute-crux port invoked its subject with no flags at all and 50 of 80 cases failed while the 30 flagless ones passed. It type-checks, it verifies, and no gate sees it | One builtin, or three. The narrow shape is `json-as-string : Json -> Result string string`, mirroring `json-get-string`'s `Result` so a wrong-typed element is a value rather than `""`; the wider one adds `json-as-int` and `json-as-bool` and makes the family total over JSON's scalar types. **Prefer the `Result` shape over a `""`-on-failure one**, because the entire cost of this defect was that its failure was indistinguishable from an empty string. A fixture is owed with it: the in-tree population reading a scalar array element is exactly one program today |
| **PROC-MERGE-1** (`wasi.proc.run` cannot merge a child's stdout and stderr) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.91**, filed by the TOOL-LL campaign's second port and closed by the convention that **EQUAL PATH STRINGS MEAN MERGE**: one handle passed to both `std_out` and `std_err`, no signature change, so `LLMLL.md`'s parameter list does not move and only its prose column does. **The prior question this row asks for was settled by measurement and it decided the shape: ordering is NOT part of the contract and cannot be.** A merged stream's interleaving is the order the CHILD flushes, not the order it writes; probed directly, a child writing stdout first and stderr second lands stderr FIRST, stdout being block-buffered when its fd is a file. So the "byte for byte" reproduction the work column below imagined was never available to promise, and containment is what the port asserts. The equality test is **syntactic**: `"log"` and `"./log"` do not merge, canonicalizing needing a syscall against a file that does not exist yet. Soundness: equal paths previously opened TWO handles at position 0 over one file and they overwrote each other, so no working program can depend on the old behaviour. Fixture: [`proc_merge.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/proc_merge.llmll), which asserts both directions. Original filing follows. The signature takes SEPARATE stdout and stderr paths ([`TypeCheck.hs:200`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) and the body opens one handle per path ([`CodegenHs.hs:717-718`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)), so a shell's `2>&1` has no counterpart: passing the same path twice yields two write handles to one file, which is a truncation race and not a merge. **The consequence is bounded, and saying so is the point**: every criterion the refute-crux gate decides tests CONTAINMENT (`SAFE`, `error:`, `requires (import`, the localized name), so no verdict moves. What moves is the three-line excerpt printed under a failing case, which the reference takes from the merged stream and the port takes from stdout-then-stderr | Not a new builtin. Either accept a sentinel on the stderr-path argument meaning "merge into stdout", or open both and dup the second onto the first when the paths are equal, which is what a shell does. **Settle whether ordering is part of the contract first**: a merged stream's interleaving depends on the CHILD's buffering, so a port promising byte-identical excerpts would be promising something the child controls. **`[SPEC]` because the signature is specified**, `LLMLL.md:2534` giving the parameter list and naming the three trailing strings as working directory, stdout path and stderr path; any merge convention changes what that row means and lands in it |
| **REPLAY-FRAME-1** (`llmll replay` read one line per event; an event is `count('\n')+1` lines) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.83**, found by DRIVER-LL Phase 3 | A turn's wire bytes are `output ++ "\n"` ([`CodegenHs.hs:1461`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)), so an event occupies `count('\n')+1` lines; `Replay.replayOne` assumed one, an assumption wrong since the log existed. Measured on nine constructed console programs, one variable changed at a time. The worst shape is **not** a plain failure: a step whose output ends in a newline **matched** at its own event, because the reader stripped the discrepancy, then desynchronized on the unconsumed line and reported the divergence against the FOLLOWING event. An oracle that misattributes is worse than one that fails. Also fixed: `actual=` was the constant `"<no output>"`, and the settle entry's result kind was `"stdout"` carrying `""` for a turn that performs no command (RC-4), so it claimed an output never produced. Line-count alignment is **total by construction**, no case converts match to mismatch, and one test per branch pins that argument by name. **Root cause, and the reason nothing caught it: no gate anywhere ran `llmll replay`**, and the three `runReplay` unit tests passed against mock fixtures using framing the emitter does not produce, hidden by `T.strip`. `build_smoke.sh` gains that gate. Two items are deferred to `REPLAY-INJECT`: `:init` output logging, and bringing `:on-done`'s output inside the oracle, which would change shipped programs' stdout bytes. **Stated limit:** a green `llmll replay` is not evidence that `:on-done` ran. |
| **DO-ACCUM-1** (a non-final `do` step's `Command` is silently discarded) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.80 as DISCARD-1** (commit `8dff514`), re-scoped **P0-marker** by user adjudication 2026-08-02 (re-put once, from P0-error). A non-final step whose `Command` is dropped now errors unless the step carries `:discard`; the marker is rejected on a final step; `:read` retired from `def-main`. **Breaking: JSON-AST schema 0.9.0 to 0.10.0**, `$id` moved in the same commit. `LLMLL.md §9.6` records the shipped rule and the grammar carries the marker | `emitDo` ([`CodegenHs.hs:741`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) returns `(finalState, _cmdN)` and drops every earlier command. That matches `LLMLL.md:1604` ("the final result is `(lastState, lastCommand)`") and `LLMLL.md:1606`, which documents the discard as **intended** ("effects are values, not statements; sequencing them is the agent's explicit responsibility") and names the future direction as tightening "to a warn-or-error." It contradicts [`archive/do_notation/do-notation-design.md`](archive/do_notation/do-notation-design.md) §2.4, which mandates `seq-commands` composition but carries `Status: Approved — Pending Implementation` for **v0.3** and was never implemented. Two normative texts in conflict, not a regression. **Settled P0-marker, specified as DISCARD-1.** Plain P0-error was adjudicated first and re-put: `DoStep` binds only the state component (`Syntax.hs:237`; `inferDoSteps` does `withEnv [(bindName, si)]` at `TypeCheck.hs:1852-1854`), so no step can name an earlier step's `Command` and §9.6's "unless explicitly wrapped in `seq-commands`" escape hatch is unreachable, which would have made every multi-step `do` illegal with no in-language workaround. DISCARD-1 adds the marker the in-code v0.8 note always intended ("when (discard expr) provides an explicit opt-out", `TypeCheck.hs:1858-1859`): surface `[s1 <- e :discard]`, and an optional `"discard": true` on the `do-step` JSON-AST node defaulting to `false`. `checkDiscardedCommand` (`TypeCheck.hs:1860-1865`) becomes an **error gated on the marker**, plus a second rule rejecting the marker on a **final** step, whose command is performed and so cannot be truthfully marked; the `:1858-1859` deferral note is removed. `emitDo` is **not touched**: the marker has no runtime denotation, so generated Haskell is bit-identical. New syntax is a lifted-freeze case (the lifted-exclusions note under [What's NOT on this Roadmap](#whats-not-on-this-roadmap-and-why), which names "new syntax constructs") and the required soundness argument is written in §DISCARD-1, resting on the marker being erasable. **Schema question SETTLED: a minor bump, 0.9.0 to 0.10.0** (`docs/llmll-ast.schema.json:18`, `ParserJSON.hs:47`). The bump is not optional: `DoStep` carries `"additionalProperties": false` (`docs/llmll-ast.schema.json:822`), so a document carrying `discard` fails validation against 0.9.0 and the field cannot ride in unversioned. Minor rather than major on the precedent recorded at `ParserJSON.hs:41-46`, where every prior additive-optional field took a minor: DEF-RET's `return_type` (0.6.0 to 0.7.0), REC-DESCENT's `decreases` (0.7.0 to 0.8.0), SRC-CONJ-1's `pre_clauses`/`post_clauses` (0.8.0 to 0.9.0). **CI trap, same commit:** `scripts/version_gate.sh:76-84` (check C4) derives `v<major>.<minor>` from `schemaVersion` and fails unless the schema `$id` contains the matching `/schemas/vX.Y/`, so `$id` must move to `/schemas/v0.10/` in the same commit or DRIFT-CI-1 goes red. Chosen over P0-compose because under EFFECT-RESP RC-2 an auto-composing `do` discards every non-final step's response and could never consume an intermediate effect result, the shape stages E and G2 need. **Blast radius: two artifacts, not zero and not one.** (a) `compiler/test/fixtures/pair_type_test/do_emit_ac.ast.json`, a two-step JSON-AST block whose step-0 command the emitter drops; no test consumer, so it pins nothing. (b) `scripts/doc-claims/do-notation-discard-warn.llmll`, a two-step **S-expression** block shipped at v0.14.57 (`3e29beb`) and run on every CI job by `scripts/doc_claims_gate.sh` (`.github/workflows/version-gate.yml:118`); `@expect: warn:discards this intermediate command`, confirmed emitted by the v0.14.78 binary, so **any change to the warning moves DRIFT-CT-2**: under P0-marker the fixture is a `[DO-DISCARD-ERR]` positive witness and its `@expect` flips to `check-error:` on the same pinned substring in the same commit. Two prior claims are retracted: "zero do-blocks in `.llmll` sources" (this fixture is one) and "`LLMLL.md §9` has no do-notation text" (`LLMLL.md:1588` is section 9.6, "`do`-notation State Threading"; the zero-hit grep missed it because the heading writes the backticks inside the term). Reproduce with `grep -rn --include='*.llmll' -E '\(do($\|[[:space:]])'` **and** `grep -rl --include='*.json' '"kind"[[:space:]]*:[[:space:]]*"do"'`; either grep alone under-reports. Design: [`design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md`](design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md) (Rev 3, §DISCARD-1). |
| **WASI-RT** (four of the seven declared `wasi.*` builtins have no runtime) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.79** (commit `dcf21ee`), with **two named residues that this closure does not resolve**, per the row's own instruction below. **Residue (i) is now CLOSED:** the `wasi.fs.read` body no longer performs and discards; EFFECT-RESP replaced it at v0.14.80 with a real read that returns contents as `RText` and IO failure as `RErr`. **Residue (ii) is carried forward and re-filed as `CAP-1-REAL`:** the four definitions land **capability-blind**, and the measurement at v0.14.80 shows the gap is wider than "enforced at typecheck only" suggested, since the typecheck enforces namespace membership and never reads the capability clause at all. Guarded from here by **BUILD-GATE-1**, which shipped in the same commit | `builtinEnv` (`TypeCheck.hs:154-161`) declares seven `wasi.*` names. The codegen preamble (`CodegenHs.hs:394-409`) defines **three**: `wasi_io_stdout`, `wasi_io_stderr`, `wasi_http_response`. Missing: **`wasi.fs.read`, `wasi.fs.write`, `wasi.fs.delete`, `wasi.http.post`**, none of which appears anywhere in `CodegenHs.hs`. `CodegenHs.hs:75` labels the whole `wasi.*` import class "stdlib preamble handles it", which is true for three names out of seven. **The failure is not a type error, not a capability error, and not a verification refusal: it typechecks clean and dies at GHC.** Reproduced at v0.14.78 on a module whose body is `(wasi.fs.read p)`: `llmll check` returns `OK (2 statements)`, and `llmll build --emit-only` emits `(wasi_fs_read (p))` at `Lib.hs:240` against a generated preamble that defines only the three names above, which is the engineer's `Lib.hs:240:4: error [GHC-88464] Variable not in scope: wasi_fs_read :: String -> IO ()`. Corpus: **one** `wasi.fs.read` call site ([`../tools/llmll-driver/shell.llmll`](../tools/llmll-driver/shell.llmll)`:46`) and **two** `wasi.fs.write` (`shell.llmll:37`, `crux-shell-undeclared-authority.llmll:19`); those programs typecheck and cannot build. Zero in-tree `wasi.fs.delete` or `wasi.http.post` call sites. **Prerequisite of EFFECT-RESP, not parallel to it:** RC-1 delivers one response per *performed* command, and the motivating command cannot be performed at all today, so a response channel wired to it would be untestable end to end. Two questions stay **open, and the row must not be closed as if they were resolved**. (i) `wasi.fs.read : string -> Command` where `Command` is `IO ()`, so a stopgap preamble body **discards the read result**: that is a legitimate placeholder for EFFECT-RESP to build on, and it is not a real read. (ii) Capability is enforced at typecheck only (CAP-1, `TypeCheck.hs:1427-1430`; `CodegenHs.hs` contains zero `capability` references), so the four new definitions land **capability-blind**. Latent rather than red: nothing in the tree builds, so no job caught this. **The fix does not ship unguarded**: BUILD-GATE-1 was settled into this same commit on 2026-08-02, so the same patch that defines the four bodies also adds the job that observes them. Design: [`design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md`](design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md) (Rev 3), "Prerequisite found late". |
| **EFFECT-RESP** (a response channel, so a program can consume the result of its own effects) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.80** (commit `bc78057`), design settled at Rev 5. All four rules landed: RC-1 one response per performed command, RC-2 `seq-commands` discard-left, RC-3 `:init` supplies the first response, RC-4 the terminating step's command is not performed. **Breaking: console `:step` gains a third parameter** `response: Response`, guarded by the new `def-main-step-arity` check; twelve programs migrated. **Phase 1's STOP is cleared by measurement:** 151 of 151 corpus `.fq` files byte-identical against a pre-EFFECT-RESP compiler, so Σ_auto did not move. **Two residues:** RC-4 changes observable output for programs that rendered from the terminating step (the four game examples are unrepaired; `hangman_sexp` loses twelve lines), and the RC-1 bijection is exercised by a run rather than a committed fixture | `wasi.fs.read : string -> Command` (`TypeCheck.hs:158`) and `Command` is opaque, so no program can read a file and branch on it; measured, `(string-length (wasi.fs.read p))` is a type error. The effect type is a **monoid**, not an applicative: `Command` is nullary (`CodegenHs.hs:830`, `toHsType (TCustom "Command") = "IO ()"`) and `seq-commands` is its `<>` (`TypeCheck.hs:161`), so the effect structure cannot depend on any value — the applicative/monad boundary, not a readability gap. Fix is a Mealy response channel in the `def-main` harness, **not** a result-returning read builtin: that was Rev 0's proposal and it was withdrawn because it performs IO during evaluation of a pure expression, abandoning `LLMLL.md:415`, and leaves effect order dependent on an evaluation order §12 never pins. Four invariants: **RC-1** one response per performed command, delivered as the next `step`'s argument; **RC-2** `(seq-commands c1 c2)` yields `c2`'s response and discards `c1`'s, which is the sentence §13.9 does not contain; **RC-3** `:init`'s command supplies the first response, so there is no special initial case; **RC-4** `done?` is evaluated only on a state that has received a response, so the harness runs a final settle step **whose returned command is not performed**. `Response` is **compiler-supplied**, not program-declared, because the command alphabet is sealed in `builtinEnv` (`TypeCheck.hs:154-161`); arms are `RNone` / `RText string` / `RCode int` / `RErr string`, with no `RBytes` arm because `bytes[n]` needs a literal length a file read does not have (named residue). `Σ_auto` unchanged; JSON-AST unchanged; no schema bump. **The migration surface is twelve programs, not the five previously recorded**: six `.llmll` sources and six `.ast.json` documents declare `:mode console`, and no `cli` or `http` entry point exists in-tree. The `:step` arity change is breaking across all twelve with **no `check`-time diagnostic today**, because `checkStatement (SDefMain {..})` (`TypeCheck.hs:1405-1414`) does `_ <- inferExpr stepE` and discards the result, so every console program stays green at `check` and dies at GHC; the new arity check is therefore the migration's only diagnostic and must land in the same commit. The command-to-response pairing is unchecked and is a **trust-channel disclosure** closed by CMD-A, **not** by R1 — R1 overstates the machinery by a wide margin. Design: [`design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md`](design/effect-response-channel-proposal.md) (Rev 3). |
| **BUILD-GATE-1** (nothing in the tree ever runs `llmll build`) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.79** (commit `dcf21ee`), inside commit A as settled. `scripts/build_smoke.sh` runs in the `spec-roundtrip` job; acceptance was met as a positive witness, red against a pre-fix compiler with six `GHC-88464` errors and green after. Scope held: one program, not a build sweep. **Two of the three witnesses below are now covered:** (a) WASI-RT is guarded, and (b) the `:step` arity change **became guarded at v0.14.80**, when `smoke.llmll` gained a `def-main` whose step matches every `Response` arm, so the response slot, the RC-4 settle branch and the three-argument step call all pass through GHC. (c) IFACE-CONFORM remains unguarded, no FFI declaration being in the fixture. **A fourth witness is now named and unguarded:** generated user definitions carry **no type signatures** (`safe_div n d = …` for a declared `[n: int d: int]`), so GHC generalizes and a build success is not evidence that declared types are honored. This is the same mechanism the campaign recorded for the FFI seam, and it is not FFI-specific. It is what hid the console-state defect filed as `CONSOLE-INIT-1`. **AMENDED v0.14.81: the gate gained an execution stage** (`scripts/build-smoke/capproc_exec.llmll`), so a second fixture is built **and run** with its output checked against known answers, rather than the gate stopping at "it compiled". **It earned the stage on first use.** Against a missing executable, `createProcess` throws and `RErr` is correct, but two redirect handles leaked still-open and the *next* `wasi.fs.read` of that path returned `resource busy (file is locked)`: a failed spawn corrupted a later read. Fixed in the same commit with `onException` guards, pinned by `Spec.hs` `CP-17`. **No compile-only check could have observed it**, which is the general form of this row's own argument applied one level up: compiling is not running | `grep -rn 'llmll build'` across `scripts/`, `.github/`, `compiler/test/`, `tools/` and `Makefile` returns **five** hits, and every one is a comment or doc-claim prose: `scripts/doc_path_lint.py:46` and `:76` (Python comments), `scripts/doc-claims/open-after-def-verify.llmll:4` and `scripts/doc-claims/checkout-requires-astjson.llmll:4` (`@claim` text), and `compiler/test/Spec.hs:13974`, which states outright that "`llmll build`'s own `stack build` self-check never ran in outDir". `check-examples.sh` typechecks and does not build. **Zero jobs invoke it.** The justification is the pattern rather than any single defect: **three independent defects now live in this seam in one release.** (a) **WASI-RT**, four declared `wasi.*` builtins with no preamble definition: `check` green, GHC fails. (b) The **`:step` arity change** EFFECT-RESP requires, which `check` reports on **no** program because `checkStatement (SDefMain {..})` discards `inferExpr stepE`'s result (`TypeCheck.hs:1405-1414`). (c) **IFACE-CONFORM**, whose `§8.8` conformance check no production path performs; this row is its generalization, reached here without any FFI declaration. **Scope minimally: one effectful program built in CI, not a build sweep.** **Placement SETTLED 2026-08-02 by user adjudication ([`design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md`](design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md) §8.3, Rev 3): the gate ships inside commit A, the WASI-RT patch, not as a follow-on.** This overrides the engineer's `§A.6` recommendation to file it separately ([`design/driver-ll-phase01-implementation-plan.md`](design/driver-ll-phase01-implementation-plan.md)), which was right about CI hygiene and wrong about which patch is unrelated: the gate is the only observer of the defect commit A fixes, so shipping them apart ships the fix together with its blind spot and defers closing it to a row that was itself unscheduled. **Acceptance is a positive witness, and this is the part that fails quietly: the gate must be demonstrated red on commit A's branch point and green after the four preamble bodies land, with the failing GHC output recorded in the commit message.** (The branch point is whatever `main` holds when the patch opens; the intervening commits are doc-only and do not move the GHC outcome.) A gate that passes on both sides of the patch has not been shown to observe anything, which is precisely how four missing builtin definitions survived since `builtinEnv` last grew. Shape: `scripts/build_smoke.sh` runs `llmll build` on a single program exercising all four newly defined builtins, invoked from `.github/workflows/version-gate.yml` beside the existing `scripts/doc_claims_gate.sh` step. Candidate program is [`../tools/llmll-driver/shell.llmll`](../tools/llmll-driver/shell.llmll) (`wasi.fs.read` at `:46`, `wasi.fs.write` at `:37`), which as written exercises two of the four; extending it or substituting a purpose-built fixture is the engineer's call, and covering all four is not. Cost is minutes of CI on every job from the first commit of the campaign onward, stated rather than elided. Without this, WASI-RT ships with no regression guard. |
| **CAP-PROC** (the capability surface the driver's effects need) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.81, and the row CLOSES AT FIVE OPERATIONS, not six.** `wasi.fs.list` shipped v0.14.80 (commit `1036245`) with its response arm `RList list[string]`, sharing the `EFsRead` label rather than widening the closed six-label catalog. The remaining four shipped v0.14.81 (commit `a46d361`): `wasi.proc.run` (the spawn/await pair collapsed into one synchronous call, the driver having no `Popen`), `wasi.clock.monotonic`, `wasi.fs.mkdir`, and `sha256` **as `wasi.fs.sha256`, taking a path rather than bytes**. **The prediction that none would need a new arm held, and is now measured:** `mkdir` delivers `RNone`, `sha256` a hex digest as `RText`, `monotonic` nanoseconds as `RCode`, `proc.run` the child's exit status as `RCode`. **`wasi.http.get` is dropped rather than deferred**, on two independent grounds, either sufficient: its Rev 5 arm mapping is `RText`, which cannot reproduce `stage_A_intake` (the driver writes bytes and hashes the file, and an `RText` round trip is not byte-faithful); and `http-client` plus `http-client-tls` moves a generated project's dependency closure from **33 packages to 79**, none of them compiler dependencies, so CI's snapshot cache misses. Re-filed as **`HTTP-GET-1`**, which is **not on the DRIVER-LL critical path**: the campaign reaches HTTP through a granted `curl` under `wasi.proc.run`. **`wasi.proc.run` reports `unbounded` by design** and must keep doing so; a bounded label would be unsound rather than imprecise under `EffectSummary`'s may-over-approximation. **STOP condition:** an operation needing an arm that *fails* the four-part admissibility rule is a Phase 1 design error; ordinary arm growth is not | `wasi.proc.spawn` / `wasi.proc.await`, `wasi.clock.monotonic`, `wasi.fs.list` / `wasi.fs.mkdir`, `wasi.http.get`, `sha256`. Each is one `EffectLabel`, one `primEffect` clause ([`ObligationAssembly.hs:419`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/ObligationAssembly.hs)), one `builtinEnv` signature, one codegen case, and each response-bearing operation must map to an **existing** `Response` arm — needing a new one is the signal that EFFECT-RESP's arm set was wrong. The point is **bounded authority**: `primEffect` maps every `haskell.*`, `c.*` and unrecognized `wasi.*` name to ⊤ and `joinEff` makes ⊤ absorbing (`:408`), so routing these through the FFI tier makes every function above them report `unbounded`, satisfying driver-spec §15.2's letter while making the report vacuous. `wasi.clock.monotonic` is **drift, not a new feature**: documented at `LLMLL.md:1125` and `:1661` and measured as an unknown function that only warns. `wasi.proc.spawn` is the first capability that can leave the sandbox by construction, so the grant must name the executable rather than granting spawn in general; that soundness argument is required by the lifted-freeze policy (the lifted-exclusions note under [What's NOT on this Roadmap](#whats-not-on-this-roadmap-and-why)). Design: [`design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md`](design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md) §3 Phase 2. |
| **TOOL-ENCODING-1** (the toolchain's own text handles take the ambient locale) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.93**, and **this row's own premise was wrong twice.** It said "nothing in the tree exercises either half" and asked for a census; the census is now taken and the answer is **142 of 259 committed `.llmll` files hold a non-ASCII byte**, 141 in comments and 20 inside string literals. The population was never empty, only invisible, because until v0.14.93 exactly **zero** files held a non-ASCII byte in **token** position, which is the only position the lexer would have complained about. Retagged `[CT]` from `[CT][SPEC]` on the settled adjudication that this is a bug against §2 rather than a change to it: §2 gained a sentence pinning locale-independence, the invalid-UTF-8 diagnostic and BOM rejection, which states existing intent rather than moving it. The same defect class as `FS-ENCODING-1` one level up: that row is about handles a **generated program** owns, and these are handles the **toolchain** owns. Two sites, both read off the source. (i) [`Replay.hs:238-239`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Replay.hs) builds the child with `std_in = CreatePipe, std_out = CreatePipe` and never calls `hSetEncoding`, so `createProcess` returns handles whose codec came from `getLocaleEncoding`; `llmll replay` of a program that prints non-ASCII would fail under a POSIX locale the same way a generated program did at v0.14.84, and every replay fixture in the tree is ASCII, so nothing would notice. (ii) the compiler reads `.llmll` source through `TIO.readFile` ([`Main.hs:447`](../compiler/app/Main.hs), also `:684` and `:836`), which decodes through the locale, while `LLMLL.md` §2 states that source files **are** UTF-8: that is spec/code drift, not only an inconvenience. The `.json` input path is unaffected, reading bytes through `BL.readFile`. **The workaround is already shipped, and is the evidence that the second half bites**: [`Dockerfile`](../Dockerfile) sets `LANG=C.UTF-8` / `LC_ALL=C.UTF-8`, a repair applied at the container instead of at the handle. **THE SECOND HALF IS NOW MEASURED ON LINUX, AND THE CENSUS THIS ROW ASKED FOR IS TAKEN.** Both were done by TOOL-RFC-003's differential cover, which scrubs the environment so its two implementations are asked the same question and therefore hands the compiler no locale at all. On Linux CI every one of the 15 fixtures in [`scripts/doc-claims/`](../scripts/doc-claims) then failed with `hGetContents: invalid argument (cannot decode byte sequence starting from 194)`, 194 being `0xC2`, a UTF-8 lead byte. **The census: 15 of 15 hold non-ASCII bytes** (`§` and `—`, in their `@doc` and `@claim` headers), so the population this row called unmeasured is not merely non-empty, it is total for that directory. The failure is in the SUBJECT and not in either implementation, and the cover says so precisely: cells 1-13 all AGREED, both sides failing identically, and what reddened was the three negative controls, which require both to PASS an unmutated tree. **On macOS none of this reproduces**, v0.14.86's finding holding: the same cover scored 17/17 locally with no locale set. Pinned back to `C.UTF-8` on both sides of the cover as a workaround pre-marked for removal when this row closes. **THE PIN IS NOW REMOVED AND THAT REMOVAL WAS THE ACCEPTANCE CRITERION**, not a tidy-up: with no locale set, `doc_claims_cover.py` is the only gate that fails if the compiler ever again decodes source through the environment, and it scored 17 cells and 3 negative controls on Linux CI at v0.14.93. Recorded by the `FS-ENCODING-1` completion at v0.14.86 (`f001394`) and deliberately not taken there | **DONE in v0.14.93.** Seven sites, not the two this column predicted: `Main.hs`'s three source reads, `Module.hs`'s `parseFile` (the site governing import resolution, which this row had missed), `HubQuery.hs`'s, and both `Replay.hs` child pipe handles. Original plan, kept because the shape held: two edits and a gate, in that order. Pin `utf8` on the two `CreatePipe` handles at the `createProcess` call site; a per-handle pin is sufficient **here** where it was not in the generated program, because the compiler has no `captureStdout` duplicate pair to rebuild the codec behind it. Decode `.llmll` source explicitly, which means reading bytes and decoding rather than `TIO.readFile`, and deciding what an invalid byte sequence reports (a parse diagnostic naming the offset, not a GHC exception escaping to the top level). Then the gate, which is the part with no precedent: the population that would exercise either half is empty because every fixture in the tree is ASCII, so a non-ASCII fixture has to exist before either fix can be shown to do anything, and it has to run on Linux CI to mean anything. Measure the corpus first, as `RESERVED-NAME-1` also owes: whether any committed `.llmll` file holds a non-ASCII byte is a one-line question nobody has asked. **Answered above, and the lesson generalizes to `RESERVED-NAME-1`: the question was not "is the population empty" but "is the population in a position anything checks".** 142 of 259 files carried the bytes all along and none of them could fire, because every one sat in a comment or a string literal. The gate that closes this row is the sixteenth doc-claim fixture, [`unicode-alias-token.llmll`](../scripts/doc-claims/unicode-alias-token.llmll), which is the tree's only non-ASCII **token**. |
| **JSON-1** (a sealed `Json` builtin, `def-shell`-only) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.82** (`c3681b0`), at **fourteen** builtins, not the three this row sketched. The "18 JSON call sites" figure below counts only lines matching `json.` and misses the 25 `read_json` / 14 `write_json` helper sites; the union is **56 lines**, 212 field reads, 64 distinct keys. The `list[a]` justification below is also false and is corrected in the design doc: lists DO enter body-faithful VCs, as an opaque sort with an uninterpreted projection, and what is firewalled is list *element* reasoning. Design: [`design/native-json-proposal.md`](design/native-json-proposal.md) (Rev 3, SETTLED; §8 carries an eleven-row deferred register for native JSON, §9 five routed findings) | The RFC-SWARM driver has 18 JSON call sites and LLMLL has no JSON and no dynamic type. `json-parse : string -> Result[Json, string]`, `json-serialize : Json -> string`, typed `Result`-returning accessors. `Json` is recursive, which is Lever C for verification and irrelevant here because it is **sealed and opaque**: it never enters a body-faithful VC, exactly as `list[a]` does not today. Rejected alternative: `haskell.aeson` through the FFI tier, which collapses authority to ⊤ and carries a signature nothing checks against the Haskell one. Design: [`design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md`](design/driver-in-llmll-campaign.md) §3 Phase 2. |
| **SIDECAR-REGEN-1** (committed `.verified.json` files stale against `checker_soundness_version`) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.82** | At least one committed sidecar (`examples/banking_ledger/banking.llmll.verified.json`) predates the shipped `checker_soundness_version` field; re-verifying adds it with every `verified_hash` unchanged. Swept at v0.14.82: **four** sidecars regenerated (`banking_ledger`, `erc20_token`, and both `heartbleed/secure-channel` files). The change is purely additive, `checker_soundness_version` gained and **zero verdict values moved**, which is also the cleanest available evidence that JSON-1 did not reach the verification surface. |
| **FACT-AG-LEN** (earn the `bytes[n]` length instead of asserting it from a declaration) `[CT]` | **ALL THREE STAGES SHIPPED, line CLOSED** (Stage 1 v0.14.76, Stage 2 v0.14.77, Stage 3 v0.14.78) | The general form of what WILD-ASSUME approximates: no fact derived from a *type* enters a VC antecedent unless an obligation discharges it. It lands **not** on `consumed_guarantees` but on the refinement channel LLMLL already uses for the §3.4.1 introduction rule, by elaborating `bytes[n]` to `{v : bytes[n] \| (= (bytes-length v) n)}` where `augmentContractPre` / `augmentContractPost` read. **Three stages, and the ordering is a correctness constraint, not a preference.** **Stage 1 (SHIPPED v0.14.76)**: parameter position. `bytesLenParamPre` conjoins the length into the effective pre, so §5.3.4's call rule proves it at each call site and the callee assumes it; `emitParamBind` keeps `byteArraySort` and drops only the predicate, since deleting the clause falls to `typeToSort`'s `FQInt` default and emits ill-sorted `bytesLen`; the LEVER-A1 gate self-activates, because `bytes-length` is in `bytesOpNames` and `arrGateActive` reads the **augmented** contract, so `typeToSort` needs no change and the review's proposed widening (which would array-sort `Result` and ADT components) was rejected on that measurement. **Stage 2 (SHIPPED v0.14.77)**: the `bytes-zero` constructor axiom. `(bytes-zero)` translates to `Map_default(0)`, a total function in the array theory carrying no length, so `bytesLen` applied to it is uninterpreted and the length came entirely from `resultLenFact` on the constraint LHS; a new `bodyToPredM` equation now emits `r = Map_default(0)` and `bytesLen(r) = n` as a `CallVC` with `cvPreObligation = Nothing`, ASSUME polarity with no PROVE side, which is what an axiom is, with `bytes-set`'s preservation fact as the shipped precedent for the shape. `reifyBytesZeroLen` supplies `n` by rewriting a whole-body `(bytes-zero)` to carry it as a literal argument at the `body'` seam, because `bodyToPredM` receives neither an `AliasMap` nor an `mRet`; the match is head-syntactic on `TBytes n`, matching the checker's determining-context rule (`TypeCheck.hs:1216`, `:1250`) and codegen (`CodegenHs.hs:586`) rather than chasing aliases. The axiom's validity is a **trust-channel dependency** on codegen faithfulness under the `codegen_semantics_version` stamp, not a contract discharge (see TRUST-AXIOM). **Rev 2's claim that the bytes algebra closes at Stage 2 is false, and Rev 3 retracts it**: the constructor and preservation lemmas cannot meet inside one body, since `(bytes-set (bytes-zero) 0 1)` is a type error, so every real composition crosses a function boundary and the length reaches the caller only through the callee's effective post. Closure is **Stage 3's**. **Stage 3 (SHIPPED v0.14.78)**: return position, and the line closes here. `bytesLenRetPost` is the symmetric twin of `bytesLenParamPre`, conjoined into `returnRefinementPost` so `augmentContractPost` folds the length into the effective post; `resultLenFact` is deleted from `lhsPred`, so a body proves its own result length and callers recover it as an assumed post through the existing assume-guarantee step; `bytesLenReft` is deleted with it, its sort role having moved into `emitParamBind` at Stage 1. The ordering constraint held, since without the constructor axiom the body VC for a `(bytes-zero)`-bodied def is UNSAT. Two implementation findings changed the settled design. **`admits` and the WILD-ASSUME seams had to split**, because "`admits` loses `bytesLenOf`" and "both seams kept" are jointly unsatisfiable while the seams read one predicate: `admits` is now the soundness set (`boolValuedMapTy` alone) and a new exported `wildAssumeRejects` is the diagnostic set (`admits` disjoined with the bytes arm), with containment holding by construction; there is also a **third** `admits` call site (`unify`, a router after `compatibleWith` has rejected) where the design named two. **Stage 3 empties a solver-crash class no revision predicted**: `calleeRetSort` sorts a bytes callee's result binder array-wise only when the callee's *stored contract* mentions a bytes op, so a **contract-free** bytes-returning callee bound its call result at `FQInt` and `bytesLen` over it crashed liquid-fixpoint on a sort error rather than returning a verdict; every `bytes[n]` return now carries `bytes-length` in its augmented post, so the population is empty and the `FQInt` fall-through on the `TBytes` arm is dead (named in place). The payoff is the composition the line existed to enable: **a contract-free `bytes[n]`-returning function is now body-faithful and `verified`**. **Residues, named rather than implied.** The class is **not** closed for *recursive* `bytes[n]`-returning functions, whose post stays on the assumption channel at tier `asserted` (`letrec` cannot reach the class at all, both parsers hardcoding `SLetrec`'s return type to `Nothing`); and the identity function on bytes still cannot prove its own length, because `bodyToPredM`'s variable clause admits no array-sorted variable, measured identical at Stage 2 and therefore a pre-existing body-VC fragment boundary rather than a Stage 3 regression. Gate: corpus **252 files, 250 verdict-identical**, the two changes being the two new fixtures, both `SOLVER-CRASH` at Stage 2, with no pre-existing file moving; `examples/` held no bytes-composition program before this change, so the sweep is a regression check and the real witness set is those fixtures plus `Spec.hs`. No `checker_soundness_version` bump: the evidence hash already folds `augmentContractPost` on both write (`Main.hs:1531`) and read (`TrustReport.hs:586`), so `downgradeStaleVerifiedSidecar` identifies the affected population per function, which is what SAFE-ARG could not do when it spent the stamp. **Rev 2's population for that argument was false on both halves, and Rev 3 corrects it**: measured under the counterfactual build, `examples/` contained **zero** `bytes-zero` occurrences (so `examples/bytes-bounds/` was never at risk), and a `bytes-set`-returning function survives, because the preservation lemma composes with the parameter length Stage 1 earned into the effective pre. The affected population was the `bytes-zero`-bodied programs in `compiler/test/Spec.hs` only. Stage 3 moved the evidence hash of every bytes-returning function, as predicted; `examples/bytes-bounds/zero-buffer.llmll` (new at v0.14.77) is the construction-class artifact that revalidation ran against, and before it there was none anywhere in the tree. **The `map[k,bool]` range arm is CLOSED, not deferred, and no peer row should be opened for it**: its establish-half is discharged by the sealed `map-empty` / `map-put` constructors, and the fact is uniform over the type constructor's inhabitants rather than parametric in an index, so there is nothing to earn and nothing to export. Design-reference precedent: F\*'s length-indexed buffers discharge the length equality at the call site, and Dafny keeps length at the term level (`a.Length`) so no type-level lie is expressible. Design: [`design/fact-ag-proposal.md`](design/fact-ag-proposal.md) (Rev 4, SETTLED and COMPLETE), whose professor review is folded into its review-log appendix and archived to [`archive/professor-reviews/fact-ag-proposal-review.md`](archive/professor-reviews/fact-ag-proposal-review.md). Tests A1-1 / A1-7 / A1-8 re-pointed, A1-4 extended, A1-10 through A1-12 new at Stage 2, and Stage 3 adds twelve more across the post augmentation, the `admits` / `wildAssumeRejects` split, and the contract-free call-site sort. |
| **ADMIT-SHARED** (one type-admissibility predicate shared by the checker and the emitter) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.75**; the bytes arm was **removed at FACT-AG-LEN Stage 3 (v0.14.78)** and the provisional status is **resolved**: `boolValuedMapTy` is where `admits` lands, not where it pauses | The generalization of CR-01. `TypeCheck.assumesFact` was a hand-written mirror of `FixpointEmit`'s fact-injection gates, and CR-01 was the two copies disagreeing about `TDependent`; a second instance was already queued at component positions, since `assumesFactBoolValue` had no `TCustom` clause and is quiet end-to-end because `expandAlias` is congruent. New leaf module `LLMLL.TypeAdmissibility` holds the alias normalization plus `admits`, **defined over** `bytesLenOf` and `boolValuedMapTy` rather than mirroring them. **ADMIT-OVER** is the invariant that generalizes: `admits` must over-approximate the emitter's injection set, one-directionally, and the question for a new arm is never "do the two sides agree" but "is `admits` still a superset". **The invariant now has two predicates rather than one, and only the first is what ADMIT-OVER is about** (v0.14.78): `admits` is the SOUNDNESS set, exactly what the emitter injects unearned, and `wildAssumeRejects` is the DIAGNOSTIC set, `admits` disjoined with the bytes arm, read by the two `TypeCheck` WILD-ASSUME seams and by `unify`'s message router. They had to split because dropping the bytes arm from a shared predicate makes the seams stop firing, so removing the arm and keeping the seams were jointly unsatisfiable as one predicate. Containment (`admits` implies `wildAssumeRejects`) holds by construction, since the second is defined from the first, not as an asserted property over the two. A new arm therefore asks two questions: does the emitter inject it unearned (then `admits`), and is a type error at the seam a better message than the downstream verdict (then `wildAssumeRejects` alone). Acceptance criterion ships as **two** properties — A1 congruence closure (bite entirely at component positions) and A2 expansion equivalence — after measurement showed a general `Type` generator **misses** the CR-01 sibling a component-position generator catches. `Module.compatibleTy` needed no change: measured test-only reachable. Note the incentive this creates: `admits` is a denial list made cheap to extend. Its principled terminal state is **`boolValuedMapTy`**, not empty, and the module header's original claim that it was the EMPTY predicate is withdrawn in the code at v0.14.78. FACT-AG-LEN Stage 3 removed the bytes arm; the `map[k,bool]` arm stays, because its fact is established by the sealed constructors and is uniform over the type's inhabitants rather than parametric in an index (see FACT-AG-LEN). Design: [`design/finding-arg-position-false-safe.md`](design/finding-arg-position-false-safe.md) (Rev 3–4). Tests ADM-1..4, SA-18/18b, SA-19/19b, three properties. |
| **SAFE-ARG** (a `bytes[n]` length fact asserted from an unvalidated declaration proved an out-of-bounds read safe) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.73** | The first defect on this line that did **not** fail closed. `compatibleWith`'s bare-wildcard clause let a `bytes[32]` reach a `bytes[64]` parameter through one unannotated hop; `bytesLenReft` then asserted `bytesLen(b) = n` from the declared parameter type and index-in-bounds discharged against it, so the run reported `SAFE` and persisted a `verified` sidecar for a read past the end of the buffer. Codegen's own guard (`CodegenHs.hs:275`) traps at runtime, so the generated program does not read out of bounds; what was falsely certified is the obligation. Affected **v0.14.34..v0.14.72**, coeval with the array class (`c4ad7b6`, LEVER-A1): `bytesLen` does not occur in `FixpointEmit.hs` at `c4ad7b6^`. Fix = **WILD-ASSUME**: a bare inference wildcard — and its `freshenFnType` instances, `?$N`, which is why exact equality with `TVar "?"` left the first implementation completely dead — no longer satisfies a type that asserts a fact no obligation discharges. Two seams (`structuralUnify` for arguments, the live path; `compatibleWith` for returns and `checkExpr`), actual side only. `.verified.json` gains `checker_soundness_version`, stamped unconditionally so absence is a sound stale signal, and sidecars in the affected range revalidate once. Stage 1 is bytes-only; the `map[k,bool]` value-range arm is a member of the same class with no reaching-SAFE witness and is deferred pending a `(map-empty)` over-breadth fixture. Refinement aliases and nullary enums are measured non-members (their type-level data is an obligation on the producer, not an assumption). Design: [`design/finding-arg-position-false-safe.md`](design/finding-arg-position-false-safe.md) (Rev 2). Tests SA-1..7, SS-1..4; corpus sweep over 151 files shows zero WILD-ASSUME rejections. |
| **MATCH-NULLARY-1** (bare nullary constructor in a `match` arm verified unsoundly) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.66** | `TypeCheck.checkPatternExpanded` hard-errors when a match-arm pattern is a bare `PVar` naming a constructor of the scrutinee's type (user sums and `Result`), with the correct `((A) ...)` form in the message; `LLMLL.md` §3.4 states the rule. Was a **soundness** defect: the bare arm parsed as a catch-all binder (`Parser.hs` `PVar <$> pIdent`), the verifier reasoned about that reading while codegen emitted a real constructor pattern, and a **false SAFE under `--strict-verified-core`** was reachable (the built program's own post-assertion fired on the input the proof covered). Blast radius was zero (0 hits across 127 in-tree sources and 1526 committed ASTs). Analysis: [`design/finding-match-nullary-ctor-unsound.md`](design/finding-match-nullary-ctor-unsound.md); fixtures `compiler/test/fixtures/match-nullary/`; tests MN-1..MN-6. Unblocked the RFC-SWARM wave, where `--strict-verified-core` is the per-fill acceptance bar. |
| **FQ-CTOR-COLLIDE-1** (a binder named like a lowercased ADT constructor crashed the solver) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.67** | User (uppercase-initial) constructor symbols emit through a single new `FixpointIR.fqCtorSym` with a reserved `ctor_` prefix (`Denied` → `ctor_denied`, selectors `ctor_denied_0`); built-in lowercase symbols (`ok`/`err`/`pair2`) stay verbatim. The declaration site and all four translation sites route through that one function, which is what was missing: the convention previously lived only in a comment asking the sites to agree. No type qualification needed, since the typechecker already rejects duplicate constructor names across type definitions. **Failed closed throughout** (a crash, never a false SAFE), so no prior verdict was affected; it cost agents retries against an error naming a type their function need not mention. Analysis: [`design/finding-fq-ctor-name-collision.md`](design/finding-fq-ctor-name-collision.md); fixtures `compiler/test/fixtures/fq-ctor-collide/`; tests FQCOLL-1..4. `.fq` byte-change: constructor symbols in files containing user sum types now carry the prefix. |
| **FQ-RESULT-SORT-1** (the `result` binder's sort defaults to `int` when the return type is unannotated) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.72; residual closed by RET-BRANCH-PREF** | Routed as **FQ-BOOL-SORT-1**; that tag is retained as an alias because [`../experiments/rfc-swarm/runs/rfc4648/RESULTS.md`](../experiments/rfc-swarm/runs/rfc4648/RESULTS.md) §"Compiler bug found in passing" is append-only and cites it. The contract channel derives `result`'s sort from the *optional* `-> RetType` annotation and falls back to `FQInt`, while the type channel derives it from the synthesized body type (`TypeCheck.hs:992`, `fromMaybe bodyType mRet`). Where they disagree the emitted `.fq` is ill-sorted and liquid-fixpoint crashes. **Not bool-specific**: string returns crash (`The sort Str is not numeric`) and pair returns crash (`The sort (Pair2 …) is not numeric`, because `sigPairUnsafe` computes `maybe False …` and so fails open on the input that needs it). **Not literal-specific**: a *computed* bool body crashes whenever the post eliminates the boolean, e.g. `(post (and result (> n 0)))`, `(or …)`, `(not result)`; the surviving shape is `(= result e)` alone. Crash path is `retSort` at `FixpointEmit.hs:1156`, consumed at `:1207` and `:1220-1221`; `:1101-1102` is the map-return-chain branch and is **not** on it; `:765` mis-sorts a qualifier, which is inert because no `wf` constraints or KVars are emitted (`FixpointIR.hs:205-211`). Six-line repro: `(def f [n: int] (pre (> n 0)) (post (not (= n 10))) true)`. **Workaround: annotate the return type.** **Fails closed** for the shapes in this row (exit 1, a crash), so no prior verdict is affected by *this* defect. That is a property of these shapes and **not** of the array class generally: SAFE-ARG (v0.14.73) records a false SAFE reached through the `bytes[n]` binder-fact path, so "never a false SAFE" must not be read as a claim about `Σ_auto` at large. Blast radius zero in tree: 29 unannotated contracted `def` heads, all returning `int`. Shipped as two stages against [`design/finding-fq-result-sort-default.md`](design/finding-fq-result-sort-default.md) (Rev 3): **(a)** the checker records tau_ret = mRet |> tau_body on `TCState` and the contract channel consumes it through `effRet` at the `emitFnConstraints` boundary, which also makes `sigPairUnsafe`/`resultReturnUnsafe` stop failing open; **(b)** `meRetTypes` on `ModuleEnv` carries it cross-module, since `seedImportedContracts` rebuilds an imported ContractEnv from statements rather than from `meContracts`, plus `emitSynthetic` for the three synthetic emit paths. The type environment (`collectTopLevel`, `toExport`) is deliberately untouched: routing tau_ret there makes pass 1 of `checkStatements` depend on pass 2. **A third stage (HOLE-RET) was implemented and withdrawn**: firing on a wildcard tau_ret demoted 12 corpus functions from `verified` to `asserted`, because a wildcard is idiomatic (104 unannotated def heads corpus-wide, 72 alongside another unannotated callee) rather than a signal of unsortability. **Accepted residual**: an unannotated contracted *recursive* def whose recursive call sits in `then` position still crashes (`inferExpr (EIf)` returns `thenType`); zero corpus instances, fails closed, one-token workaround. The principled fix is RET-BRANCH-PREF, routed separately because it makes the type channel stricter. Tests FQRS-1..9; gates were 1424 examples/0 failures, 128 `.fq` byte-identical vs a rebuilt pre-change compiler, CDP invariance across 11 axis rows. |
| **CONTRACT-READ-LINT** (lint for contract-position partial reads) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED (bytes literal slice)** | Per the settled [`contract-position-reads-disposition.md`](design/contract-position-reads-disposition.md) disposition (c): a non-blocking `contract-read-oob` warning (JSON-visible, `diagKind`) fires on the decidable slice — a literal index against a literal `bytes[n]` bound in a `pre`/`post`, e.g. `(bytes-get b 9)` where `b : bytes[8]` (`TypeCheck.lintContractReads`; `mkContractReadOOBWarning`). Syntactic, no solver; hooks `SDef`/`SDefShell`/`SDefLogic`. Deferred: the `map-get`-without-`map-has` heuristic tier (disposition (c), "may ship later") and the Dafny-style well-formedness side-obligation (disposition (b)). |
| **OBLIG-PBT-5b** (`tested-joint` display level) | **SHIPPED** | A real `DLTestedJoint` lattice tier — below `tested`, above `asserted`, incomparable to `contract-checked` — replacing 5a's demote-to-asserted. Joint-only `DLTested` post clauses are reclassified to `DLTestedJoint` **before** the transitive meet (`reclassifyJointCS` in `TrustReport`), so the tier **propagates to callers** via `evidenceMeet`. Additive `tested_joint` count in `summary` + each `tier_profile`; `tested-joint` label; `trust_report_version` 1.5.0 → 1.6.0. `EvidenceRecord.scope` was **not** added — joint-ness is derived at report-build from shared witness hashes (`computeJointHashes`), avoiding a persisted field. EVID-0 lattice laws (commutativity/associativity) extended and green (125 triples). |
