| **CI-BUILD-TEST-1** (`stack test` has never run in CI) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.94** (`bc7107d`). The example suite runs in `spec-roundtrip` under `--fail-on=pending`, ordered before the refute-crux gate. **The flag and the position are both part of the fix rather than preferences:** 55 of the examples call `pendingWith` when `fixpoint` is absent, and hspec scores pending as non-failure, so a solver-free job would have printed the same `1679 examples, 0 failures` headline and the same exit 0 while skipping all 55. `--fail-on=pending` turns that into `1679 examples, 55 failures` and exit 1, verified both ways. **The one figure this row could not supply is now measured**, the suite's own first CI run being what measured it: about **4m07s** including building the test component, which `stack build llmll` does not build, against a 203s local estimate. So the cold-cache third-job option rejected during design stays rejected. Filed at v0.14.93 carrying **1675** examples and **53** pending; both figures were already stale when the fix merged, `FEAS-MIN-1` having added four examples of which two need a solver. The column at right is preserved **as filed**, including its request for a `compiler-engineer` feasibility read, which `bc7107d` is | **Owed: a `compiler-engineer` feasibility read, and this column deliberately does not pre-empt it.** Whether the example suite belongs in CI, in which job, and at what cost is an engineering call, and the row was filed by a documentation pass, which has no standing to make one. Two measured inputs it can start from, and nothing beyond them: `spec-roundtrip` already builds `llmll` and the build is not free (the job's own history puts the port step at ~5 min and `Build liquid-fixpoint` at 6.0 min when the cache misses, per the CI-image note in the TOOL-LL restart record's debt section), and `stack test` at this tree is 1675 examples whose wall-clock nobody has recorded in CI conditions because it has never run there. When the shape is settled, `version-gate.yml`'s header sentence calling build/test CI "a separate ticket" updates to cite this row, so the next reader finds a decision rather than a deferral |
| **REGEX-LOWER-1** (`regex-match` typechecks and verifies and then does not build) `[CT]` | **SHIPPED v0.14.96, and the census it demanded corrected the row itself.** `isOperator` ([`Parser.hs:940-947`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/Parser.hs)) named two things `emitOp` cannot lower. A name there parses to an `EOp` and reaches emitOp's fallback, which intercalates the operator name LITERALLY between its arguments and is correct exactly for names already valid Haskell infix, so `(regex-match p s)` emitted `(p regex-match s)`, GHC lexed the hyphen as subtraction, and the preamble's own `regex_match` binding was referenced by nothing for the builtin's whole life. **Fixed by the narrower of the two shapes this row offered**: the names are REMOVED from `isOperator` rather than given `emitOp` cases, so no second list remains to drift from the first. `regex-match` now parses as an `EApp`, reaches `emitApp`'s default and is mangled by `toHsIdent` into `regex_match`, the preamble binding's exact name; **no codegen was written**. Admissibility is preserved by [`TypeCheck.hs:956`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs), whose test is `trustedPrelude \|\| Map.member func builtinEnv`. The refinement position was measured on both sides and is unchanged: a `(where [s: string] (regex-match ...))` refinement verifies SAFE with `body-fallback` and builds clean, refinements being erased at codegen. **THE ROW'S REMAINING CLAIM WAS WRONG, for the second time and in the same way.** It grouped `regex-match` with `is-valid?` as the unmeasured pair it still owed; executed, they are DIFFERENT CLASSES and the discriminator is `builtinEnv` membership, which this row already stated for the six aliases. `is-valid?` is in no `builtinEnv`, never reached `emitOp`, and was a **phantom**: named at `Parser.hs:943` and nowhere else in the compiler, with no type, no preamble, no spec entry, no callers and a firing population of zero across 261 committed `.llmll` files. So this row's population reaching the fallback was exactly ONE name, and `is-valid?` belonged to `ALIAS-LOWER-1`'s class throughout. Removing it moves `check` from exit 0 with a warning to **exit 1** through strict-core admissibility, on a program that could never have worked | **The gate had no precedent, and its negative control caught a cell of ours wrong by construction.** The defect is downstream of every check-time gate in this repository and `check` reports OK on it, so a `scripts/doc-claims` fixture would have been green on both sides of the fix and graded nothing; [`regex_lower.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/regex_lower.llmll) therefore lives in BUILD-GATE-1, which compiles through GHC, and exercises both argument positions because the fallback was insensitive to which was which. Run against a deliberately reverted parser the gate exits 1 while the smoke stage still passes, so it discriminates. It failed at the BUILD assertion, so its two grep assertions never executed, and checked directly against the broken emission one of them could not fire: it matched `[a-z_]+ regex-match `, modelled on `(p regex-match s)`, while the fixture's literal-pattern def emits `("^[a-f0-9]{64}$" regex-match s)` whose preceding character is a quote, so it missed the very call it was written for. Measured on both sides instead: a correct build holds ZERO hyphenated occurrences, so the assertion tests for zero. That is TOOL-RFC-004 defect 3's class, one release later, in our own cell. Tests 1679 to 1683 |
| **FS-WALK-1** (`wasi.fs.list` is flat; there is no recursive directory walk) `[CT]` | **CLOSED as COSMETIC 2026-08-10, by the measurement the row itself asked for, and NO compiler work follows.** `wasi.fs.list : string -> Command` ([`TypeCheck.hs:183`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)) answers one directory's entries as `RList` and has no recursive variant. That stays true and is no longer a gap for any gate in scope. **The row asked whether [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh)'s walk sites need arbitrary depth or a known one. Measured: there are TWELVE, not the nine the row claimed**, all `find`, zero globstar, zero directory globs, and all twelve are one query, locating one executable by name under a `.stack-work/install` tree. **That tree is depth 4 on both platforms in scope**: `aarch64-osx/<hash>/9.6.6/bin` on macOS from the live tree, and `x86_64-linux-tinfo6/<hash>/9.6.6/bin` on Linux **read out of the log of green run `31439956284`** rather than inferred from Stack's documentation. Only the component NAMES vary. So the requirement is *enumerate at known depth with unknown names*, which composes from flat `wasi.fs.list` calls, and port 006 needs no builtin. **The row's framing had also expired**: it was filed "not because it blocks the next port", true on 2026-08-07 when 003 was next and false once 006 became next. Both halves are now moot. The disposition matches what [`tool-rfc-002`](design/tool-rfc-002-refute-crux.md), [`003`](design/tool-rfc-003-doc-claims.md), [`004`](design/tool-rfc-004-doc-archive.md) and [`005`](design/tool-rfc-005-doc-path-lint.md) had each already recorded for their own gate | **Closed, and SUPERSEDED IN PART on 2026-08-11: the twelve walk sites no longer exist.** This row closed on the reading that port 006 would compose flat `wasi.fs.list` calls at depth 4. Measuring the selection then showed the *search itself* was the defect, not its depth: with more than one install root, `find | head -1` is unspecified and a sorted pick is deterministically stale (this repo's compiler tree yields `llmll 0.14.96` by readdir order and `llmll 0.13.0` sorted). [`build_smoke.sh`](../scripts/build_smoke.sh) now asks `stack path --local-install-root` through one `exe_path` helper, so there is no enumeration to port. **Port 006 copies that**, reachable from LLMLL because `wasi.proc.run` carries a `cwd`; an mtime pick is not portable and is `FS-STAT-1`. The close stands, because no builtin is owed either way.** **The general capability was examined too, and it needs no builtin either**: `Rc = Json` already admits a worklist through `json-of-list` / `json-array` ([`TypeCheck.hs:340`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs), `:379`, five in-tree users), and `wasi.fs.list` answers `RErr` on a file ([`CodegenHs.hs:503-506`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs)) so the response arm discriminates kind. **Three prerequisites are recorded for anyone who revisits it, and none is a depth bound**: a symlink policy, since `listDirectory` follows a symlink to a directory so a cycle diverges and no step budget repairs that (see `LIST-KIND-1`, which now carries this as its second cause); an explicit acyclicity assumption in the trust channel, the walk being well-founded only relative to it; and a `[Json]` queue rather than the newline-delimited string [`docarchive.llmll`](../tools/doc-archive/docarchive.llmll) uses, since `TypeCheck.hs:251-255` names a newline in a filename as the reason `RList` exists at all. **The second half of the original question, what bounds an unbounded worklist, is deliberately NOT a row**: its diagnostic half is settled and disclosed at [`design/proc-boundary-1-proposal.md`](design/proc-boundary-1-proposal.md) §4.5, and its remainder is evidence under `MODE-CLI-1`. Settled by language-team 2026-08-10 after professor review |
| **LIST-RANGE-1** (nothing constructs a list from a count) `[CT]` | **REFUTED and CLOSED 2026-08-16, and NO compiler work follows.** The builtin this row called absent has existed since v0.11. `range : (int, int) -> list[int]` is a `builtinEnv` entry at [`TypeCheck.hs:133`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs) with a real body at [`CodegenHs.hs:312-313`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/CodegenHs.hs), `range from to = [from .. to - 1]`, marked LT-INT and dated v0.11, and [`LLMLL.md`](../LLMLL.md) §13.5 documents it in the builtin table. **PROBED 2026-08-16 against `llmll 0.16.0` on aarch64-osx, by a program that was built, was run, and printed what the runtime answered.** `(range 1 6)` gives `1,2,3,4,5`, so the interval is half-open. `(range 1 61)` gives 60 elements ending `58,59,60`. `(range 3 3)` gives 0 elements. `(range 9 4)`, inverted, gives 0 elements and does not crash. `(list-map (range 1 13) int-to-string)` gives 12 DISTINCT strings, which is exactly what this row said a program cannot produce. **The surviving witness is refuted with the row.** After the 2026-08-15 narrowing the claim rested on `v-feed-text` in [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll), which builds 60 lines from a literal 60-element int list and whose comment states the gap as fact; `(range 1 61)` replaces that literal, and a sibling correction is making that change to the code and to the comment. **Fifteen call sites were in the tree the whole time**, across five files, among them the TOOL-RFC-003 port [`docclaims.llmll`](../tools/doc-claims/docclaims.llmll) and [`sequencer.llmll`](../tools/llmll-driver/sequencer.llmll), so the campaign's own programs were already calling the name this row reported missing. **The class is what this record is for.** Filed 2026-08-15, narrowed the same day by the stage it was filed against, and never real; one search of `builtinEnv` would have settled it at filing time, which is the correction at [`design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md`](design/llmll-tooling-campaign.md) §5, read the builtin list and check whether the operation COMPOSES from what is there before filing an absence, sitting about fifteen lines from where that same section discusses this row. This is the second row the campaign has carried whose disposition one cheap measurement settles, `FS-WALK-1` being the first. **Original filing follows, preserved so the claim survives the correction.** **OPEN**, filed 2026-08-15 by TOOL-RFC-006's port while scoping its PROC-STDIN-1 stage. **Every list in LLMLL today is a literal or is derived from a list that already exists**: `string-split`, `wasi.fs.list`, `wasi.proc.args` and `json-array` all take something and give back a sequence. Nothing turns the integer `12000` into 12000 elements. `list-map` and `list-fold` consume a list and `int-to-string` exists ([`TypeCheck.hs`](../compiler/src/LLMLL/TypeCheck.hs)), so the only absent piece is the generator. **The consequence is NARROWER than this row first claimed, and the correction is a measurement rather than an argument.** The row was filed on 2026-08-15 and refuted the same day, by the stage it was filed against. It had said a program cannot build a large repeated string at all, and that the port must reach for `awk` to write the 12000-line stdin fixture whose own comment calls it "the whole test" (`scripts/build_smoke.sh`, the PROC-STDIN-1 block). **A program CAN build that string**: [`buildsmoke.llmll`](../tools/build-smoke/buildsmoke.llmll) nests a two-line doubling helper fourteen times over a 9-byte literal and gets 147456 bytes, against the 108894 bytes that `printf 'LINE%s\n' $(seq 12000)` produces. The stage therefore spawns no `awk`, and the prediction that it would is **WITHDRAWN**. The assumption under the prediction was that the fixture needed the bytes `LINE1` through `LINE12000`; [`proc_stdin.llmll`](../scripts/build-smoke/proc_stdin.llmll) never inspects its own step input, so only the byte count was ever owed. **What a program still cannot do is produce N DISTINCT elements from the count N, and the witness is in the same file**: `v-feed-text` writes a LITERAL 60-element int list to make 60 stdin lines for the version-gate stage, because nothing generates the indices. That site is measured and in the tree; the `awk` one never existed. A 12000-element list literal is not a program | **Nothing is owed, and the sub-question this column left open was answered in the spec before the row was filed.** The column asked for `list-range : int -> int -> list[int]`; `range` is that function under another name. Its "open sub-question, not a blocker", whether the bound is inclusive and what a descending or empty range answers, is stated in [`LLMLL.md`](../LLMLL.md) §13.5 as half-open and empty when `from >= to`, and both halves were then confirmed by execution rather than read off the table. **The column as filed follows.** `list-range : int -> int -> list[int]`. **Pure**: no capability import, no effect label, no `Response` arm, no JSON-AST schema delta, and it grants no authority. **Tier S** under [`design/tier-route-1-proposal.md`](design/tier-route-1-proposal.md), so it batches rather than taking a design round of its own. With it the fixture is one expression: `(string-concat-many (list-map (list-range 1 12000) (fn [i: int] …)))`. **Open sub-question, not a blocker**: whether the bound is inclusive and what a descending or empty range answers; settle it with the builtin rather than before it |
| **RESP-FACT-1** (a `Command` result cannot carry a proved property to its caller) `[CT][SPEC]` | **SHIPPED v0.17.0** (`d5dd5a8`, 2026-09-04), design [`design/resp-fact-proposal.md`](design/resp-fact-proposal.md) Rev 6 after five professor rounds ([`design/resp-fact-review.md`](design/resp-fact-review.md)). The fact is keyed on the program's own control tag: an issuing rule binds a tag to the one builtin every returned `(σ, Command)` pair carries beside it, a delivery rule admits the fact only on values that reach the step from `:step` unchanged, and an export condition closes the import boundary (`(export)` is the idiom). One fact declared: `wasi.http.response`/`RCode` `{v : int | v >= 100}`, program-determined, its premise folded at `check` or proved as `call-pre:wasi.http.response` under the issuing def's `pre`. Trust report: per-function `assumed_facts` rows naming the premise case (`TRUST-AXIOM`'s first disclosed population). `checker_soundness_version` "2". hspec 1774 to 1831. **Residues, recorded rather than fixed:** `Sites` is not memoized; delivery row (t4) admits a `let` alias of (t2)/(t3) only; cell E's importer is a warning at `check` and an error at `build` (proposal §16 item 5); `W-RESP-FACT-NONE` is new; proposal §16 items 2, 3, 5, 8, 9 and 10 name follow-on rows not yet filed (`PAIR-PROJ-LET-1`, `CALL-PRE-ARGCALL-1`, `XMOD-QUAL-CTOR-1`). `CMD-A` retires the delivery rule when it lands (§5.5). Unblocks `FS-STAT-1` | The established shape puts the fact in the primitive's **declared type**: Liquid Haskell's `assume` for primitives (Vazou et al., ICFP 2014), F\*'s `assume val`, Dafny's `{:axiom}`. In each the caller discharges against a citable assumption and the trusted set stays enumerable, which is the LCF discipline [`LLMLL.md`](../LLMLL.md) §5.4 already applies. **Do not fix this inside a capability row.** It bounds any future claim that an effectful program is verified end to end. Design: [`design/resp-fact-proposal.md`](design/resp-fact-proposal.md) (Rev 2), which supersedes [`design/fs-capability-trio-proposal.md`](design/fs-capability-trio-proposal.md) §5 |

</details>
